Activation is one of the partial nodes: blended time to first Action is identically 0 in every month at this data's monthly grain, so the series exists but carries no variance signal and the scorecard reports it Not computable. **20 of the tree's 28 Layer-2 nodes have a computable actual** (14 computable, 6 partial, 8 blocked), and the two overlay nodes among the 28 are excluded from ranking by the tree's own text, so 20 of the 26 that can compete in a ranking are computable (6 of those 26 blocked), and **17 of the tree's 32 Layer-3 leaves do** (6 computable, 11 partial, 15 blocked). **13 of those 17 leaves are reachable in a drill-down**, because a leaf surfaces only under a Layer-2 outlier that is itself computable: organic/content, paid and community/events under Pipeline generated; rep fully-loaded cost and marketing spend allocation under S&M cost; POC pass rate, loss-reason mix and rep ramp mix under Win rate; discount rate versus list and deal-size trend within band under Avg initial commitment; and the three workflow-chain legs under Workflow chain under-utilization. The other four, the Account health score's inputs, are computed but sit under a blocked parent (see below). Gaps are reported per node with their reason rather than filled with a proxy; every gap note lives on the node itself in `_TREE` and travels into the engine's output.

| Layer-1 node | Layer-2 coverage | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| New logo consumption revenue | 3 of 3 | `pipeline_generated`, `win_rate`, `avg_initial_commitment` computable. Pipeline generated is read from `analytics/marketing_attribution.py` (see the subsection below) and has a real Layer 3: organic/content, paid and community/events. Win rate has three computable Layer-3 leaves (POC pass rate, loss-reason mix, rep ramp mix) and Avg initial commitment two (discount rate versus list, deal-size trend within band), all read from `mart_deal_funnel`; stage-to-stage conversion is blocked as degenerate. The marketing-sales handoff overlay and Brand & awareness are excluded from the ranking by the tree's own text; the handoff overlay's three leaves are not computable (no MQL/SAL lifecycle exists in any source) |
| Activation | 1 of 3 | `onboarding_completion_rate` computable but constant at 1.0. Time-to-first-integration and quickstart/docs engagement have no source at all — the build spec's `content_engagement` table was never generated |
| Expansion consumption revenue | 1 of 2 | `overage_realization` is computed as overage share of MRR (partial; see the Wave 10 wiring subsection). Wallet share progression has no denominator anywhere (nothing estimates an account's non-Acme workflow footprint), so its three Layer-3 leaves stay blocked too. **The branch yields a single-candidate read, not a sibling comparison**, and Overage realization is a leaf with no Layer 3, so a drill-down ends at `branch_depth_2` |
| Contraction + churned revenue | 3 of 4 | `cyclical_vs_structural_usage_dip` (usage-dip breadth, `partial`, see below), `workflow_chain_underutilization` (`partial`, with three computable Layer-3 legs) and `renewal_win_rate` (computable; both of its Layer-3 children stay blocked) rank against each other. The Account health score composite is not computable (no monthly history; see below) |
| Magic number | 1 of 1 | `sm_cost` computable. Its Layer 3: `rep_fully_loaded_cost` computable, `marketing_spend_allocation_by_channel` `partial` (channel spend summed over channels and attributed to segment — the per-channel split is in no mart). `cost_per_channel_activity` is not computable: cost/MQL and cost/SDR meeting need channel spend joined to per-channel lead and meeting counts, a Phase 2 mart gap |
| Consumption payback | 2 of 2 | Both legs `partial`: `cac_by_channel` is available only in blended form (the per-channel unblending the tree names is in no mart), and `utilized_vs_committed_action_volume` is ranked as a utilisation-haircut margin, so movement can come from utilisation or the MRR base. The raw committed and utilized Action sums are in `mart_consumption_utilization`, but the leg keeps the margin series that Consumption payback itself is built on |
| Onboarding/CS efficiency | 2 of 2 | Both fully computable |
| AM efficiency | 1 of 2 | `am_cost_by_segment` computable. `expansion_revenue_drivers` is defined by reference to Growth's expansion drivers (wallet share progression, overage realisation); overage realisation is computed but wallet share is not, so the reference cannot be recombined and stays blocked. Two layers deep, so no Layer 3 |
| NRR | 3 of 3 | The tree gives NRR a single child, the reference "See Growth — expansion, contraction, churn drivers". The engine expands that reference into its three named drivers as shares of starting revenue (the identity NRR = 1 + expansion − contraction − churn), labelled "See Growth: Expansion (share of starting revenue)" and so on, each carrying `cross_reference_to` and `cross_reference_label` for the Growth node it mirrors so a readout can suppress a duplicate drill-down (the design brief: "GRR reuses this same node, so it doesn't get a separate drill-down"). The decomposition differs from the tree only by this expansion of the reference; the tree is unchanged, and each drill-down carries a note stating the tree definition |
| GRR | 2 of 2 | Contraction / churn, same treatment (the tree's reference is "See Growth — contraction, churn drivers") |
| Logo retention | 1 of 2 | `tenure_at_churn` computable from `mart_account_health`; churn reason category (loud vs. silent) needs a churn-reason or churn-mode field that no table carries |

Two coverage decisions worth stating plainly:

- **`usage_dip_breadth` (Contraction+Churn's cyclical-vs-structural child)** — the share of the month's account population whose trailing-3-month mean `actions_consumed` sits below 70% of that account's own cumulative-to-date mean. This operationalises the node's own stated normalisation ("against the account's own historical baseline") from `mart_account_health` alone. It is descriptive — no model is fitted, no probability produced — so it is not a second risk model. Flagged `partial` because it measures the *breadth* of account-relative dips without performing the cyclical-vs-structural classification the node's headline asks for; that needs the node's second leaf (cohort comparison, same account type, same period last cycle), which no mart supports. The 0.70 cut is this artifact's own resolved decision: deep enough to exclude ordinary month-to-month usage noise, shallow enough to catch a fade well before it reaches zero.
- **Account health score is NOT usable as a Layer-2 trailing series.** `analytics/health_score.py` scores the currently active accounts at one `as_of_date`, and `score_accounts()` defines its scored population as accounts whose `customer_status` is `'Active'` — a final-status field. No monthly history of the composite exists in any table, and scored at a historical month the population silently drops every account that has churned since, biasing older months upward and manufacturing a spurious deterioration trend. Rather than publish a biased series, the composite is reported as not computable with that reason attached. The health model's **current-state** output is still used, at `as_of_date` only, for the watchlist. Producing an honest historical series needs `score_accounts()` to gate its population on `churn_month > month`, a change to `analytics/health_score.py` outside this artifact's scope. The composite's four Layer-3 inputs are plain `mart_account_health` columns and are computed (below), but they can only be reached through this node.

Layer-3 leaves are therefore reachable under five Layer-2 nodes: Pipeline generated, S&M cost, Win rate, Avg initial commitment and Workflow chain under-utilization. A real-data drill-down returns `surfaced` when one of those is the flagged outlier, `branch_depth_2` where the branch has no Layer 3 (the honest answer, including for Overage realization, a leaf), and `no_computable_layer3` where the tree has a Layer 3 that nothing computes (renewal win rate, cyclical usage dip). At `as_of_date` 2025-11-30 New logo's outlier is avg initial commitment (+101.5% against its baseline) and its drill-down is `surfaced`: deal-size trend within band (the share of wins at the ACV band floor, 35.0% against a baseline of 49.5%, −29.3%) and discount rate versus list (20.8% against 18.4%, +13.2%), with Pipeline generated ranked third (53 converted leads against a trailing-8-month mean of 62.25, −14.9%). At 2025-06-30 New logo is −9.0% against plan, Win rate is the outlier (42.5% against 25.6%, +65.8%), and the Layer-3 walk surfaces rep ramp mix (5.0% of closed deals owned by ramping reps against 10.0%) and POC pass rate (22.2% against 35.6%). At 2024-08-31 New logo is +15.6% against plan and Pipeline generated is the outlier (+37.6%), and the walk continues to paid (29 against a baseline of 17.5) and community/events (8 against 4.6). The Layer-3 code path is also exercised by the `new_logo_miss_win_rate_outlier_with_layer3` and `contraction_workflow_chain_branch_with_layer3` synthetic scenarios.

### Wave 10 wiring — deal funnel, workflow chain, overage and account-health inputs

Fifteen nodes are computed from three marts that expose data already present in fact tables (`mart_deal_funnel`, `mart_workflow_chain_health`, `mart_consumption_utilization`) and from `mart_account_health`, which already carried the four Account health score inputs. The engine reads only `mart_*` tables for these, at monthly grain. Deal-level nodes sum Commercial and Enterprise through `_REP_SOLD_SEGMENTS` (SMB opportunities are created already won and have no funnel); the workflow-chain nodes span all three segments (SMB is most of the chain accounts); overage is Commercial and Enterprise (SMB carries no commitment); every ratio is a ratio of sums, never a mean of segment ratios. Each Layer-2 series is a different statistic from its Layer-3 children, so a parent and its child can be ranked as separate evidence.

| Node | Layer, parent | Status | Series (monthly) | Caveat |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `poc_pass_rate` | 3, Win rate | partial | Enterprise passed POCs over closed Enterprise new-business deals with a POC outcome, by close month; months with fewer than 5 outcomes are blank | Monthly n is 10-15 from 2023 and 0-4 before, so the series is noisy (2025-02 was 0 of 5, 2025-03 9 of 14). A POC can fail on a won deal |
| `loss_reason_mix` | 3, Win rate | partial | Competitive share of lost new-business opportunities | A scalar view of a three-way mix; the mart carries all four reason counts |
| `rep_capacity_ramp_mix` | 3, Win rate | partial | Share of closed new-business deals created within 180 days of the owning rep's hire date | Owner is the current rep after any reassignment on a departure, so ramp attribution is approximate |
| `discount_rate_vs_list` | 3, Avg initial commitment | partial | 1 − Σ won amount ÷ Σ won list price | An identity by construction: list price is generated as amount ÷ (1 − discount rate), so it is not an independent check on pricing |
| `deal_size_trend_within_band` | 3, Avg initial commitment | partial | Won-count-weighted share of won deals at or below the segment's ACV band floor | Amounts are clipped to the band at generation; 195 of 365 Commercial wins (53%) and none of the Enterprise wins sit at the floor, and the combined share also moves with the Commercial/Enterprise win mix |
| `overage_realization` | 2, Expansion | partial | Σ overage MRR ÷ Σ total MRR (overage share of MRR, in dollars) | The realization *rate* is identically 100% (see findings), so the series is dollars. Runs 16-42% of MRR from 2021 (mean 32%) |
| `renewal_win_rate` | 2, Contraction + churned | computable | Closed-won renewals ÷ closed renewals (`opportunity_type = renewal`) | Renewal opportunities begin 2021-02; Enterprise has only 38 in total |
| `workflow_chain_underutilization` | 2, Contraction + churned | partial | Share of chain accounts whose full-chain completion rate is below 0.70 | The 0.70 cut is the playbook rule's value, proposed and not confirmed. Last 5 months of the window blank (censoring, below) |
| `ingestion_without_completion_rate` | 3, Workflow chain | computable | 1 − Σ downstream ÷ Σ upstream Actions (Actions-weighted) | Same censoring rule |
| `mid_chain_abandonment` | 3, Workflow chain | partial | Share of chain accounts partial this month and in the calendar-prior month | A subset of the Layer-2 breadth (79.2% of partial account-months are sustained over the whole window, 78.1% through 2025-08, the last month before the censored tail) |
| `full_vs_partial_chain_share` | 3, Workflow chain | partial | Share of chain accounts at or above 0.70 | The exact complement of the Layer-2 breadth: no information beyond it, kept because the tree names it |
| `usage_trend_account_relative` | 3, Account health score | partial | The usage-dip breadth series (same signal as `cyclical_vs_structural_usage_dip`) | A breadth measure, not a per-account trend slope |
| `support_ticket_volume_severity` | 3, Account health score | partial | Σ (ticket count × average severity score) per active account-month | One scalar for two attributes |
| `engagement_login_frequency` | 3, Account health score | computable | Mean logins per active account-month | — |
| `am_sentiment_notes` | 3, Account health score | partial | Mean AM sentiment over account-months that have one | Present for 4% of SMB, 47% of Commercial and 72% of Enterprise account-months |

**Reachability.** The four Account health score inputs are computed and rank cleanly under their parent in `rank_siblings()`, but the composite is not computable, so no Layer-2 outlier can be it and no drill-down can reach them. Their gap notes say so, and `layer3_computable_reachable` in the coverage report counts a leaf only under a computable Layer-2 parent. Registry v5 serves three of them through `query_metric` (mart columns); the usage trend needs the engine's account-relative window and stays engine-only.

**End-of-window censoring of the workflow-chain series.** A partial workflow chain exists in the data only for accounts within `DECLINE_MONTHS_BEFORE_CHURN` = 4 months of the churn month (a 5-month span including it): all 7,037 partial account-months belong to eventually-churned accounts, and none is further than 4 months from churn. A month's partial share therefore needs the churn events of the next 4 months to be present. The data window ends in 2025-12 and that month is itself truncated (4 churn events against 66-82 a month from 2025-06 to 2025-11), so a month is clean only if it is at least 5 months before the last: the partial share is 6.0% in 2025-07 and already 4.7% in 2025-08, then 3.6%, 2.3%, 1.3% and 0.1% purely from truncation. Reading that as an improvement would be wrong, so the four workflow-chain series are blank for the last 5 months of the window (the engine mirrors the config's decline length and adds one for the truncated month; a test ties both to `generators/config.py`); at the final evaluation month (2025-11) the node is listed as a missing sibling instead of being ranked. An `as_of_date` before the tail is unaffected. This also means the partial-chain share is a leading churn indicator by construction, not independent evidence.

**Win rate and the closed-deal share are blank before the first logged loss.** The generator logs no lost Commercial or Enterprise new-business opportunity before 2023-01 (`mart_growth_bridge` and `mart_deal_funnel` carry 0 lost deals in every month before it), so a win rate computed earlier counts wins only and reads 100% from 2020 to 2022-11 (2022-12 has no rep-sold deals). The same fact already left Loss-reason mix blank. Win rate and Rep capacity / ramp mix (a share of closed deals, which are wins only then) are blanked before the first month with a logged loss in the data available at the as-of date, not before a literal date; the first such month is 2023-01. Left in, the fake constant would sit in the trailing baseline of every 2023 month and in persistence streaks. Win rate was the top-ranked New logo driver in 4 of those 2023 months (10 months in all before the change, 6 after).

**A sibling needs the full trailing window to be ranked.** `rank_siblings()` returns a blank deviation, and `_with_signal()` drops the sibling, when it has fewer than `baseline_months` (8) observations before the month; the value and the observation count stay visible and the node is reported as a missing sibling, the path a series with no baseline always took. Before this rule a series with 1 to 7 observations was ranked on the mean of those few (Win rate in 2023-02 on one month; CAC by channel in each of 2023-02 to 2023-08, which led Consumption payback every one of those months). Win rate now enters a ranking at 2023-09 and CAC by channel at 2023-09. No committed readout month is affected (every series has a full baseline by 2024).

**Still not computable, with the specific reason.**

| Node | Reason |
|---|---|
| `stage_to_stage_conversion` | Degenerate: every one of the 1,669 closed Commercial and Enterprise new-business deals logs every stage of its segment's path, won or lost, so conversion is 100% at every hop |
| `account_health_score` (composite) | No monthly history: scored at a single as-of date over the active accounts |
| `wallet_share_progression` and its three children | No footprint denominator; the children need business-process events, AM-to-recommitment outcomes and community membership that do not exist |
| `expansion_revenue_drivers` | Defined by reference; one of its two drivers (wallet share) is blocked |
| `marketing_sales_handoff_quality` and its three leaves | No MQL/SAL lifecycle or lead status history |
| `brand_awareness` and its three leaves | No web-traffic, search or competitive-intelligence source |
| `time_to_first_integration`, `quickstart_docs_engagement_rate` | No integration event; the content-engagement source was never generated |
| `account_specific_baseline_deviation`, `cohort_comparison` | The first restates its parent; the second needs an account-type by usage-cycle cohort baseline |
| `time_to_respond_churn_risk_flag`, `loud_vs_silent_churn_mix`, `churn_reason_category` | No churn-risk flag event, and no churn mode or reason field |
| `cost_per_channel_activity` | Channel spend is not joined to per-channel lead and meeting counts |

**Cross-artifact guard.** `WAVE10_MART_NODE_MAP` declares which engine node each column of the three marts feeds, and `WAVE10_MART_UNUSED_COLUMNS` gives the reason for each column the engine does not read. `stale_markings_against_wave10_marts()` returns any mapped node that the tree marks not computable, and `tests/test_phase4_wave10_engine_wiring.py` fails on a non-empty result and on any mart column that is neither mapped nor explained, the same pattern as the marketing-attribution guard above. Persisted as `nodes_marked_not_computable_but_fed_by_wave10_marts` (0).

### Wave 10 findings

- **Stage-to-stage conversion is degenerate.** All 1,669 closed Commercial and Enterprise new-business deals have a stage-history row for every stage of their path, so the share advancing at each hop is 100% in every month. Deals differ in how long they spend in a stage (`mart_deal_funnel.avg_days_in_*`), not in whether they reach it. A real series needs the generator to let lost deals stop where they died, a Phase 1 change.
- **Overage realization as a rate is constant.** Billed MRR equals a constant per-account price times the larger of committed and utilized Actions on every committed account-month, so every overage Action is billed and the realization rate is identically 100%. The series that carries variance is overage share of MRR in dollars.
- **The utilized-only haircut in Consumption payback is nearly a no-op.** Utilization exceeds commitment on 16,536 of 17,874 committed Commercial and Enterprise account-months (92.5%; Commercial 93.2%, Enterprise 89.4%), and `mart_efficiency` caps the haircut at `least(1, utilized ÷ committed)`. For 92.5% of account-months the haircut is therefore 1, which undercuts the tree's "utilized Actions only, not billed" premise. Recorded as a finding; it is not changed here.
- **A POC can fail on a won deal.** Of 113 Enterprise wins, 22 carry a failed POC (91 passed); of 339 Enterprise losses, 101 carry a passed POC. POC outcome is informative but not deterministic.
- **The POC regression incident is visible in the series but not reliably attributed.** The POC pass rate over the incident window (2025-04 to 2025-06) is 8 of 34 (23.5%) against 28 of 65 (43.1%) over the prior six months; monthly deviations from the trailing baseline are −30%, −43% and −38%. Ordinary monthly noise is as large (2025-02 −100%, 2025-03 +94%), and the drill-down reaches POC pass rate only when Win rate is the Layer-2 outlier, which happens in one of the three months (2025-06, where POC pass rate ranks second behind rep ramp mix).
- **Expansion and Contraction plan-basis is an open question.** Both Layer-1 nodes are tagged `comparable`, but they are built from the same month-on-month expansion and contraction buckets whose scope caveats NRR and GRR (any usage decline counts as contraction, so both gross legs are large). At 2025-11 they read +338% and +919% against plan. If the NRR and GRR explanation holds, these two gaps are also definitional. Not changed in this work; the tag is the owner's call.
- **The workflow-chain signal is a pre-churn state** (see the censoring paragraph above): it is a leading indicator by construction, and it cannot be read in the last five months of the window.

### Pipeline generated — how the engine reads it

Pipeline generated and its organic / paid / community legs are computed by `analytics/marketing_attribution.py` (its own Pipeline-generated entry is above), which the engine calls rather than re-deriving; the mapping from that module's channels to tree keys is its `PIPELINE_CHANNEL_TO_TREE_KEY`, so which nodes it covers is declared in one place.

- **Basis: a monthly flow indexed by conversion month.** The engine reads `compute_pipeline_generated_flow()`, the converted leads (this funnel's PQL, i.e. signup) landing in each calendar month by the lead's sourcing sub-channel. It is the same population `compute_pipeline_generated()` counts in `leads_converted`, re-indexed from creation month to conversion month, and the two tie out exactly. The creation-month cohort basis is right for the tree's three-factor formula and wrong for a month-over-month read: at 2025-11-30 the latest six creation-month cohorts are 60–100% unresolved, so their point estimates are right-censored (the November cohort's PQL rate runs from 1.3% to 100% across its bounds). A conversion in month M is fully observed at the end of M.
- **Scope: company-wide, inbound-marketing-sourced.** Three segments including SMB (1,586 of the 1,986 converted leads at 2025-11-30 are SMB accounts), no segment cut: the lead panel's segment attribute comes from won-deal bookings and is missing for 189 converted leads with no won new-business opportunity, and `dim_accounts.segment` is the current segment, which would import later migrations into a historical month. Self-serve and outbound-SDR accounts carry no leads (the tree tracks outbound under win rate). Win rate and avg initial commitment are measured on rep-sold Commercial/Enterprise opportunities, a different population and unit.
- **Ranked, not multiplied.** The engine ranks Pipeline generated against its own trailing baseline like any sibling and attaches this scope note to every drill-down that ranks it (`PIPELINE_GENERATED_SCOPE_NOTE`), but it does not decompose New logo revenue as Pipeline × Win rate × Avg commitment: that identity is not computable at a shared population (the governance module's `new_logo_equals_pipeline_x_winrate_x_commitment` edge stays `NOT_COMPUTABLE` for that reason), and the engine has never computed an arithmetic contribution split at this level. What is reconciled arithmetically is the branch under Pipeline generated.
- **Reconciliation.** `reconcile_pipeline_generated_branch()` checks, on the exact series the engine ranks, that the three channel legs sum to the parent with zero difference in every month, and `marketing_attribution.reconcile_pipeline_generated_flow()` ties the flow to (1) an independent SQL recount by conversion month and channel from `fact_leads`, (2) a channel-agnostic recount, so a converted lead outside the three channels breaks the check instead of dropping out, and (3) the creation-cohort `leads_converted` totals per channel. All three are exact (max abs difference 0.0 over 71 months at 2025-11-30 and over 66 months at 2025-06-30).
- **Cross-artifact guard.** `stale_markings_against_attribution()` lists any tree node that `marketing_attribution.py` covers but the tree marks `NOT_COMPUTABLE` or omits. `tests/test_phase4_pipeline_generated_wiring.py` fails on a non-empty result, and `analytics/proxy_metric_health.py` re-runs it on every governance pass. Persisted as `nodes_marked_not_computable_but_covered_by_attribution` (0) and `pipeline_generated_children_max_abs_diff` (0.0).
- **Still blocked, with specific reasons.** The three leaves under Marketing–sales handoff quality need lifecycle data no source carries: `fact_leads` has no MQL/SAL stage or status (the raw leads source has none either), so an MQL→SAL acceptance rate has no field; MQL response SLA needs a per-lead first-sales-touch time but `fact_sales_activities` is keyed to `opportunity_id`/`contact_ref` with no `lead_id` (and lost new-business opportunities carry no `account_id`), while `days_to_conversion` is lead-to-signup time; lead recycling needs status history and `fact_lead_scoring_history`'s re-scores are not re-qualification events. Closing them is new Phase 1 data. Brand & awareness stays blocked on ungenerated web-traffic and competitive sources.
- **A property of the trailing-baseline read, not of this node.** A series that grows fast is compared with a lower trailing mean, so Pipeline generated's deviation is biased upward while lead volume is rising (it was the top-ranked New-logo sibling in 6 of the 35 evaluation months from 2023-01 to 2025-11, all six in 2023–2024: 2023-02, 2023-05, 2023-09, 2023-10, 2024-08 and 2024-11).

### Persistence — the same driver, adverse, month after month

Each drill-down carries a `persistence` record computed in the engine by `compute_persistence()`; the readout reads it and computes nothing. It speaks to "weather or decision" only as far as a trailing-baseline method can: has the same Layer-2 driver been the adverse outlier for more than one month?

**Definition.** For a drill-down whose Layer-2 outlier is D under Layer-1 node P, the streak is the number of consecutive months, counting back from the evaluation month, in which all four hold:

1. **Sibling comparison.** At least 2 of P's ranking-eligible children have a usable value and a full 8-observation trailing baseline that month (the rule the drill-down itself uses: a sibling with fewer observations is not ranked). A month with fewer ends the streak. A lone sibling would persist trivially, so a single-candidate branch (Expansion, Magic number, AM efficiency) is `not_applicable` with reason `single_candidate_read`, not a streak.
2. **Same driver.** D is the top-ranked sibling that month under P's own ranking basis (relative deviation, or absolute deviation on the additive branches NRR and GRR), each sibling measured against its own trailing 8-month baseline. If another sibling is on top, the streak ends and starts again from that driver; it never accumulates across drivers.
3. **No tie.** If the top two siblings are equal on the ranking key (relative tolerance 1e-9) there is no single outlier that month and the streak ends.
4. **Adverse direction.** D moved away from its baseline in the direction that hurts P. The direction comes from D's declared effect on P (`_EFFECT_ON_PARENT`, read off the tree's arithmetic) and P's `favorable_direction`: for New logo revenue (higher is better) all three children are adverse when they fall; for Consumption payback (lower is better) CAC is adverse when it rises and the utilised-margin leg when it falls; for Onboarding/CS efficiency (touches per Action, lower is better) touchpoints are adverse up and Action volume down; for NRR and GRR expansion is adverse down and contraction and churn up; for Contraction + churned revenue usage-dip breadth and workflow-chain under-utilization are adverse up and renewal win rate down. Tenure-at-churn has no defined direction (an earlier churn is not unambiguously better or worse), so Logo retention is `not_applicable` with reason `no_adverse_direction_defined`. An import-time check fails if a computable Layer-2 node has no entry. A favorable move, or no move, ends the streak.

Every prior month is read as of that month. The engine rebuilds the branch's Layer-2 series from the marts as of that month's end and cuts them at the month, so no later value reaches a baseline or a rank; prior months are requested newest first and only as far as the first month that breaks the streak. The evaluation month must be a complete month: when it is the truncated final month of the data window the status is `not_applicable` with reason `truncated_final_month`. A month in which a sibling has no value or no full baseline (the workflow-chain tail, a series that has not started, the first 8 months of a series) is a month with no comparison and ends the streak; when the month is within 8 months of the first observation the reason is `start_of_history`, otherwise `no_sibling_comparison`.

**Record.** `status` (`flagged`, `not_flagged`, `not_applicable`), `flagged`, `threshold_months`, `streak_months` (null when not applicable), `driver_key` and `driver_label`, `driver_direction` (`adverse`, `favorable` or `flat` for the evaluation month), `adverse_direction` (`up` or `down`), `first_month_of_streak`, `streak_detail` (month, deviation and baseline length for each streak month, newest first), `streak_break` (the first month that did not extend the streak and why: `driver_changed` with the new outlier, `not_adverse`, `tie_at_top`, `no_sibling_comparison`, `start_of_history`), `reason` (the `not_applicable` code), `basis`, `caveat` and a one-line `note`. The `caveat` is the fixed line "Repeat marker: the same driver as the adverse outlier in consecutive months; not evidence of a trend or cause.", shown beside every applicable record in the readout. `Drilldown` refuses a record that tracks a driver other than its own Layer-2 outlier or whose flag disagrees with its status.

**Threshold: 2 consecutive months — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed.** The signal (the same Layer-2 driver as the adverse outlier against its own trailing baseline for two consecutive months, genuine sibling comparisons only, the truncated final month excluded, streak length shown) is the owner's decision. The number is `K_PERSISTENCE = 2`: the smallest streak that separates a repeat from a single month. It is not derived from a loss function, and what it produces on real data is measured below rather than tuned to a target. Persistence was not keyed to plan breaches because about 86% of metric-months breach ±8% at this grain (the threshold table below), so a breach-keyed streak would saturate.

**Selectivity on real data** (`measure_persistence_selectivity()`; every Layer-1 branch, every evaluation month 2023-01 to 2025-11, the truncated 2025-12 excluded; 377 branch-months with a ranked outlier). Re-measured after the workflow-chain cut moved to 5 months, win rate and the closed-deal share were blanked before 2023-01, and a sibling needed a full 8-observation baseline; the earlier measurement (209 eligible, 37 flagged, 17.7%) is superseded:

| Measure | Result |
|---|---|
| Eligible branch-months (a genuine comparison with a defined direction) | 202. Activation, Expansion, Magic number and AM efficiency are single-candidate, Logo retention has no direction, and Consumption payback has 8 months (2023-02 to 2023-09) in which only the margin leg has a full baseline |
| Flagged | **38 of 202 (18.8%)**; among the 180 eligible branch-months where the Layer-1 node breached and a drill-down is shown, 37 (20.6%) |
| Streak length (eligible months) | 0: 110, 1: 54, 2: 20, 3: 10, 4: 5, 5: 2, 6: 1 |
| By branch | NRR 12 of 35 (34.3%), Consumption payback 6 of 27 (22.2%), Contraction + churned revenue 7 of 35 (20.0%), GRR 5 of 35 (14.3%), New logo revenue 4 of 35 (11.4%), Onboarding/CS efficiency 4 of 35 (11.4%) |
| Direction of the top outlier | adverse in 92 of 202 months (45.5%), favorable in 110 |
| Stability | The flag changes between consecutive months of a branch in 20.4% of transitions; a flagged month is followed by a flagged month 47.4% of the time; 20 flagged runs, mean length 1.9 months |
| Sensitivity | Baseline window 6 months: 18.1% flagged (204 eligible), 93.6% agreement with the 8-month setting, same driver in 88.6%. Window 12: 18.2% (198 eligible), 92.4%, 87.9%. Threshold 3: 8.9% flagged, 90.1% agreement |

What moved against the earlier measurement: 7 branch-months fewer are eligible (Consumption payback's 2023 months before CAC by channel had a baseline); in 4 of the 10 months in which Win rate had been the top New logo driver on the fake 100% history (2023-01, -03, -04, -06) another driver now leads, so the flagged New logo month in 2023-04 is gone and 2023-07 (Avg initial commitment, streak 2) is flagged instead; and Contraction + churned revenue's 2025-08 month is now flagged (the workflow-chain series, which led it on a deflated 2025-08 value, is blank, and the usage-dip breadth driver has a streak of 2). The flag fires on 18.8% of eligible branch-months: it is neither saturated nor silent (the review bounds set for this build were a flag on more than 60% or fewer than 2% of eligible branch-months). The threshold was not adjusted to reach that rate.

**Chance level — the flag is not evidence of a non-random sequence in this data.** `persistence_permutation_null()` shuffles the months of every sibling series independently (seeded, 200 permutations) and recomputes the flag. A shuffled series has the same values and spread but no month order. The shuffled flag rate is **17.9% on average (range 11.4% to 24.3%, 95th percentile 21.8%) against 18.8% observed; 40.5% of the permutations flag at least as often as the real data**. At a threshold of 3 the shuffled rate is 7.9% against 8.9% observed (37.5% of permutations at or above). Branch by branch the real rate is above the shuffled mean for Consumption payback (22.2% against 10.3%), Contraction + churned revenue (20.0% against 13.8%) and New logo revenue (11.4% against 10.4%), and below it for NRR (34.3% against 38.0%), GRR (14.3% against 19.8%) and Onboarding/CS efficiency (11.4% against 13.4%). In aggregate, then, the flag fires about as often as it would if months were unrelated. The reason is structural: with 2 or 3 siblings a more volatile sibling is the top outlier in most months, and its sign alternates, so "the same driver, adverse, twice" has a high base rate; NRR's expansion share is the flagged driver in all 12 of its flagged months. The flag is a faithful repeat detector, and a flagged streak should be read as "this driver has led the adverse deviations two months running", not as proof that the pattern is non-random or has a persistent cause. The readout says so beside every applicable record (`caveat`).

**Validation.** `run_persistence_scenarios()` runs 16 hand-built month-by-month histories through the same `compute_persistence()` and `Drilldown` path the engine uses, each with a declared answer; all 16 pass (logged as `persistence_scenarios_passed` / `persistence_scenarios_total`). They cover a streak of 1, exactly 2 (the boundary) and 3 with its first month named; a change of driver that resets the streak rather than bridging it (adverse again three months back, streak still 2); a missing month that breaks it; a favorable move of the same size in the same driver (streak 0); the direction following the parent's favorable direction (Onboarding/CS efficiency, denominator down); a tie at the top; the additive-share branch ranking by absolute deviation (NRR contraction persists at 2 while churn leads on percentage deviation); a single-candidate branch with four adverse-looking months (not applicable); the truncated final month; no outlier; an undirected driver; a streak that ends at the start of the data (4 months, after 8 months without a full baseline); a sibling with only 6 months of history that jumps 30% (not ranked, so a single-candidate read); and a history with three later months appended that must not change the record. Mutation tests in `tests/test_phase4_persistence.py` confirm the scenarios are not vacuous (flipping a child's declared sign, ignoring ties or changing the threshold each makes a scenario fail). The future-months case is an invariance check: a deviation is computed from the month and the months before it, so it holds by construction, and the cut is what keeps a provider that returns later data honest.

The profile builds each branch's series once and cuts them per month; the engine rebuilds them as of each prior month. `verify_persistence_asof_equivalence()` compares the two on every drill-down of full engine runs at 2023-06-30, 2024-03-31, 2024-09-30, 2025-06-30 and 2025-11-30: **42 of 42 records are identical** (driver, status, streak) after the change, so the profile describes what a readout shows.

At the committed checkpoints: 2025-06-30 flags Contraction + churned revenue's Renewal win rate (3 consecutive months since 2025-04), NRR's expansion share (5 since 2025-02) and GRR's contraction share (2 since 2025-05), and does not flag New logo revenue, Consumption payback or Onboarding/CS efficiency (each outlier is moving the favorable way); 2025-11-30 flags nothing (NRR and GRR have a streak of 1).

**Limitations.** Persistence inherits the trailing-baseline property that a series moving steadily in one direction stays above or below its own trailing mean for several months, so a trend reads as a streak (Pipeline generated's upward bias is the same effect). A prior month counts only with a full 8-observation baseline for the siblings compared, so the first 8 months of a series never enter a streak. The streak is adverse months only: a driver that is the top outlier but favorable has streak 0. Marketing-sales handoff quality and Brand & awareness are excluded from ranking by the tree and never enter a streak. The flag does not say why, and at this sample size it cannot be told apart from chance in aggregate (above).

### Watchlist

Accounts whose composite health score breaches the risk threshold, ranked by ARR at risk (build spec Section 5). Reuses `analytics/health_score.py`'s scored output — `risk_tier == 'High'` and `churn_probability` — directly; **no second risk model is built.** Grain: one row per flagged account.

**ARR caveat, stated rather than buried**: no `mart_*` table exposes ARR at account grain. `fact_revenue_monthly` does, but it is a `fact_*` table outside the mart-only read scope this Phase 4 code operates under. `est_arr_at_risk_usd` is therefore the account's **segment-average** ARR for the evaluation month (`mart_durability` `starting_mrr / starting_accounts × 12`), not its own contracted ARR — a genuinely computed segment figure, named as an estimate. Because this estimate is constant within a segment, sorting by it first degenerates to "order segments by average ARR, then churn probability within segment" — the ranking does what the build spec's "ranked by ARR at risk" literally asks for, but with a real consequence worth naming precisely: **at `as_of_date` 2025-11-30 the top 25 are 100% Commercial.** Enterprise has the highest segment-average ARR (~$470K) but zero accounts in the High risk tier that period (Enterprise churn probabilities sit below the population's 80th percentile), so it never appears; SMB has by far the largest absolute concentration of risk (1,085 High-tier accounts, vs. Commercial's 33) but SMB's much lower segment-average ARR (~$3.3K) means none of it surfaces until `top_n` exceeds Commercial's 33. A reader must not take "Commercial-only" as "SMB and Enterprise are fine" — it reflects this proxy's segment-constant shape and a ranking cutoff, not the real risk distribution. Exposing account-grain ARR in `mart_account_health` (or a new mart) is a Phase 2 change that would make this exact; a stratified or per-segment top-N is a Phase 4-side option that would surface SMB's concentration without waiting on that mart change, and is worth considering before this watchlist is read by anyone who won't have this paragraph in front of them.

### Threshold: ±8% — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed

Adopted from the hand-illustrated sample weekly readout in `acme-corp-claude-design-brief.md`, which used ±8% to decide which Layer-1 nodes earned a drill-down. Build spec Section 7 lists "exact variance threshold for triggering a drill-down (used ±8% in the sample readout — confirm or adjust)" as explicitly still open, so this is carried forward as the documented starting point, not settled — the same treatment the Segment migration entry's 10% firmographic-rescore floor carries. Breach is symmetric (either direction triggers a drill-down), matching what "±8%" says; direction only decides the Ahead/Behind label.

**Build-time evidence on selectivity** (`measure_threshold_selectivity()`, trailing 12 months ending 2025-11, 10 plan-comparable metrics per month):

| Threshold | Mean metrics flagged / month | Share of metric-months flagged |
|---|---|---|
| ±5% | 8.83 of 10 | 88.3% |
| **±8%** | **8.58 of 10** | **85.8%** |
| ±10% | 8.25 of 10 | 82.5% |
| ±15% | 7.83 of 10 | 78.3% |
| ±20% | 7.50 of 10 | 75.0% |
| ±30% | 7.00 of 10 | 70.0% |
| ±50% | 5.08 of 10 | 50.8% |

At this data's monthly grain, ±8% flags around six sevenths of all metric-months — far from the sample readout's 3-of-11 drill-down cadence, and the curve is flat enough that no threshold in this range reaches it: even ±50% still flags half the metric-months. Two structural reasons, neither of which argues for silently moving the number: (1) the sample readout is **weekly** and ±8% was calibrated on that illustrative shape, while every actuals mart here is monthly; (2) `generators/gtm_plan.py` is deliberately decoupled from every other generator's realised output — "a plan that read the actuals it's meant to be compared against would make variance from plan circular" — so plan-vs-actual gaps here reflect that deliberate independence as much as they reflect performance. The number is reported, not adjusted unilaterally; resolving build spec Section 7's open question is a decision for the readout build, when the reporting period's grain is fixed. Note also that the `baseline_variance_pct` column (every metric against its own trailing baseline) is available alongside the plan diff and is the more usable signal for the five `caveated` metrics, whose plan-level offsets are definitional.

### Validation — synthetic test cases, not statistics

`run_synthetic_scenarios()` in `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py` (clearly marked test-support section). Each scenario is a hand-constructed set of sibling series with one unambiguous true answer, declared alongside, driven through the same `rank_siblings()` / `layer3_evidence()` / `Drilldown` path the real engine uses. `analytics-model-validator` runs these and confirms every case resolves to its declared expectation. **All 7 pass at build time** (logged as `synthetic_scenarios_passed` / `synthetic_scenarios_total`).

| Scenario | What it pins down | Expected answer |
|---|---|---|
| `new_logo_miss_win_rate_outlier_with_layer3` | The design brief's own worked example. New logo misses plan −18%; win rate sits 22% below its own trailing baseline, avg initial commitment within 1.5%. Win rate has real Layer-3 children, so its three computable leaves are supplied (stage-to-stage conversion is degenerate and has no series) and the two largest must be surfaced | L2 `win_rate`; L3 `[poc_pass_rate, loss_reason_mix]`; status `surfaced`; branch depth 3 |
| `contraction_workflow_chain_branch_with_layer3` | Contraction + churn runs 25% over plan; workflow chain under-utilization sits 30% above its own baseline while renewal win rate (−4%) and usage-dip breadth (+2%) are flat. The branch has three computable Layer-3 legs | L2 `workflow_chain_underutilization`; L3 `[mid_chain_abandonment, ingestion_without_completion_rate]` (the parent's own complement is left out); status `surfaced`; branch depth 3 |
| `expansion_overage_single_candidate_depth_2_leaf` | Expansion beats plan by 30%; only overage realization has a series (wallet share has no denominator). It must be reported as a single-candidate read, and since overage realization is a leaf the status is `branch_depth_2` although the branch is three deep through wallet share | L2 `overage_realization`; L3 `[]`; status `branch_depth_2`; branch depth 3; not a genuine sibling comparison |
| `consumption_payback_cac_leg_two_layer_branch` | The named anti-fabrication test. Payback runs 20% long; CAC leg +25% vs. baseline, margin leg +1%. The design brief says in as many words that this branch stops at Layer 2 | L2 `cac_by_channel`; L3 `[]`; status `branch_depth_2`; branch depth 2 |
| `onboarding_cs_efficiency_denominator_outlier` | The intuitive culprit is the numerator (AM touchpoints, within 2%); the real outlier is the denominator (automated Action volume, −13%). Confirms ranking is by each sibling's own deviation, not by position in the ratio | L2 `automated_action_volume`; L3 `[]`; status `branch_depth_2` |
| `true_siblings_only_guard` | The named failure mode, tested head-on. Contraction+churn breaches while a Layer-2 node from a **different** parent (win rate, a child of New logo) deviates far harder. `rank_siblings()` must raise on the intruder rather than rank it, and pick the real answer on the branch's own candidates | Intruder rejected; L2 `cyclical_vs_structural_usage_dip`; status `no_computable_layer3` |
| `nrr_additive_share_basis` | Validates the `additive_share` ranking basis. Churn swings −40% relative but only −0.0006 absolute; contraction moves +13% relative but +0.0060 absolute — ten times more of NRR's actual movement. % deviation would name churn, which is wrong | L2 `nrr_contraction_rate`; L3 `[]`; status `branch_depth_2` |

Every scenario additionally asserts, on the emitted record, that the Layer-1 record is labelled layer 1, the Layer-2 record layer 2, and every leaf layer 3 — the label check runs on the output, not only on the tree.

**Real-data smoke test against the QA plan's injected incidents.** The QA plan requires "at least one injected incident period is detectable by a straightforward variance check." Two incidents exist in `generators/config.py`:

- **CAC-creep incident #2** (`inbound_marketing`, 2024-09 → 2024-11, spend × 1.8): **detected.** Running the engine at each month in the window, Consumption payback's trailing-baseline variance jumps to **+107% (2024-09)** and **+38% (2024-11)** against quiet shoulder months (+3.9% in 2024-08, −4.5% in 2024-12), and the Layer-2 outlier is correctly identified as `cac_by_channel` in both — +83% and +68% above its own trailing baseline, against the margin leg's +13%. The middle month (2024-10) is a near-tie (CAC +13.2% vs. margin +14.5%) and the engine names the margin leg there; 2 of 3 incident months are cleanly attributed. Note this detection runs off the **trailing-baseline** signal, not the plan diff, whose −99% payback offset is definitional (see the caveat above).
- **POC-regression incident #1** (2025-04 → 2025-06, Enterprise POC pass rate depressed): **visible in the Layer-3 series, only partly attributed in a drill-down.** POC pass rate is now a computed Layer-3 leaf under Win rate. The incident window runs 8 of 34 (23.5%) against 28 of 65 (43.1%) over the prior six months, and the monthly deviation from the trailing baseline is −30%, −43% and −38%. Two structural facts limit what a drill-down says. `generators/opportunities.py` sets the incident-window POC pass rate *conditional on the deal's already-decided won/lost outcome*, so the incident moves POC pass rate without moving win rate (the blended win rate in the window, 0.303, is slightly *above* the surrounding six months, 0.270), and the leaf is reached only when Win rate is the Layer-2 outlier of a breaching New logo node: that happens in one of the three months (2025-06, where POC pass rate ranks second behind rep ramp mix). The monthly series is also noisy at 10-15 outcomes (2025-02 −100%, 2025-03 +94%), so a single month is not a reliable detector; the pooled window is.

### Known limitations

- **End-of-window truncation.** The simulation's final month (2025-12) is not a representative evaluation period: every still-active account's last observed month lands in the contraction bucket (contraction+churn spikes to $1.17M against a $300–500K norm), Enterprise marketing spend is absent so blended CAC is incomplete, and Action volume is partial. `run_diagnostic()` returns a `data_window` block that flags this explicitly rather than excluding the month silently; the canonical build-time checkpoint uses `as_of_date = 2025-11-30`. A checkpoint at 2025-12-31 is also logged; 2025-11-30 shows 9 breaching nodes and 2025-12-31 shows 8.
- **Layer-3 evidence is reachable for five branches** — 13 of the tree's 32 Layer-3 leaves (organic/content, paid and community/events under Pipeline generated; rep fully-loaded cost and marketing spend allocation under S&M cost; POC pass rate, loss-reason mix and rep ramp mix under Win rate; discount rate and deal-size trend under Avg initial commitment; the three workflow-chain legs). Four more (the Account health score inputs) are computed but unreachable under a blocked parent. The other 15 depend on sources that do not exist (`content_engagement`, `community_membership`, an MQL/SAL lifecycle, web traffic, churn reasons, integration events) or are degenerate (stage-to-stage conversion). Closing any of these is a Phase 1 change, or for the health composite a change to `analytics/health_score.py`.
- **Several newly computed leaves are noisy or partial.** POC pass rate rests on 10-15 outcomes a month; discount rate versus list is an identity by construction; deal-size trend measures how often the ACV band clip binds; full-chain share is the complement of the Layer-2 workflow breadth. Each says so in its gap note, and none is read as independent evidence beyond what the note states.
- **Not subject to drift monitoring.** This artifact has no calibration to drift. `fact_model_performance_history` carries only its structural coverage and correctness scalars (`synthetic_scenarios_passed`/`_total`, `variance_threshold`, `layer1_nodes_*`, `layer2_siblings_*`, `drilldowns_with_genuine_sibling_comparison`, `pipeline_generated_children_max_abs_diff`, `nodes_marked_not_computable_but_covered_by_attribution`, `nodes_marked_not_computable_but_fed_by_wave10_marts`, `persistence_scenarios_total`/`_passed`, `persistence_eligible_branch_months`, `persistence_flag_rate`) so a future build can be checked against this one. Recurring `drift-monitor` runs apply to the health score, not here.

## Weekly executive readout

- **Built**: `analytics/weekly_readout.py`. Grain: one readout per evaluation period — the last *complete* month at or before `as_of_date`, company-wide/blended, the same evaluation month `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py` defines. Rendered deliverable and structured payload land in `analytics/outputs/weekly_readout_<as_of_date>.md` / `.json` (version-controlled, unlike `data/acme_gtm.duckdb`, so the real-data document is inspectable without rebuilding the pipeline). No dbt model was added or changed for this artifact.
- **What it is**: Wave 1's fifth and final artifact — the build spec's "forcing function that proves the first four work against real generated data." It assembles the already-built artifacts' outputs into the document structure build spec Section 5 specifies (header · executive summary · Layer-1 scorecard · drill-downs · playbook triggers · forecast · watchlist), in the shape of the hand-illustrated sample readout in `acme-corp-claude-design-brief.md`, backed by real data instead of illustration. It also carries two additions the spec does not list: a persistence record on each drill-down and a segment-mix section ("are we moving upmarket"), both below.
- **Inputs — four artifacts, deliberately**: (1) `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s `run_diagnostic(as_of_date)` output, in full: `layer1_scorecard` (all 11 Layer-1 nodes), `drilldowns` (one `Drilldown` per breaching node, each with its persistence record), `watchlist` (which itself consumes `analytics/health_score.py`'s `score_accounts()`), `coverage`, and `data_window`; (2) `analytics/playbook_triggers.py`'s `run_playbook_triggers(as_of_date)` for the trigger section; (3) `analytics/forecast.py`'s `latest_forecast_call_date()` and `run_forecast()` for the forecast section; (4) `analytics/segment_migration.py`'s `compute_segment_mix()` for the segment-mix section. **This module reads no mart, no `fact_*` table and no database directly** — it holds no DuckDB connection and issues no SQL. Every business figure enters through the artifact that owns it and that has already been validated against its own stated target, so a number in the readout and the same number in its source artifact cannot diverge.
- **It computes nothing, and that is the design**: there is no arithmetic on a metric value anywhere in the module. Formatting a float into `$45.2K`, grouping the eleven nodes into three pillar tables, and selecting which drill-downs exist (by reading the engine's own `breaches_threshold` flag) are presentation and routing; deriving a figure is not done. One consequence worth stating: Onboarding/CS efficiency renders as **touches per automated Action** — the metric tree's own orientation — rather than the Actions-per-touch inversion the illustrative sample readout showed, because inverting it here would be this artifact publishing a number no mart computed.
- **Model type selection and rationale (why there is no model)**: this artifact is pure assembly, so the question is not which model class but whether a model belongs here at all. Two candidates were considered and rejected. (1) A *scoring/prioritisation model* over the scorecard (ranking which of the eleven nodes most deserves leadership attention) would introduce a second, unvalidated opinion competing with the variance engine's own threshold rule — the engine already answers "which nodes earned a drill-down," and Section 5's drill-down section is specified as variable-length-by-breach, not top-N. (2) A *template-based prose generator* for the executive summary would produce text that reads as narrative while carrying none of the causal reasoning the section exists for; it is excluded, and the narrative is instead produced by a separate, validated step (the next entry), never by this module. What remains is a structural/logic artifact in the sense `analytics-engineering-conventions` names — the same category as the variance-diagnostic engine and the segment-migration analysis — so **no coefficient table, no AUC, no confusion matrix, no calibration note and no R²/RMSE applies here, and their absence is deliberate, not pending.**
- **Point-in-time design**: `as_of_date` is a required parameter and is passed straight through to `run_diagnostic()`, whose loaders all filter `month <= as_of_date`. The forecast section is anchored to the reporting period's end rather than to `as_of_date` (selection rule below), so it can never read past the period the scorecard describes. This module introduces no additional data access, so it introduces no additional leakage surface. The rendered document names both the `as_of_date` and the evaluation month it resolved to.
- **Determinism / seeding**: no stochastic step exists in this module — no sampling, no simulation, no train/test split — so no random seed applies, matching `analytics/segment_migration.py`'s and `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s precedent. The one indirect exception is inherited: the watchlist section renders `analytics/health_score.py`'s scored output, seeded there via that module's `_RANDOM_SEED = 42`.

### Resolved scope decision — the reporting period is monthly, not weekly

The design brief's sample readout is weekly ("August 24 – August 30, 2026"). Every actuals mart in this project is segment × month, and the variance-diagnostic engine evaluates the last complete month at or before `as_of_date` (diffing a partial period's actual against a whole period's plan is a unit mismatch in disguise). A weekly readout over monthly marts would have to either re-window the marts inside this artifact — the one thing a pure-assembly artifact must not do — or repeat a single monthly figure under four weekly labels. **Resolved: the reporting period is the evaluation month**, and the rendered header says so in place of borrowing the sample's weekly framing. The artifact keeps the name "weekly executive readout" (the build spec's and Section 8's own name for it); the cadence question is a grain question about the marts, not a naming one.

This settles the input build spec Section 7's open threshold question was waiting on. The Variance-diagnostic engine entry's selectivity table records that ±8% flags ~86% of metric-months at monthly grain against the sample readout's illustrative 3-of-11 weekly cadence, and notes that resolving Section 7 "is a decision for the readout build, when the reporting period's grain is fixed." The grain is now fixed at monthly. **The threshold itself is not changed here**: it belongs to the variance-diagnostic engine, this artifact consumes it read-only, and ±8% remains PROPOSED per that entry. What this entry adds is the settled premise — any revision to the threshold should be argued at monthly grain, against that selectivity table, and made in the engine.

### One section this module does not fill itself

The executive summary is carried in the assembled structure and the rendered document with an explicit status, rather than omitted (which would make the readout look complete when it is not) or filled with invented content. Automated playbook triggers and the forecast are not gaps: each reads its own built artifact directly (`run_playbook_triggers(as_of_date)`; `run_forecast()`, below) and performs no computation on what it reads.

| Section | Status in the readout | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Executive summary narrative | `not_generated` until `analytics/executive_summary.py` has run (reason `pending_generation`, then `no_api_key` without a key); `generated` or `validation_failed` after | Build spec Section 5 specifies a narrative naming a specific Layer-2/Layer-3 cause for any real miss, generated via the Claude API (Section 3's stack). It is its own step with its own entry (next section). **No LLM is called from `analytics/weekly_readout.py` and no template-based prose generator substitutes for one.** `assemble_readout()` returns the fully-assembled structured readout, which is exactly the input that step consumes. Re-assembling the readout never discards a generated summary whose input is unchanged (matching `input_hash`) and never writes unvalidated prose. |

### Forecast section — the forecast artifact's lenses at the period-end call

**Contract.** `readout["forecast"]` is always present with `status` `present` or `unavailable`; no section carries `not_yet_built` and the logged `sections_not_yet_built` scalar is 0.

| Field (status `present`) | Content |
|---|---|
| `forecast_as_of_date`, `period`, `call_to_quarter_end_days` | the forecast call selected (below), the quarter that call sits in (`2025-Q4`), and the calendar days from the call to that quarter's end (a date fact, not a metric) |
| `selection` | `{rule, reporting_period_end, forecast_call_date, call_cadence}` |
| `segments` | one row per segment (Commercial, Enterprise) with open deals in the quarter, read verbatim from `run_forecast()['reconciliation']`: `open_deals`, `open_pipeline_amount`, `deals_without_submission`, the four lenses `bottoms_up_rep` / `bottoms_up_manager` / `ml` / `cro_adjusted`, `cro_adjusted_ml_forecast`, `cro_adjustment_amount` / `cro_reason` / `has_logged_cro_adjustment`, `lenses_computable`, `lens_max` / `lens_min` / `lens_mean`, `lens_spread_pct`, `diverges_materially`, `widest_pair`, plus `*_display` strings |
| `segments_without_open_deals` | segments the forecast artifact returned no row for (no open deal scoped to the quarter after the call) |
| `lens_labels`, `lens_definitions` | the four lens names and `analytics/forecast.py`'s `LENS_DEFINITIONS` |
| `divergence_threshold`, `divergence_threshold_status` | 0.25, and the forecast artifact's own proposed-not-confirmed statement |
| `ml_lens` | the classifier's stated accuracy and calibration context from the fitted-model result `run_forecast()` returns (nothing re-fitted): out-of-time holdout AUC against the 0.70–0.85 target and its position relative to it (`auc_target_position`: `within`, `above` or `below`; the rendered text says "above the 0.70-0.85 target range" or "below" rather than only "outside"), manager-category lookup baseline AUC and the AUC ratio against it (the leak diagnostic), calibration gap against ±0.05, `ml_targets_passed`, train/holdout sizes and class counts and date ranges; or `{computable: false, reason}` |
| `plan_comparison` | `{status: not_available, reason}` — see below |
| `data_window_note`, `caveats`, `note` | the forecast artifact's own `data_window` note (period-aware: the end-of-window statement is printed as applying only to calls inside the quarter that contains the window end, 2025-Q4, and as not applying to a Q2 call) and its `FORECAST_CAVEATS`, verbatim; the caveats include the measured rebuild variation of the ML lens (below) |

`unavailable` carries `reason` (`no_forecast_call_on_or_before_period_end` or `no_open_deals_scoped_to_the_forecast_quarter`), a specific `detail`, `forecast_as_of_date` (null for the first), `selection`, `note` and `caveats`, and no figure of any kind.

**As-of selection rule.** The readout reports a month and the forecast is quarter-grain, so the section does not pretend to forecast the month. It carries the forecast call that was current at the reporting period's end: `analytics/forecast.py`'s `latest_forecast_call_date(reporting_period_end)` — the latest `fact_forecast_submissions.snapshot_date` (a Friday) on or before the evaluation month's last day — and then `run_forecast()` at that date, which forecasts the quarter containing it. Anchoring to the period end rather than to `as_of_date` keeps the section on the same period as the scorecard when `as_of_date` falls mid-month. The one field read from after the call is each open deal's close date, used only to decide which quarter the deal belongs to (the data has no separate expected-close field, so `close_date` stands in for it); no outcome after the call is read. Each committed readout shows:

| Readout | Reporting period end | Forecast call | Quarter | Days to quarter end | What it shows |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2025-11-30 | 2025-11-30 | 2025-11-28 | 2025-Q4 | 33 | Commercial (104 open deals) and Enterprise (25), four lenses each, ML AUC 0.799 within the 0.70–0.85 range; neither segment above the 25% threshold (spreads 18.0% and 10.8%); the forecast artifact's own `run_build_time_validation` at this call passes 6 of 6 |
| 2025-06-30 | 2025-06-30 | 2025-06-27 | 2025-Q2 | 3 | Commercial only (2 open deals, $38.5K open pipeline); Enterprise had no open deal scoped to the quarter after the call. The CRO's quarter-level override (+$117.6K) is not scaled to the nearly exhausted pipeline, so CRO-adjusted ($126.8K) exceeds open pipeline and the artifact flags the segment as diverging materially (spread 301.3%). The ML AUC (0.859) sits above the 0.85 ceiling of the 0.70–0.85 target range, at 1.03× the leak-proof baseline; the forecast artifact's own `run_build_time_validation` at this call passes 5 of 6, failing only that ceiling. The Forecast entry analyses the analogous 2025-08-15 breach (composition tested and rejected) and has no analysis for this one, so no cause is stated here. The section carries all of it plus the late-quarter caveat; it does not suppress or soften it |

**Unavailable paths.** (1) No forecast call exists on or before the period end (before 2023-01-06, the first snapshot): `unavailable` / `no_forecast_call_on_or_before_period_end`. (2) The selected call leaves no open Commercial/Enterprise deal scoped to its quarter (for example a call on the quarter's last day): `unavailable` / `no_open_deals_scoped_to_the_forecast_quarter`. Neither is exercised by the two committed dates; both are covered by tests.

**No plan or quota comparison.** The build spec asks the forecast section to follow "the same variance/threshold logic as above". The forecast has no plan or quota at its grain and unit — closed-won opportunity amount for Commercial and Enterprise, per quarter. `mart_gtm_plan` states monthly MRR-movement plans at company grain and quota history is per rep across all segments; either would need a conversion neither this readout nor the forecast artifact owns. The section states `plan_comparison: not_available` with that reason rather than manufacturing a comparison. The variance logic the forecast does have is its own divergence flag between lenses, which is carried.

**Trace.** `verify_source_trace()`'s `forecast_traces_exactly_to_a_fresh_run` re-selects the call, runs `run_forecast()` again from scratch, and compares the section to that second run at exact equality (every lens, count and flag per segment, the period, the ML-lens context, the divergence threshold, the caveats and the data-window note), or confirms the `unavailable` reason is the one the fresh run implies. `forecast_section_is_built_and_declares_its_status` rejects `not_yet_built`, an `unavailable` section without a specific reason or carrying figures, and a forecast call after the period end.

The Phase 5 web dashboard (`dashboard/`, Streamlit) is separate infrastructure and is not this artifact either -- its Digest page renders this artifact's own JSON output verbatim rather than recomputing anything. The rendered Markdown document *is* the deliverable.

### Persistence on each drill-down

Every drill-down entry carries the engine's `persistence` record verbatim (definition, threshold and measured selectivity in the Variance-diagnostic engine entry) plus one display string, `status_display` (`Flagged: N consecutive months`, `Not flagged: streak N of 2 months` or `Not applicable`), and renders as one line under the Layer-2 outlier: `- **Persistence:** <status_display>. <note>`, followed for an applicable record by a sub-line carrying `caveat` ("Repeat marker: the same driver as the adverse outlier in consecutive months; not evidence of a trend or cause."). The caveat is there because the flag fires about as often as on month-shuffled data (engine entry), so the markdown reader sees it beside the flag. `status_display` and `note` keys are unchanged; `caveat` is an added key. The readout computes no streak. At the committed checkpoints 2025-06-30 shows three flagged drill-downs (Contraction + churned revenue, NRR, GRR) and 2025-11-30 none. `verify_source_trace()` checks that each record equals the engine output the readout was assembled from (`drilldown_persistence_matches_engine_output`: the same record less the display string, tracking the drill-down's own Layer-2 outlier, flag agreeing with status, a not-applicable record carrying a reason and no streak) and equals a fresh engine run at the same as-of date (`drilldown_persistence_traces_exactly_to_a_fresh_engine_run`); `verify_rendered_document()` checks the rendered line against the payload (`drilldown_persistence_rendered_per_payload`).

### Segment-mix section — are we moving upmarket

**Contract.** `readout["segment_mix"]` is always present with `status` `present` or `unavailable`. It is read from `analytics/segment_migration.py`'s `compute_segment_mix(evaluation_month)`, which reuses that module's velocity and graduated-revenue functions and adds each segment's share of ending MRR from `mart_growth_bridge`; the readout adds display strings and a headline only.

| Field (status `present`) | Content |
|---|---|
| `evaluation_month`, `window` | the reporting month; its prior month and the month a year earlier (null when the data does not reach back that far), the 12-month trailing window (`trailing_window_first_month` to `trailing_window_last_month`), whether the year-earlier 12-month window exists, `last_month_in_marts`, and `partial_month_handling` |
| `total_ending_mrr`, `segments` | one row per segment (SMB, Commercial, Enterprise): `ending_mrr`, `mrr_share`, `mrr_share_prior_month`, `share_change_vs_prior_month`, `mrr_share_12m_ago`, `share_change_vs_12m_ago` (differences of shares, in fractions of company MRR) |
| `upmarket_share` | the Commercial plus Enterprise share, its prior-month and year-earlier values and the two changes, and `smb_to_commercial_graduated_share_of_total_mrr_trailing_12m` (the MRR that moved from SMB to Commercial in the trailing 12 months over company ending MRR) |
| `migration` | one row per pair (SMB to Commercial, Commercial to Enterprise): `events_in_month`, `velocity_in_month`, `events_trailing_12m`, `velocity_trailing_12m`, `avg_from_segment_accounts_trailing_12m`, the same 12-month window a year earlier (`events_trailing_12m_year_ago`, `velocity_trailing_12m_year_ago`, `velocity_change_vs_year_ago`), `graduated_mrr_in_month` and `graduated_mrr_trailing_12m`, and the graduated MRR as a share of the source segment's MRR (`graduated_share_of_source_mrr_in_month`, `_trailing_12m`), with the same flow over the source segment's ending MRR (`graduated_share_of_source_ending_mrr_trailing_12m`) and over its MRR a year earlier (`graduated_share_of_source_mrr_a_year_earlier_trailing_12m`) and `migrating_account_mrr_multiple_trailing_12m` (MRR per migrating account over MRR per average source-segment account) |
| `reconciliation` | graduated MRR against `mart_growth_bridge`'s migration columns (max absolute difference, tolerance, `reconciles`) |
| `basis`, `caveats`, `note`, `headline`, `*_display` | the definitions in one string, six caveats (four fixed, two carrying the month's own figures), a one-line note, one headline (`Commercial and Enterprise hold 87.9% of ending MRR (+5.2 pp against 12 months earlier).`) and display strings for every figure |

`unavailable` carries `reason`, `detail`, `evaluation_month`, `basis`, `caveats`, `note` and `headline: null`, and no figures. Reasons: `evaluation_month_is_truncated_final_month` (the final month of the data window, whose contraction is inflated by end-of-window truncation, is not a representative month, so the section is not reported for it; the prior month is the last representative month) and `no_segment_data_for_evaluation_month` (a month the marts have no MRR for in all three segments, which is every month before 2020-07).

**Definitions.** Velocity is events divided by the source segment's average starting accounts over the window (the Segment migration entry's definition, called not re-derived); the monthly rate is one month of events over that month's starting accounts and the 12-month rate twelve months of events over the 12-month average, so the two are not comparable with each other. Graduated MRR is `mrr_reclassified` summed over the window, and its share is that sum over the source segment's average starting MRR across the window (the month's own starting MRR for the in-month figure). A 12-month flow against an average base can exceed 100% when the base is replenished by new logos and expansion; at 2025-11 it is 66.5% for SMB to Commercial and 52.5% for Commercial to Enterprise.

**What it shows.** At 2025-11-30 Commercial and Enterprise hold 87.9% of ending MRR, +5.2 pp against 12 months earlier (Enterprise 68.7%, +4.2 pp; Commercial 19.1%, +1.0 pp; SMB 12.1%, -5.2 pp). SMB-to-Commercial migration ran at 10.7% of SMB accounts over the trailing 12 months against 6.2% a year earlier (415 events against 157), and Commercial-to-Enterprise at 9.0% against 4.8% (55 against 14). At 2025-06-30 the share is 83.5%, +2.0 pp. Migration is upward-only by construction, so raw event counts and graduated MRR are trivially up with the base; the section leads with the rates and the mix, says so in its first caveat, and notes that a segment's share also moves with expansion, contraction and churn inside each segment, not only with migration.

**What the share and the mix do and do not say.** Two of the six caveats carry the month's own figures. (1) The graduated share of source-segment MRR is a trailing-12-month flow set against an average base, so it can exceed 100%; the accounts that migrate carry about 6 times the MRR of the average SMB account (SMB to Commercial, 2025-11: $1,739 against $279), so the $722K that left SMB is 66.5% of average SMB MRR over the window, 60% of ending SMB MRR and 79% of SMB MRR 12 months earlier. Any of the three is a defensible denominator; none should be read as the share of SMB accounts that graduated (10.7% by count). (2) The upmarket MRR share rises partly because existing accounts are reclassified upward rather than because new revenue is sold upmarket: the $722K that moved from SMB to Commercial over 12 months is 7.3% of company ending MRR, more than the +5.2 pp rise in the upmarket share over the same period, so the share answers "where is MRR booked" more than "who are we selling to". Commercial-to-Enterprise moves do not change the upmarket share.

**Trace.** `segment_mix_traces_exactly_to_a_fresh_run` re-runs `compute_segment_mix()` and compares the section, less its display strings, at exact equality. `segment_mix_shares_tie_to_ending_mrr` checks that segment ending MRR sums to the total, shares sum to 1, each share is its ending MRR over the total and the upmarket share is Commercial plus Enterprise. `segment_mix_section_is_built_and_declares_its_status` rejects an unlabelled status, a section for another month, an unavailable section without a reason or carrying figures, and a present section that does not reconcile. `segment_mix_section_rendered_per_status` checks the rendered heading, headline, every table row and every caveat against the payload, or the status line and no table for an unavailable section.

**The segment mix is not part of the executive summary's evidence.** It is not in the summary's prompt view, has no cite id and cannot be quoted by a statement. It is part of the readout, so it is in the readout's `input_hash`.

### Target — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed

No accuracy or error target applies (pure assembly, per the shape decision above). Three structural/trace criteria stand in its place, in the same spirit as the health score's stated AUC band but newly derived here, since nothing upstream states a number for an assembly artifact. All three are stated as **exact equality rather than a tolerance band**, and that difference from the health score's 0.65–0.80 range is the grounding, not an oversight: an AUC band exists because a fitted model's measured performance carries genuine sampling variance, whereas every check below is deterministic and re-derivable from the same inputs with no variance at all. A tolerance on any of them would imply an error source that does not exist, and would quietly license exactly the failure the criterion is meant to catch.

1. **Structural completeness: exactly 11 Layer-1 rows in the scorecard, every period, unconditionally — 11/11, no tolerance.** Build spec Section 5 says "all 11 nodes, every week." Any number other than 11 means a node was dropped or an intermediate node was admitted; both are defects, neither is drift. Includes Activation's degenerate status (the one node with no computable actual) and the five plan-comparability-caveated nodes — they are shown with their reason, not hidden.
2. **Drill-down cardinality fidelity: the drill-down count equals the number of scorecard rows with `breaches_threshold == True`, exactly, and the keys match in order.** Equality, not a band: "never padded to a fixed count" (Section 5) and "never truncated" are both violated by any inequality, in either direction. Zero is a valid result and renders as an explicit statement that nothing breached.
3. **Source-trace fidelity: 100% of the readout's numeric fields re-read identically from a freshly-run `run_diagnostic()`, at exact float equality.** Since assembly performs no arithmetic, a non-zero difference anywhere means a computation was introduced into the artifact that is specified not to compute — which would place that computation outside the validation of the artifact that owns it.

`analytics-model-validator` confirms or adjusts these three as the right criteria for an assembly artifact before they are treated as settled — they carry the same PROPOSED status the variance engine's ±8% threshold and the segment-migration analysis's 10% firmographic-rescore floor carry.

### Notes, details and cross-references in the payload

Text the dashboard prints verbatim is written as one short declarative line per item. `plan_comparability_note` (scorecard rows) and each drill-down `notes` entry are the short form; the long form with every disclosed figure sits beside it as `plan_comparability_detail` and `notes_detail` (`[{note, detail}]`). The NRR and GRR drill-downs list their drivers under the metric tree's single child, "See Growth — expansion, contraction, churn drivers" (GRR: "contraction, churn drivers"): the tree defines that child as a reference, and the engine expands it into the named drivers as shares of starting revenue. Those engine nodes are labelled "See Growth: Expansion (share of starting revenue)" and so on, carry `cross_reference_to` and `cross_reference_label` naming the Growth node each mirrors, and the drill-down's first note states the tree definition. The tree file is unchanged.

### Validation — structural and trace checks, not statistics

`run_build_time_validation(as_of_date)` in `analytics/weekly_readout.py` runs the engine once, assembles, renders, and runs both check suites: `verify_source_trace()` (20 checks — the correctness claim, field-by-field against the engine output, including the Not-computable count, two playbook-triggers checks, two forecast checks, two persistence checks and three segment-mix checks) and `verify_rendered_document()` (13 checks — that the Markdown says what the structure says, including playbook-triggers and forecast numeric re-derivation checks, the persistence line and the segment-mix section). Both return a list of `{name, passed, detail}` rather than raising, so a validator sees every failure at once.

**Build-time result — `as_of_date` 2025-11-30 (the engine's own canonical checkpoint; 2025-12 carries the end-of-window truncation artifact): 33 of 33 checks pass.**

| Check | Result at 2025-11-30 |
|---|---|
| `required_sections_present` | pass — all eight sections present (Section 5's seven and segment mix) |
| `layer1_scorecard_has_all_11_nodes` | pass — **11 of 11** |
| `not_computable_count_matches_scorecard_status` | pass — **1 node** (Activation) reports status `Not computable`: the section's `nodes_not_computable` and `nodes_not_computable_keys` equal the rows whose status is `Not computable` and the engine's own scorecard.
