The rule counts the status the card shows, not the variance mechanism: Activation's mechanism is `trailing_baseline` (so a mechanism-based count read 0 while the card said Not computable), and its value is degenerate (blended time to first Action is 0 in every month, so the baseline is 0 and no variance exists). The rendered document states the same count on a line above the scorecard tables |
| `layer1_scorecard_node_keys_match_engine` | pass — the engine's own rows, in its own order |
| `every_scorecard_row_is_layer_1` | pass — re-read from `_TREE`, not from the emitted record alone |
| `scorecard_values_trace_exactly_to_engine` | pass — `actual`, `plan`, `variance_pct`, `trailing_baseline`, `baseline_variance_pct`, `prior_month_value`, `status`, `mechanism`, `pillar`, `plan_comparability`, `breaches_threshold` on all 11 rows |
| `drilldown_count_equals_breach_count` | pass — **9 drill-downs, 9 breaching nodes** |
| `drilldown_keys_are_exactly_the_breaching_nodes` | pass |
| `drilldown_layers_are_structurally_sound` | pass — every drill-down head is Layer 1, every outlier is Layer 2 *and* a child of that head, every leaf is Layer 3 *and* a child of that outlier |
| `watchlist_traces_exactly_to_engine` | pass — **25 accounts**, same order, `churn_probability` / `health_score` / `est_arr_at_risk_usd` exact |
| `forecast_section_is_built_and_declares_its_status` | pass — status `present`, forecast call 2025-11-28 not after the 2025-11-30 period end |
| `forecast_traces_exactly_to_a_fresh_run` | pass — a second, independent `run_forecast(2025-11-28)` equals the section at exact equality: 2 segments × 4 lenses plus counts, flags, ML-lens context, caveats and data-window note |
| `playbook_triggers_section_is_built_and_present` | pass — status is `present`, not `not_yet_built` |
| `playbook_triggers_trace_exactly_to_a_fresh_run` | pass — **1,558 trigger rows**, every `rule_id`/`account_id`/`timestamp`/`resulting_action` matches a freshly-run `analytics/playbook_triggers.py.run_playbook_triggers(as_of_date)` exactly |
| `drilldown_persistence_matches_engine_output` | pass — all 9 drill-downs carry the engine's record untouched, tracking their own Layer-2 outlier, with a flag that agrees with the streak |
| `drilldown_persistence_traces_exactly_to_a_fresh_engine_run` | pass — a second `run_diagnostic()` at the same as-of date produces identical records |
| `segment_mix_section_is_built_and_declares_its_status` | pass — status `present`, the reporting month, the three segments in order, graduated MRR reconciled to the growth bridge |
| `segment_mix_traces_exactly_to_a_fresh_run` | pass — equals a fresh `compute_segment_mix()` at exact equality, less display strings |
| `segment_mix_shares_tie_to_ending_mrr` | pass — shares sum to 1 and tie to ending MRR; upmarket share is Commercial plus Enterprise |
| `executive_summary_slot_honors_contract` | pass — the slot is `generated` (passing grounding validation, `input_hash` equal to this readout's, statements re-validated now) or explicitly `not_generated`/`validation_failed` with a reason and no statements; at the committed checkpoints it is `not_generated` (`no_api_key`) |
| 13 rendered-document checks | pass — all 11 Layer-1 labels in the Markdown; exactly one `### N. ` drill-down heading per breaching node (a regex scoped to that heading shape, not a `>=` count against every `### ` heading in the document); the playbook-triggers heading carries its own count; the forecast section is rendered per its status (`forecast_section_rendered_per_status`: heading, call date, quarter and days to quarter end for `present`, or the status and reason with no forecast figures for `unavailable`); the executive summary is rendered as exactly what its status says (`executive_summary_rendered_per_status`: validated statements with provenance, or the status and reason with no prose); watchlist rendered; the persistence line of every drill-down (`drilldown_persistence_rendered_per_payload`), the segment-mix section per its status (`segment_mix_section_rendered_per_status`), and five **numeric re-derivation** checks (`scorecard_rendered_values_match_structured_payload`, `watchlist_rendered_values_match_structured_payload`, `playbook_triggers_rendered_values_match_structured_payload`, `forecast_rendered_values_match_structured_payload` [each segment's table row and CRO line, the ML-lens context line, every caveat], `drilldown_headings_match_structured_payload`) that rebuild each table row's exact expected line from the same `*_display` fields `render_markdown()` used and require that exact line to appear verbatim — closing a real gap the first version of this check left open: label/heading presence alone would pass even if a rendered *value* were wrong (a formatter bug, a wrong `_UNITS` entry), since nothing compared the rendered number back to the structured payload it came from |

**Drill-downs surfaced at 2025-11-30 (9, variable — not a fixed count):** New logo consumption revenue → *Avg initial commitment* (+101.5% vs. its own trailing baseline; Layer-3 evidence: deal-size trend within band −29.3%, discount rate versus list +13.2%); Expansion consumption revenue → *Overage realization* (+9.5%, single-candidate read, a leaf with no Layer 3; wallet share progression is not computable); Contraction + churned revenue → *Cyclical/planned usage dip* (−56.9%; renewal win rate second at −17.8%; workflow chain under-utilization blank in the censored tail of the window and listed as a missing sibling); Magic number → *S&M cost* (single-candidate read; its Layer-3 evidence is rep fully-loaded cost and marketing spend, ranked on absolute dollars); Consumption payback → *CAC by channel* (−53.9%, branch genuinely 2 layers deep); Onboarding/CS efficiency → *Automated Action volume delivered* (+33.9%, the denominator, not the intuitive numerator); AM efficiency → *AM cost* (single-candidate read, branch genuinely 2 layers deep); NRR → *Expansion share* (`additive_share` basis); GRR → *Contraction share* (`additive_share` basis). Activation and logo retention produced no drill-down — Activation because no variance is computable, logo retention because it is on track at +1.1%. Magic number reads 2.51× against a 0.77× plan (+225.9%) and AM efficiency 2.65× against 2.88× (−8.0%), both under their level-comparability caveats (see the Variance-diagnostic engine entry).

**Second checkpoint, 2025-06-30**: 11 of 11 nodes, 9 drill-downs (3 with a flagged persistence record), 25 watchlist accounts, **1,404 playbook triggers**, a forecast section on the 2025-06-27 call (above), a segment-mix section, 33 of 33 checks pass — confirming the assembly is not tuned to one period. **Zero-drill-down path exercised** by running the same period at an intentionally unreachable threshold: 11 nodes, 0 drill-downs, all checks pass, and the section renders as an explicit "no Layer-1 node breached … that is a real result, not a missing section" rather than an empty heading.

### Drift monitoring — not applicable

This artifact has no calibration to drift, on the same reasoning the Variance-diagnostic engine entry gives. Its three criteria above are binary structural invariants with no sampling variance, so the doc's general 2-consecutive-checkpoint rule does not apply: a single failure is a defect to fix, not a trend to watch. Recurring `drift-monitor` runs apply to the health score, not here.

**Persistence**: structural/trace scalars are logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `log_performance()` — `structural_checks_total` / `structural_checks_passed` (33/33), `layer1_nodes_in_scorecard` (11), `layer1_nodes_not_computable` (1 — Activation), `drilldowns_rendered` (9), `watchlist_accounts` (25), `playbook_triggers_fired` (1,558 — new), `sections_not_yet_built` (0 — no section is an unbuilt placeholder), `forecast_section_available` (1 when the forecast section is `present`, 0 when `unavailable`). The rendered document and the full structured payload do not fit that log's flat scalar grain and live in `analytics/outputs/` instead.

### Known limitations

- **Every gap the readout displays is inherited, not introduced.** The two not-computable Layer-1 nodes, Activation's degenerate TTFA, the seven plan-comparability caveats, the absent Layer-3 evidence and the watchlist's segment-average ARR estimate are all the source artifacts' limitations, reproduced verbatim with their own explanations attached. They close where those artifacts close them — in Phase 1 or Phase 2 — not here.
- **The persistence flag and the segment mix are descriptive.** In aggregate the persistence flag fires about as often as it does on month-shuffled data (Variance-diagnostic engine entry), and a segment's share of MRR moves with expansion, contraction and churn as well as with migration. Both are shown with their basis and caveats, not as findings about cause.
- **The forecast section's ML-lens figures vary slightly across dbt rebuilds.** Measured against a fresh run after a rebuild: up to 0.53% on a lens value (Commercial ML 2025-11-28 +0.0014%, Enterprise ML +0.47%, Commercial ML 2025-06-27 +0.51%, CRO-adjusted ML up to +0.53%) and 0.0001–0.0008 on holdout AUC; flags, widest pairs and pass/fail verdicts unchanged (Forecast entry, Known limitations, for the cause). The section's caveats say so, and the executive summary's `input_hash` covers these figures, so a rebuild can mark a generated summary stale.
- **The document is long because the caveats are long.** Each caveated metric carries its full explanation inline rather than a truncated version, on the reasoning that a reader deciding whether a −44.7% GRR variance is a business finding needs the whole reason it is not. The executive-summary step (next entry) is the piece that turns this into an executive-length read.
- **No rendered-output retention policy.** One `.md`/`.json` pair per `as_of_date`, overwritten on re-run for the same date. Whether readouts accumulate as a historical series or only the latest is kept remains open, and does not need settling until the artifact runs on a cadence.

## Executive summary narrative

- **Built**: `analytics/executive_summary.py` (pipeline node `executive_summary`, after `weekly_readout`). Grain: one summary per assembled weekly-readout JSON — one evaluation period, company-wide — written into that JSON's `executive_summary` slot and re-rendered into the readout Markdown. **Inputs — one**: the structured readout `analytics/weekly_readout.py` wrote. No mart, no database connection, no re-query: the prose and the tables it sits beside come from the same object and cannot disagree. No dbt model was added or changed. This is the build spec's Section 5 executive summary (the narrative that names a specific Layer-2/Layer-3 cause for a real miss), the Claude API integration `analytics/weekly_readout.py` left as a seam.
- **Method**: one Messages API call (`anthropic` SDK) with a tool call as the structured-output mechanism (`tool_choice: auto` plus an explicit instruction to call the tool, because this model family rejects forced tool use; a reply with no tool call fails the same way a malformed one does and takes the retry) (`submit_executive_summary` → 1–6 `{text, cites}` statements), the readout passed whole as a delimited data block (`<readout_data>`, `<` escaped inside the JSON so no field can forge the delimiter; the system prompt states the block is data and never instructions), and prompt caching on that block so the one permitted retry re-reads it from cache. Then a deterministic, LLM-free **grounding validator** accepts or rejects the output before anything is published; on failure the validator's specific errors are fed back once, and a second failure publishes nothing. There is no template fallback and no path by which unvalidated text reaches the readout.
- **Model type selection and rationale**: the class is *language model with schema-constrained output, gated by a deterministic validator*. Alternatives considered: (1) **a template / rule-based prose generator** — rejected; it would produce sentences that read as narrative while carrying none of the causal reasoning the section exists for, and the project's standing position (the readout entry above) is that a canned-sentence generator is worse than an honest gap. The same objection is why the no-key path is `not_generated`, not a template. (2) **Free-form LLM prose** — rejected; unchecked prose cannot be tied back to the readout, and "the readout computes nothing" would stop being true of the page a CRO reads. Forced-tool output with per-statement `cites` is what makes every claim checkable. (3) **A second LLM as judge of the first** — rejected; it adds a second stochastic opinion where a deterministic rule set suffices, and its verdicts would not be reproducible. (4) **Extractive summarisation of the scorecard** — rejected; it cannot state a causal chain across Layer 1 → Layer 2, which is the point. The validator is deterministic by design: same readout, same statements, same verdict.
- **Model, prompt and parameters**: default model `claude-sonnet-5-5`, overridden by `ACME_SUMMARY_MODEL` or the `model=` argument; the id the API reports is what is recorded. `PROMPT_VERSION = "exec-summary-v5"` (bumped when the persistence rule and its validator check were added and when the check was extended to more phrasings, which invalidates any summary generated under an earlier version); `max_tokens` 8000 (thinking tokens count against it); no sampling parameters are set. At most two calls per run (first try plus one retry). The prompt view is about 67 KB of JSON (about 17k input tokens) for either committed readout: the scorecard rows, drill-downs, watchlist, coverage and header, with the duplicated by-pillar tables and the 1,404–1,558 playbook-trigger rows dropped (the count and the stored rules stay).
- **Point-in-time design**: `as_of_date` enters only through the readout file the step is pointed at (`weekly_readout_<as_of_date>.json`); the step adds no data access and therefore no leakage surface.
- **Determinism / seeding**: the model's sampling is the one stochastic step and is not seedable through the API. Reproducibility is therefore provided by **caching against the input**, not by a seed: the slot carries `input_hash` (sha256 of the canonical readout excluding the `executive_summary` key); a still-valid generated summary with a matching hash and `prompt_version` is reused with no API call, so a re-run, a readout re-assembly and a pipeline re-run do not churn published text. A changed input is stale and regenerates (or, with no key, is reported `not_generated` with reason `stale_summary_no_api_key`; the stale text is not carried forward). Everything except the model call is deterministic.

### Output contract

`readout["executive_summary"]` is always present, always one of three statuses, and never carries prose unless it was validated:

| Field | `generated` | `not_generated` | `validation_failed` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `status` | `generated` | `not_generated` | `validation_failed` |
| `statements` | 1–6 × `{text, cites}` | `[]` | `[]` (rejected text is stored nowhere) |
| `model` / `generated_at` | model id the API reported; UTC ISO timestamp | `null` | recorded (a call was made) |
| `input_hash`, `prompt_version` | sha256 of the readout input; `exec-summary-v5`
| `validation` | `{passed: true, checks: [19 × {name, passed, detail}]}` | `{passed: null, checks: []}` | `{passed: false, checks, errors}` |
| `reason` / `note` | absent | `no_api_key`, `pending_generation`, `stale_summary_no_api_key`, `api_error` (with a redacted `detail`) or `anthropic_sdk_not_installed` | `grounding_validation_failed_after_retry` |
| `attempts`, `usage` | calls made (1–2); summed input/output/cache token counts | present after an API error | present |

`weekly_readout.verify_source_trace()`'s `executive_summary_slot_honors_contract` check enforces the contract on every assembly (a `generated` summary must carry passing validation, an `input_hash` equal to the readout it is published with, and statements that re-validate now); `verify_rendered_document()`'s `executive_summary_rendered_per_status` check enforces that the Markdown shows exactly what the status says.

### Grounding validator — what is checked, deterministically

`validate_statements(readout, statements)` returns `{passed, checks, errors}` over 19 named checks; `errors` are worded to be fed back to the model. Everything echoed back into the retry prompt (the errors quote readout labels and the model's own text) is sanitized first: angle brackets escaped, whitespace and control characters collapsed, the delimiter's name and role-prefix lookalikes defused, length capped.

| Check | Rule |
|---|---|
| `schema_valid` | a non-empty list of `{text, cites}`; `cites` a non-empty list of strings; no extra fields |
| `statement_count_in_range` | 3–6 statements when a headline driver exists, 1–6 when no drill-down has a Layer-2 outlier ("nothing notable" is a valid, short summary) |
| `cites_resolve` | every cite is an id the readout defines (`header`, `data_window`, `scorecard`, `scorecard:<metric_key>`, `drilldowns`, `drilldown:<layer1_metric_key>`, `watchlist`, `watchlist:<account_id>`, `playbook_triggers`, `forecast`, `forecast:<segment>`, `coverage:<metric_key>`, `provenance`) |
| `numbers_grounded_in_cited_objects` | every figure in a statement — percent, dollar amount (`$45.2K`, `$1.2M`, `$121,963.53`), multiple (`2.5x`), count, ratio, scientific-notation value — matches a value **inside the objects that statement cites**, within the rounding the prose itself displays (half a unit of the last digit shown; sign ignored; a percentage also matches its fraction). **Units are checked**: every number in the readout carries a kind (currency, percent/fraction, multiple, count, plain number), typed from the field, the scorecard row's unit or the node's own unit, and numbers written inside strings take the unit they are written in. A `$`-denominated figure matches only a currency value, a `%` figure only a percent/fraction value, an `x` multiple only a ratio, a bare number a count, plain number or raw fraction/ratio (a scaled `1.6K` with no `$` may match a currency value or a count; a scientific-notation figure copied from a display string matches any unit). `$919.1`, `919.1x`, `1.28x` for a 128.2% fraction, `25%` against a count of 25, `1,558%` and `$1.6K` against a count of 1,558 are all rejected, and the error names the unit conflict. A figure that exists elsewhere in the readout but not in the cited objects is rejected with the cite to add |
| `dates_grounded` | every ISO date, `Month D[, YYYY]`, `D Month [YYYY]` (day-month order is checked), `Month YYYY`, numeric `m/d/y` or `d/m/y` date, quarter label (`Q3 2025`, `2025-Q3`, `Q3`, "third quarter"), lone month name and bare year must be a date, quarter, month or year the readout contains; the quarters allowed are the forecast section's quarter and the reporting period's quarter. Half-year labels (`H1`, `H2`) are rejected. The lone-month-name check skips "May" (also a verb) |
| `account_ids_grounded` | every account id is a watchlist row, and is cited as `watchlist:<id>` |
| `no_spelled_out_numbers_or_derived_quantities` | figures are digits (so they can be verified): number words ("two" to "ninety", "hundred", "dozen", and "one" except in idioms such as "no one"), fractions and vague quantities ("a third", "a quarter of", "an order of magnitude", "majority", "nearly all"), and "double", "half", "-fold" style claims, which state a ratio the readout does not contain, are rejected |
| `layer_labels_match_tree_depth` | no node is labelled with a layer it does not sit at (`Win rate (Layer 1)` is rejected; `Layer 4` does not exist); the same structural guarantee the variance engine gives, applied to prose |
| `top_driver_named` | the headline driver (below) is named — Layer-1 label and Layer-2 outlier label in one statement that cites the drill-down; `single-candidate read` appears when the engine reports `is_genuine_sibling_comparison = false`; a non-outlier sibling is not presented as the outlier |
| `caveats_stated_when_caveated_metric_used` | any statement that names a `caveated` node or cites its scorecard row contains the word "caveated" |
| `not_computable_stated` | any statement that uses a node whose status is `Not computable` says so |
| `status_words_match_readout` | "ahead of plan", "behind plan", "on track" wording agrees with the readout's own status for the one node the sentence names |
| `layer3_claims_match_drilldown_status` | a non-negated Layer-3 claim may cite only drill-downs whose `layer3_status` is `surfaced`; "no Layer 3 is computable" is the honest form elsewhere |
| `non_additive_nodes_not_summed` | Brand & awareness and Marketing–sales handoff quality are never presented as summing into the pipeline math |
| `persistence_claims_match_cited_drilldown` | a **vocabulary and pattern check**, not a reading of meaning. A sentence is a streak claim when it states a length (`3 consecutive months`, `3 months in a row` or `running`, `a 2-month streak`, `streak of 3`, `has persisted for 4 months`, `adverse for the last 4 months`, an ordinal such as `second month running` or `2nd month`), says `since <month>` (ISO, `Month YYYY`, or a bare month), uses persist/streak/consecutive/successive/month after month, or, in a sentence that names a cited driver, uses again, recurring, sustained, repeat, ongoing, continued, keeps, an improvement word (improved, recovering, rebounded, better) or a one-off word (only this month, one-off, isolated, not a repeat). It must name the Layer-2 driver of a drill-down the statement cites, by its label or, for the NRR and GRR drivers, by forms such as `expansion share` or `NRR's expansion share`, and a driver named but not cited is rejected. Then for EVERY driver the sentence names: a stated length equals `streak_months` (with several lengths and drivers each length is paired with the nearest preceding driver mention) and the record is not `not_applicable`; `since` equals `first_month_of_streak`; a bare repeat word needs status `flagged`; a one-off word is rejected when flagged; an improvement word is rejected when the streak is at least 1 month; a rise word for a driver whose adverse direction is down (or a fall word for one whose adverse direction is up) is rejected when the sentence names only that driver; the streak is adverse months only, so `favorable` is rejected; a denial (`has not persisted`) is rejected when flagged, and a denial of a stated length when the streak reaches it. A denial naming no driver asserts nothing and passes. Labels that contain `vs.` are not split into two sentences, and a word inside a label (`Growth` in `See Growth: ...`) is not read as a claim |
| `forecast_statements_attributed_and_consistent` | any statement that cites the forecast section (`forecast` or `forecast:<segment>`) says "forecast"; names the forecast call date (`forecast_as_of_date`, as ISO or "Month D" — the call is not as of the reporting period's end, so the period end is not a substitute); does not call a segment's lenses divergent when the forecast artifact's own `diverges_materially` is false, or in agreement when it is true; and, when the section is `unavailable`, says so (any figure it quotes fails `numbers_grounded_in_cited_objects` as there is none to match) |
| `figures_bound_to_labelled_object` | label binding, applied conservatively (an error only when the binding is clearly violated). **Segment**: a sentence naming one segment may not quote a figure that lives only in another segment's forecast object, and may not pair a watchlist account with a segment other than the one the watchlist lists. **Forecast lens**: a dollar figure placed beside a lens name (rep, manager, ML, CRO-adjusted, CRO-adjusted ML; nearest preceding mention, else nearest following, with no other dollar figure between) may not be another lens's value rather than that lens's. **Metric**: a sentence naming a scorecard or drill-down metric label may not quote a figure that lives only in other metrics' scorecard or drill-down objects (Contraction figures under a New logo label are rejected whether or not the Contraction row is also cited) |
| `late_quarter_read_carries_caveat` | a statement that gives a CRO-adjusted figure (CRO-adjusted lens, CRO-adjusted ML lens or the override amount) for a segment the view flags `late_quarter_degenerate` — a logged CRO override with a CRO-adjusted lens above the open pipeline, the forecast caveat's own late-quarter condition — must say it is a late-quarter read and that the CRO override is "not scaled" to the remaining pipeline. At 2025-06-30 this is the Commercial row (2 open deals, $38.5K open pipeline) |
| `banned_terms_and_length` | after whitespace normalization: no "tier" in any form (`Tier1`, "tiered", "tiering"; the watchlist's risk level is written "High risk"), no legacy segment names, no non-USD currency (symbols, codes in any case, currency names such as euros, pounds sterling, yen, Canadian dollars), no forward-looking or speculative wording of the model's own ("will", "won't", "would", "could", "may", "might", "expect", "project", "outlook", "trajectory", "is set to", "poised", "next quarter", "next fiscal quarter", "by year-end", "heading toward", "in coming months"; the forecast section's figures may be restated as the forecast artifact reports them, never extrapolated), no causal wording ("because", "due to", "caused by", "causes", "driven by", "thanks to", "owing to", "attributable to", "stems from", "led to", "explained by", "as a result", "results in", "on account of"), each statement under 600 characters (the prompt states the same limit) |

**Headline driver — this artifact's own selection rule, PROPOSED, not yet confirmed.** The tree and the variance engine rank siblings within one Layer-1 node; nothing ranks the Layer-1 nodes against each other, and with the ±8% threshold 9 of 11 nodes breach at monthly grain, so "the top driver" needs a stated rule. `select_headline()`: among drill-downs with an identifiable Layer-2 outlier, **comparable plan level first** (a `caveated` node's gap measures a definitional difference, not performance), then **unfavourable status**, then the **largest absolute variance from plan**. The prompt tells the model which driver this is and the validator requires it to be named; the other drill-downs are passed in the same order for the two-sided story (what helped, what offset). Expansion consumption revenue and Contraction + churned revenue are `caveated` (see the variance-diagnostic entry's level-comparability section: the actual's gross month-on-month buckets against a plan derived from the benchmark NRR and GRR), so neither can be the headline while a comparable node has a Layer-2 outlier. The comparable nodes with an outlier are New logo consumption revenue and Onboarding/CS efficiency. At 2025-06-30 the headline is New logo consumption revenue (−9.0% against plan, Behind) → Win rate (+65.8% against its own trailing baseline, moving the favourable way), a genuine comparison among three siblings with three Layer-3 leaves surfaced. At 2025-11-30 the headline is New logo consumption revenue (+128.2%, Ahead) → Avg initial commitment (+101.5% against its baseline), again among three siblings with Layer-3 evidence; no comparable node is Behind at that date, so the ranking falls through to the largest absolute variance. The headline is therefore not always an adverse node, and the prompt's two-sided story (what helped, what offset) carries the caveated Contraction and Expansion nodes in the order the rule gives them. Before the two nodes were caveated the headline at both dates was Contraction + churned revenue (+800.1% and +919.1% against plan), a single-candidate read; that selection no longer occurs. A summary generated before the change is stale (the readout's `input_hash` covers the plan-comparability fields) and is regenerated, not carried forward; the committed readouts carry `not_generated` / `no_api_key`, so none exists.

### Validation — structural and adversarial, not statistical

Per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' "Structural/logic artifacts" category: **there is no coefficient table, AUC, confusion matrix or R² here, and their absence is deliberate, not pending.** No model is fitted in this repository; the deterministic validator is the thing whose correctness is claimed, and the claim is checked on real data without an API call:

- **Golden sets on the real readouts.** For each committed readout (2025-06-30, 2025-11-30) a hand-authored 6-statement set (test fixtures only, never written to `analytics/outputs/`) must pass all 19 checks, and a variant that swaps its last statement for a faithful forecast statement built from the readout's own display strings must pass too. Forecast tampering that must be rejected: another segment's figure under this segment's cite, an invented or averaged lens figure, a missing call date (or the period end in its place), a forecast cite without the word "forecast", a divergence claim contradicting the artifact's flag in either direction, four forward-looking phrasings, and an unavailable section described without saying so or with a figure.
- **Tampered variants that must be rejected**, per date: an altered figure, an invented figure, a real figure outside the cited objects (with the suggested cite), an unresolved cite, a statement with no cites, the headline-driver statements removed, the headline driver named without its drill-down cite, `single-candidate read` removed, the caveat word removed, "tier" in three forms, eight banned-vocabulary phrases (legacy segment name, non-USD currency, forward-looking, hard-causal), a Layer-2 node labelled Layer 1, a Layer-1 node labelled Layer 2, a nonexistent Layer 4, a Layer-3 claim where none is computable, "ahead of plan" for a Behind node, a Not-computable node used without saying so, an unknown account id, an uncited account id, three dates not in the readout, four spelled-out / derived-quantity phrases, a non-additive node presented as summing, statement counts of 2 and 7, and seven malformed statement shapes. Hardening cases, each rejected and each with an accepted counterpart: ten figures in the wrong unit and five faithful unit variants; the other segment's figure labelled Commercial, a manager figure labelled ML and a rep figure labelled ML (and the swap back accepted), Contraction figures under a New logo label with and without the New logo row cited, a Layer-2 label with another branch's figures, an account paired with the wrong segment; fifteen quarter or date strings the readout does not contain (`Q3 2025`, `2025-Q3`, "third quarter", `Q4` in a Q2 readout, "30 July", `7/30/2025`, `30/07/2025`, a wrong year) and twelve it does (`30 June`, `June 30`, `6/30/2025`, `30/06/2025`, `Q2 2025`, "second quarter of 2025"); twelve number-word or vague-quantity phrases, seventeen causal phrasings including `due  to`, `due` + newline and `due`-NBSP-`to`, fifteen forward-looking phrasings, thirteen banned-vocabulary phrasings; five ordinary phrasings that must not be over-rejected; a hostile headline label containing a closing delimiter, role-prefix lookalikes and a line separator, which must not survive into the retry prompt; a forged cite string; a late-quarter CRO-adjusted figure with and without the caveat wording, and the same figure in a normal-quarter call; and a count of untyped numbers in the committed readouts, which must be zero so that a new numeric field cannot silently loosen unit matching. A synthetic variant drops the contraction drill-down so New logo becomes the headline and confirms that presenting a non-outlier sibling (Win rate) as *the* outlier is rejected. Every cite-able figure the readouts display (scorecard value/plan/variance strings, drill-down and sibling-ranking strings, watchlist ARR estimates, section counts: 135 indexed per readout) is confirmed to resolve within its own cite.
- **Persistence wording.** A faithful streak statement for every drill-down of both readouts passes, and the same statement with the length plus 1 fails; at 2025-06-30 `Renewal win rate has been the adverse outlier for 3 consecutive months.` passes all 19 checks. 44 rejected phrasings and 19 accepted ones are pinned in `tests/test_executive_summary.py`. Rejected: lengths that disagree with the record in every pattern above (including `2`, which is also the threshold and so passes number grounding on its own); `since` months that are not the first month of the streak, and `since` for a driver with no streak; improvement and opposite-direction words (`persistently risen` for a driver whose adverse direction is down); one-off wording for a flagged streak; again/recurring/sustained/repeat/keeps about a driver that is not flagged; repeat or length claims about a single-candidate read; claims naming no driver, a driver under another drill-down's cite, or a second driver whose drill-down is not cited; a flagged streak denied; a streak described as favorable; several drivers in one sentence where any one disagrees (and swapped lengths); a spelled-out length (the number-word check). Accepted: faithful lengths, ordinals, `since` phrasings, possessive forms (`NRR's expansion share`), persistence stated as not applicable, denial of a longer streak than the record has, and a flagged driver called recurring. The segment-mix section is checked to be outside the evidence: absent from the view and the valid cites, not citable, and its figures not groundable.
- **Generator with a fake client** (no network): valid first try; invalid-then-valid (the retry prompt carries the validator's errors and a byte-identical cached data block); invalid twice (status `validation_failed`, nothing published, rejected text absent from the output); six malformed-response shapes; an API error on the first call and on the retry (status `not_generated`, reason `api_error`, key redacted); the no-key path (no client is ever constructed); model resolution and recording; cache reuse, stale input, changed method version, cached text that no longer validates, `force`; slot-contract acceptance of each honest state and rejection of six dishonest ones; Markdown for each status; the run step end to end against a staged copy of the real readout including a no-churn assertion that nothing but the summary slot changes; performance-log rows for each outcome; and, against the database, that re-assembling the readout preserves a generated summary byte for byte.
- **Prompt construction**: the readout is delimited and marked as data, a field cannot forge the closing delimiter, every hard rule is present in the system prompt, the whole scorecard / all drill-downs / the whole watchlist are in the data block, and the API key is asserted absent from every prompt and output.

**Not verified here, stated plainly**: no live API call has been made — this environment has no key — so **no real model output has been run through the validator and no generated summary exists in `analytics/outputs/`**. What is established is that the prompt is well-formed, the validator accepts faithful prose about the real readouts and rejects the tampered variants, and the pipeline behaves correctly with and without a key. What a first live run will show is whether the model meets the validator's rules within two attempts; if it does not, the result is `validation_failed` with the failing checks listed and no prose published, not a bad sentence on a page.

### Target — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed

Derived here; nothing upstream states a target for a language-model artifact. All three are stated as exact equalities, for the reason the readout entry gives: the validator is deterministic, so a tolerance band would imply an error source that does not exist.

1. **Validator pass: 100% of published statements pass the validator's 19 checks; any failure means the set is not published.** This is a statement about the checks, not about the prose: passing means every figure appears, within its displayed rounding and with unit and sign handled as implemented, in an object the statement cites, with segment, forecast-lens and metric label binding checked where implemented (listed in the table above and in Known limitations). It does not mean the prose is the right story or a causal explanation. Enforced at publication, and re-checked against the current readout every time the slot is read by `executive_summary_slot_honors_contract`.
2. **Zero unvalidated prose shown**: a `not_generated` or `validation_failed` slot carries no statements, and the Markdown renders the status and reason instead. Enforced by the slot contract and by `executive_summary_rendered_per_status`.
3. **Headline fidelity: when a summary is published, it names the driver the variance engine ranked first under the selection rule above, and inherits that drill-down's single-candidate / Layer-3 / caveat wording.**

`analytics-model-validator` confirms or adjusts these, and the headline selection rule, before they are treated as settled.

### Drift monitoring — not applicable (executive summary narrative)

No fitted model, no calibration. The three criteria are binary invariants, and the model's output is not seedable, so a single failed run is a review trigger, not a trend. **Proposed operational review trigger — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed:** two consecutive runs with `validation_failed` (the prompt or the validator needs a look), or any run whose published summary later fails `executive_summary_slot_honors_contract`. Recurring `drift-monitor` runs apply to the health score, not here.

**Persistence**: scalars are logged to `fact_model_performance_history` as model `executive_summary_narrative` on every run, including runs that generate nothing: `narrative_generated` (1/0), `statements_published`, `validation_failed` (1/0) and, when a call was made, `grounding_checks_total`, `grounding_checks_passed`, `api_attempts`. A run without a key logs `narrative_generated = 0` — which is what lets the freshness contract tell "the step ran and nothing was generated" apart from "the step did not run".

### Known limitations

- **Grounded in the readout is not the same as a correct causal explanation.** What the validator checks: that each figure appears in a cited object within its displayed rounding, in its written unit and sign-insensitively; that dates, quarters, months, years, account ids and cites exist in the readout; that the engine's top-ranked driver is named with its single-candidate / Layer-3 / caveat status; that caveated and Not-computable nodes are labelled so; that Layer labels match tree depth; that segment, forecast-lens and metric labels sit with their own figures where the binding is unambiguous; that a late-quarter CRO-adjusted read carries its caveat; and the vocabulary lists. What it does not check: that the sentence draws a correct conclusion, that a direction word ("rose", "fell", "widened") matches the sign of the quoted change (only "ahead of plan / behind plan / on track" is checked against status), that an unlabelled figure belongs to the object the prose implies, that two figures in one sentence are the right pair, that statements are the right selection or the right story, or that a causal reading is warranted. The engine ranks deviation from a trailing baseline, which says where a miss concentrates, not why; at both committed checkpoints the headline Layer-2 outlier (Win rate at 2025-06-30, Avg initial commitment at 2025-11-30) is moving the favourable way against its own baseline, so even where the parent is Behind the honest summary says where the movement concentrates rather than naming a cause.
- **Residual limits of the figure checks, measured.** Rounding tolerance and a finite set of values mean a fabricated figure can still match by coincidence. Measured as 2,001 three-significant-digit percentages from 1% to 300% offered as fabricated figures (`%` tokens): against the union of every cite scope, 14.3% (2025-06-30) and 15.5% (2025-11-30) matched with value-only matching; with unit-aware matching 4.9% and 5.9% match. Against typical one-to-three-cite scopes (300 seeded random draws from scorecard, drill-down, forecast, header and trigger scopes), the mean fell from 1.9% and 1.8% to 0.43% and 0.50%, and the worst draw from 5.6% and 6.0% to 2.4% and 3.1%. Those residual matches are real percent-typed values (variance, deviation, rate and probability fields) that a fabricated percentage happens to round to; they are not eliminated, only reduced, and a model that cites a broad set of objects has a larger target. Label binding is skipped when a sentence names two segments (figure positions are ambiguous), when a lens name has no currency figure beside it, when a metric label is too short or common to bind (for example "Paid"), and when a figure also matches a value in a neutral cited object (header, section-level), so coarse rounding ("$1.3M" matches rep, manager and ML at once) also defeats lens binding.
- **A generated summary goes stale when the readout gains a section.** The persistence records and the segment-mix section changed the readout's `input_hash` and the prompt version moved to v4, so a summary generated earlier is not reused; with no key the slot reports `not_generated` (reason `stale_summary_no_api_key` when an earlier summary existed). A live run is needed to publish a v4 summary; none has been made in this repository, so the persistence wording rule, like the rest of the prompt, is covered by the fake-client and tampered-variant tests only.
- **The persistence gate is a vocabulary and pattern check that paraphrases can evade.** It catches the wordings listed in the check's row and holds each to the cited record. It does not catch a paraphrase none of the patterns covers (`has led the adverse moves each month since the spring` passes), a claim about a driver the sentence refers to only by a pronoun or by a noun phrase that is not one of its labels or forms (a count with no named driver is caught, a pronoun claim without a count or a repeat word is not), a length written as a spelled-out word in a form the number-word check does not list, a claim split across sentences (the second sentence names no driver, so it is rejected, but the first is judged alone), or a rise or fall word attached to the wrong object in a sentence that names one driver. The rise/fall check applies only to a sentence naming a single driver and cannot tell which object a direction word describes. Residual rate: unmeasured; the check was built against a list of known bypasses, not sampled against model output, and no live model output has been run through it. A summary that passes says its streak figures match the record, not that its sentences are free of misleading phrasing.
- **Model nondeterminism.** Two runs on the same readout can produce different, both valid, text; reuse-by-`input_hash` is what keeps the published text stable, and `--force` is the deliberate way to re-roll it.
- **Strictness can reject valid prose.** Figures must match within displayed rounding inside the cited objects and in the readout's own unit, labels must be the readout's own, the caveat word must be literal, the late-quarter wording must contain "not scaled", and the vocabulary lists include everyday words ("would", "could", "may", "one"). The forecast section's own caveats are paraphrased, not quoted: three of them contain wording the lists reject ("expected closed-won", number words such as "two" and "four"), so the prompt asks for a paraphrase in digits instead of verbatim copying. A model that paraphrases a label or forgets a cite is sent back once with the specific error; if it still fails the result is `validation_failed` and no text, which is the intended failure direction.
- **No qualitative context.** Build spec Section 5 asks the narrative to pull in AM notes and exit-interview-style reasons "where available". The readout carries none, and the step reads only the readout, so none is used or invented.
- **The forecast section may be restated, never extended.** The readout's forecast section is the forecast artifact's own output at an earlier call date than the reporting period's end, in a different grain and unit from the scorecard (quarter, opportunity dollars, Commercial and Enterprise). The prompt and validator let the summary restate those figures with the lens, segment, quarter and call date, and forbid extrapolating, combining lenses into a figure of its own or describing a lens as "the" forecast. Whether the summary picks the section up at all is the model's choice; the headline driver and scorecard come first. The forecast rules (prompt v3) are covered by the fake-client and tampered-variant tests only; like the rest of this entry, they have not been exercised against a live model output in this repository.
- **Cost (estimate, not measured)**: about 17k input tokens per call at Sonnet-class list prices is on the order of $0.05–$0.10 for a first-try success and about twice that with the retry (the retry reads the data block from cache); one run per weekly readout.
- **Headline selection rule and the 600-character bound are this artifact's own proposals**, not tree or build-spec values.

## Capacity planning

- **Built**: `analytics/capacity_planning.py`. Grain: one row per quota-bearing rep (ISR/AE) per **complete** calendar quarter in which that rep had at least one active day, quarter end ≤ `as_of_date`. Everything the module publishes — attainment, ramp-adjusted capacity, the achievability assessment — is an aggregation of that one panel. No dbt model was added or changed for this artifact, and no Phase 1 generator or raw table was added; quota history, rep status history and rep dimensions all already exist as real generated data.
- **What it is**: build spec item #3, the artifact that absorbs quota setting and attainment analysis — "is the quota mathematically achievable given capacity, not just headcount count." The distinction it exists to make is that **two teams with identical headcount and identical stated quota do not have identical real capacity if their ramp mixes differ**, and this module turns that from an assertion into a computed, decomposed number.
- **Scope — ISR/AE only, deliberately**: `generators/reps.py` scopes `quota_history` to ISR and AE ("the new-business, quota-bearing roles per build spec Section 2; AM comp isn't described as quota-based in either doc"), and `dim_reps` carries a null `quota_amount` for every AM and SE period. Quota attainment and quota achievability are therefore only meaningful concepts for ISR/AE. **AM headcount/book-size capacity is a related but distinct question and is not forced into a quota-attainment framing it does not have** — it already has a home in the metric tree (Onboarding/CS efficiency and AM efficiency, both `mart_efficiency` nodes, whose Layer-2 children name "book size, ramp status, check-in cadence" explicitly). This artifact does not duplicate or compete with it. SE carries no quota either and is likewise out of scope.
- **Relationship to the metric tree**: this is not a parallel concept to anything in the tree — it computes two nodes the tree already names. **"Rep capacity / ramp mix"** is a Layer-3 leaf under Win rate (Growth → New logo consumption revenue → Win rate), and this module's `ramping_quota_share` / `ramp_capacity_haircut` per team-quarter is exactly that leaf's ramp-mix quantity, now computable. **"Rep fully-loaded cost, incl. ramp"** sits under S&M cost in Magic Number's branch; this module supplies the *ramp* half of that leaf (which rep-quarters are ramping, and what fraction of capacity that costs) but **not** the cost half — no rep comp/cost source exists anywhere in Phase 1, which is the same gap that makes `magic_number` and `am_efficiency` structurally not-computable in `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`. That gap is named here, not papered over with an assumed comp figure.
- **Shape decision — structural/descriptive, not predictive**: every number in this module is a direct computation from real empirical inputs (observed closed-won ARR, stated quota from `dim_reps`, hire dates, status history) plus one ramp rule taken verbatim from the build spec. Nothing is fitted; there is no train/test split, no estimated parameter, no probability. Per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' **"Structural/logic artifacts"** category — named there for the variance-diagnostic engine, applied here on the same reasoning the Segment migration entry uses — **there is no coefficient table, no R²/RMSE, no residual diagnostics, no AUC, no confusion matrix and no calibration note here, and their absence is deliberate, not pending.** This is stated explicitly rather than left as blank/TBD statistics fields, which would misleadingly imply they are still coming. Note this differs from the conventions doc's *expectation* that capacity planning would be a regression model ("Regression models (forecast, capacity planning — not yet built)"): that line was written before the data was examined. Against the data that actually exists, there is nothing here to regress — see the next bullet.
- **Model type selection and rationale (why not a fitted model)**: a regression predicting rep-quarter attainment from tenure, segment, quota level and headcount was the credible alternative and was rejected on three grounds. (1) **The ramp effect is already an exact known rule, not something to estimate.** Build spec Section 4 states it ("unramped reps at ~50% quota capacity for first 2 quarters") and `generators/config.py`'s `RAMPING_REP_WIN_ASSIGNMENT_FACTOR = 0.55` is the mechanism that makes it true in the data. A tenure coefficient fitted on this data would mostly be re-deriving the generator's own ramp rule — the same trivial-accuracy failure mode the Account health score entry's calibration note documents for scoring inside a deterministic decline window, and the same reason the Segment migration entry rejected a migration-propensity classifier. (2) **The quantity a planner needs is a sum, not a conditional expectation.** "Does this team's aggregate quota exceed its aggregate achievable capacity" is answered by adding up rep-level capacity; a fitted model would replace an exact sum with a noisy estimate of it, the same argument the Variance-diagnostic engine entry makes for walking a known arithmetic identity rather than fitting coefficients to it. (3) **Sample size does not support the alternative anyway** — 673 rep-quarters total, of which only 105 are ramping, spread over 2 rep types; a per-team fitted tenure effect would rest on ~50 observations each of a lumpy, low-count outcome (an AE closes ~0.35 deals per quarter, and 53.1% of all rep-quarters close zero). The empirical baseline this module computes instead is a single pooled ratio per team over all 268–300 ramped rep-quarters, which is the more stable read of the same data. A genuinely predictive rep-level artifact exists elsewhere in the portfolio — build spec item #14, "Rep productivity & coaching diagnostics," Wave 4 — and is not pre-empted here.
- **Inputs**, both read from dbt's marts layer:
  - `dim_reps` (`rep_id`, `rep_type`, `segment`, `hire_date`, `period_start_date`, `period_end_date`, `quota_amount`, `rep_status`) — one row per rep per capacity period, where a boundary is any date quota or status changed. This is the model the build spec Section 2 designates for exactly this purpose ("joined against `quota_history` and `rep_status_history` for point-in-time capacity queries").
  - `fact_opportunities` (`rep_id`, `is_won`, `amount`, `opportunity_type`, `owner_role`, `close_date`) — actual attainment: closed-won, `opportunity_type = 'new_business'`, ISR/AE-owned, summed by rep and close quarter. SMB's system-owned auto-won records and AM-owned renewal/expansion records are excluded (no quota attaches to either).
  - **Read-scope note, stated rather than assumed**: `analytics-engineering-conventions` says Phase 4 reads `mart_*` tables and never reaches into `stg_`/`int_`/raw. Both tables above are marts-layer models in the `main_marts` schema, so the rule's actual intent — no reaching around the modelling layer — is respected, and `int_rep_capacity_periods` (which `dim_reps` is built on) is deliberately *not* read directly. But no `mart_*`-prefixed table exposes rep-grain quota or rep-grain opportunity outcomes, so the stricter prefix-literal reading the Variance-diagnostic engine entry applies to `fact_revenue_monthly` would leave this artifact unbuildable. Resolved in favour of reading the designated dimension. **A `mart_rep_capacity` exposing the rep-quarter panel is the Phase 2 change that would close this cleanly**, and is worth doing before a second consumer needs the same panel; it is a mart gap, not a reason to reach around anything.
- **Ramp-capacity rule and its grounding**: a rep-quarter is classified `ramping` when the rep's tenure **at the quarter's midpoint** is under 180 days, `ramped` otherwise. Both halves are taken from existing artefacts rather than invented: 180 days is `generators/opportunities.py`'s own `_RAMP_FULL_DAYS`, the cutoff the data was generated against, and it is that file's reading of build spec Section 4's "first 2 quarters." The midpoint evaluation is this artifact's own resolved refinement — evaluating tenure at the quarter *start* makes the rule cover two quarters for a rep hired on the first day of a quarter but three for a rep hired on the last day, whereas the midpoint lands on exactly two quarters for every hire date.
- **Ramp-capacity discount: 0.50, adopted not invented.** Build spec Section 4 states the design assumption directly — "Unramped reps at ~50% quota capacity for first 2 quarters, any segment/role" — and that stated number is what the model applies. It is not taken on the spec's authority alone, because a discount nobody checked against the data would be decoration. `check_ramp_discount_reconciles()` measures what ramping reps actually produced per capacity-day of quota against what ramped reps produced, and confirms the two agree. **Observed pooled ratio: 0.488 at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31 (0.515 at 2025-06-30)** — i.e. a ramping rep really does deliver just under half a ramped rep's output in this data. This reconciles the three numbers in play rather than introducing a fourth: `RAMPING_REP_WIN_ASSIGNMENT_FACTOR = 0.55` is the *mechanism* (a ramping rep gets 0.55× a ramped rep's probability of being assigned a won deal), ~50% is the *stated assumption*, and 0.488–0.515 is the *realized outcome*. The realized figure lands slightly under 0.55 because that factor is a relative weight inside an assignment pool, not a direct multiplier on a rep's output. Per rep_type at 2025-12-31: **AE 0.529, ISR 0.280** (AE 0.558 / ISR 0.271 at 2025-06-30). ISR sits further below the rule than AE; with 46–48 ramping ISR rep-quarters against a lumpy low-count outcome this is within what that sample supports, and the pooled figure is the one the reconciliation check is stated on.
- **Capacity-day weighting (why the ramp ratio is not simply "ramping ARR ÷ ramped ARR")**: ramping reps average **~0.81 of a quarter on seat** — they are hired mid-quarter by definition — so an unadjusted comparison would charge partial tenure to the ramp effect and overstate it by roughly a fifth. Every baseline and every ratio in this module is weighted by `capacity_day_quota` (`stated_quota × coverage_share`), so partial tenure and ramp status are measured as separate things. Stated quota itself is **never** pro-rated: the generator does not reduce it for ramp or for a mid-quarter start, and quota diverging from achievable capacity is precisely what this artifact exists to measure.
- **Quota grounding — the stated quota this artifact measures against is itself derived, not guessed.** `generators/reps.py`'s `_QUOTA_BASE_RANGE` is back-solved from the opportunity generator's realized deal supply over the fully-supplied window (2023-01 onward, the first quarter in which `opportunities.py` generates lost pipeline as well as won deals), measured on fully ramped rep-quarters only: **AE — 2.01 closed new-business opportunities per ramped rep-quarter × 20.98% realized win rate × $250,804 average won deal = $103,619 of ARR per ramped rep-quarter; ISR — 5.98 × 25.85% × $22,595 = $34,363.** Each band's midpoint is that anchor divided by the mean realized step-up multiplier over the window (~1.25×), so the *stated* quota lands on the anchor: AE $60,000–105,000 base (midpoint $82,500, −0.4% off the derived $82,858), ISR $22,500–33,000 (midpoint $27,750, +0.5% off $27,601). Band half-widths are AE ±27.3%, ISR ±18.9%, a meaningful spread since rep-to-rep quota differentiation within a team is real practice, distinct from where the band's midpoint sits. **Resolved decision on the target band**: quota is set *at* a fully ramped rep's realized quarterly production, so a ramped rep on seat all quarter expects ~100% attainment and company-wide blended attainment lands in the mid-80s to mid-90s once ramping and partial-tenure rep-quarters fold in. That is the conventional SaaS shape — a target at, not far above, median rep capacity, genuinely missed and genuinely beaten. It is not set higher because it cannot be: total AE new-business pipeline is **452 opportunities across the whole simulation**, so even at a hypothetical 100% win rate the supply caps what any AE roster can produce (452 × $256,122 = $115.8M). **Realized shape at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31**: company-wide attainment **84.4%** across all 28 quarters and **94.8%** over the fully-supplied 2023+ window (AE 94.9%, ISR 94.6%); across 151 complete rep-years, median **75.7%**, mean **87.0%**, **36.4% at or above 100%** (46.6% over 2023+), max 456.7%. Quarter to quarter the company figure swings from **74.1%** (2025-Q2) to **132.9%** (2025-Q4) — variance above and below, which is what a real target produces.
- **Empirical baseline productivity, derived not assumed**: the baseline is the attainment a *fully ramped* rep of a given `rep_type`/segment actually achieves per capacity-day of quota — observed ramped won ARR ÷ observed ramped capacity-day quota. **At `as_of_date` 2025-12-31: AE 0.9689 (n = 268 ramped rep-quarters), ISR 0.8016 (n = 300), pooled 0.9245 (n = 568).** Expected achievable capacity per rep-quarter is then `stated_quota × baseline_attainment × coverage_share × ramp_factor`, where `ramp_factor` is 0.50 while ramping and 1.0 once ramped. A baseline near 1.0 is the expected consequence of `_QUOTA_BASE_RANGE` being back-solved from ramped-rep deal supply (see the quota-grounding bullet below) — a ramped AE on seat all quarter is now expected to land near quota, which is what a quota is for. ISR sits lower because the baseline spans the full panel including the thin 2020–2022 back-dated era; restricted to the fully-supplied 2023+ window the two teams are level (AE 0.987, ISR 0.988).
- **Two baseline modes, both point-in-time honest** (`compute_ramped_baseline(mode=...)`):
  - `as_of_date` (default, and what the build-time checkpoint reports) — one baseline per team estimated over every complete quarter up to `as_of_date`, applied uniformly. The descriptive read. Two runs at different `as_of_date`s can legitimately assign the same quarter different capacity, because the baseline is itself an as-of estimate; that is stated rather than hidden.
  - `prior_quarters_only` — an expanding window using only quarters strictly *before* the quarter being evaluated, so a quarter's quota is never judged against that same quarter's own realized productivity. The forward-planning read. Reported as NaN until 8 ramped rep-quarters accumulate rather than computed off a handful of lumpy Enterprise deals. **The two modes converge closely** (final-quarter baselines: AE 0.9231 vs. 0.9689, ISR 0.8017 vs. 0.8016), which is the evidence that the default mode's in-sample baseline is not materially circular.
- **Segment × rep_type is a degenerate cross in this data**, and saying so avoids implying more resolution than exists: ISR maps 1:1 to Commercial and AE 1:1 to Enterprise (`generators/reps.py` assigns segment by role), so grouping by (segment, rep_type) yields the same partition as rep_type alone. SMB has no rep at all. The module keys on both because that is the grain the question is asked at, not because they vary independently.

### The achievability diagnostic — a three-way gap decomposition, and two comparison bases

For each team-quarter (segment × rep_type) and company-wide, `assess_quota_achievability()` sums the team's stated quota from its active reps' `dim_reps` rows and its ramp-adjusted expected capacity, and decomposes the gap into three **exactly additive** parts:

| Component | Formula | What it isolates |
|---|---|---|
| `productivity_gap` | `quota × (1 − baseline)` | The shortfall that would exist even with every rep fully ramped and on seat all quarter — i.e. the quota's own level vs. what a ramped rep produces |
| `coverage_gap` | `quota × baseline × (1 − coverage)` | Selling time lost to mid-quarter hires and departures |
| `ramp_mix_gap` | `quota × baseline × coverage × (1 − ramp_factor)` | **The ramp-mix effect specifically** — the capacity two same-headcount teams differ by purely through who is ramping |

Two comparison bases are reported, and the difference between them is load-bearing:

- **`quota_coverage_ratio` = expected capacity ÷ stated quota** — the literal answer to "is the quota mathematically achievable." Flagged **`comparable`**. Quota and capacity are denominated in the same units because `generators/reps.py`'s `_QUOTA_BASE_RANGE` is back-solved from the opportunity generator's own realized deal supply — opportunities closed per ramped rep-quarter × realized win rate × average won deal size — so this ratio reads as capacity, not as a gap between two independently-parameterised generators. **Observed coverage runs 0.565–0.969 across the 48 evaluated team-quarters (median 0.771), and 34 of 48 exceed the 15% margin** at a quota-over-capacity gap of 3.2–77.1%. The residual gap is a real capacity reading with two named sources: the ramp haircut, and — for the 2020–2022 team-quarters — an opportunity supply that had not yet reached steady state. From 2023 onward the AE team clears the margin in **every one of its 12 team-quarters** (coverage 0.885–0.969) while ISR clears none (coverage 0.736–0.802), a difference between two teams the diagnostic can state cleanly because quota and capacity share the same grounding — see the quota-grounding bullet above.
- **`ramp_attributable_gap_pct` = `ramp_mix_gap` ÷ expected capacity** — the level-immune basis, and the one that carries the artifact's actual signal. A constant offset in the baseline cancels out of this ratio entirely, in the same way the variance engine's trailing-baseline signal is immune to its caveated plan-level offsets. This is what distinguishes a ramp-mix problem from a quota-level problem.

**Achievability margin: 15% — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed.** A team-quarter is flagged when stated quota exceeds ramp-adjusted expected capacity by more than 15% of that capacity. Nothing upstream (build spec, QA plan, metric tree) states a number for this, so it is this artifact's own proposal at build time, carrying the same PROPOSED status as the variance engine's ±8% threshold and the segment-migration analysis's 10% firmographic-rescore floor. **Grounding, corrected**: the margin gates `ramp_attributable_gap_pct` (`ramp_mix_gap ÷ expected_capacity`), and the empirical ramped-attainment baseline — the one estimated quantity in this artifact — cancels out of that ratio exactly (a constant per-team-quarter factor in both numerator and denominator), confirmed by recomputing the ratio with the baseline term algebraically removed and matching the module's own column to 5.5e-17. So the baseline's estimation precision is not what this margin is actually protecting against, and citing its relative standard error as the grounding was wrong. The real grounding is **selectivity and robustness**: 15% flags 13 of 48 team-quarters — neither near-zero nor near-universal — and that result is stable across a wide band of the one input the ratio does depend on, the ramp-capacity discount itself (breach count moves only from 14 to 10 across discount values 0.40–0.60, and the 13-of-48 result holds exactly across the discount's own reconciled range of 0.488–0.500). A margin sitting on a flat part of that selectivity curve, rather than a knife-edge, is a defensible floor even without a precision argument behind it.

**Result at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31** (48 team-quarters with genuine opportunity supply):

| Basis | Flagged | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| `quota_coverage_ratio` (absolute, `comparable`) | **34 of 48** | Real capacity reading: ramp haircut plus the 2020–2022 era's pre-steady-state opportunity supply. Splits cleanly by team from 2023 on — AE 0 of 12, ISR 12 of 12 |
| `ramp_attributable_gap_pct` (level-immune) | **13 of 48** | The ramp-mix signal specifically, and genuinely selective. Unchanged by the quota recalibration, as the level-immunity claim predicts |

The 13 breaches are all concentrated in the 2020-Q1 → 2022-Q3 build-out era, when ramping reps carried **27–64% of team quota** (max ramp-attributable gap **44.5%**, at 2021-Q2 Enterprise: 6 AEs of whom 4 were ramping, so the team held only 69% of the capacity its headcount implied). **Not one quarter from 2023 onward breaches** — by then each team carries 0–3 ramping reps out of 14–21 and the ramp-attributable gap falls to 0–7.19%, still comfortably under the 15% margin in every one of those 24 team-quarters. That contrast is the artifact working: same diagnostic, same threshold, firing hard on a ramp-heavy roster and staying quiet on a mature one. Median ramp-attributable gap across all 48 team-quarters is 4.4%.

### Point-in-time design

Every function takes `as_of_date` and it is honoured on all three data surfaces. `dim_reps` periods are filtered to `period_start_date <= as_of_date` (a period beginning later is future information — a future quota raise or a departure not yet known). Opportunity data is filtered to `close_date <= as_of_date`. Only **complete** quarters are admitted to the panel (`quarter_end <= as_of_date`): a partial quarter's realized ARR measured against a whole quarter's stated quota is a unit mismatch in disguise, the same reason `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py` evaluates the last complete month rather than a part-month. **No stochastic step exists in this module** — no train/test split, no sampling, no simulation — so no random seed applies, matching `analytics/segment_migration.py`'s and `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s precedent.

**Data-window guard.** `generators/reps.py` back-dates ~65% of hires to before the account generator's earliest possible account, so the earliest rep-quarters carry a real stated quota against a market that does not yet exist in the data. `load_opportunity_supply_window()` derives the first quarter containing any ISR/AE new-business opportunity (2020-Q1) from `fact_opportunities` itself rather than hardcoding a generator constant, and every achievability row carries `has_opportunity_supply`. **4 company-quarters (all of 2019) are flagged False** — their attainment is structurally zero and says nothing about capacity. They are reported with the flag rather than dropped silently, the same treatment the variance engine's `data_window` block gives its end-of-window truncation.

### Validation — structural tie-outs and a non-vacuousness check, not statistics

In place of an accuracy/error target (none applies — see the shape decision), five checks in `run_build_time_validation()`. Three are exact structural invariants in the spirit of the segment-migration analysis's growth-bridge tie-out (the decomposition tie-out runs in both baseline modes, since only `prior_quarters_only` exercises the not-yet-estimable `NaN` path); two confirm the ramp mechanism this model is built on is real in the data rather than an assumption the model quietly assumes into existence. **All 5 pass at both checkpoints** (`as_of_date` 2025-12-31 and 2025-06-30).

| Check | What it pins down | Floor / tolerance | Result (2025-12-31) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `attainment_reconciles_to_fact_opportunities` | The rep-quarter panel's `won_arr` sums to exactly the closed-won new-business ARR `fact_opportunities` reports for ISR/AE-owned deals in the same complete quarters — no dollar dropped (a win with no matching `dim_reps` period) and none double-counted (a rep with several capacity periods inside one quarter fanning out the join, a real risk given `dim_reps`' period grain) | ≤ $0.01 max abs diff, both in total and row-by-row | **$0.00**, 0 unmatched rep-quarters, $37,212,986.21 / 478 deals both sides |
| `capacity_gap_decomposition_is_exact` | `productivity_gap + coverage_gap + ramp_mix_gap == stated_quota − expected_capacity` for every team-quarter. Exact arithmetic by construction, so anything beyond floating-point noise is a real bug | ≤ $0.01 max abs diff | **$5.2e-10** (floating-point noise only), 84 rows checked (56 team-quarters + 28 company-wide) |
| `capacity_gap_decomposition_is_exact_prior_quarters_only` | The same identity under the expanding-window baseline, the only mode in which a rep's `baseline_attainment` can be `NaN` (fewer than 8 prior ramped rep-quarters). That `NaN` must poison its team-quarter's aggregate rather than be dropped by pandas' default `skipna=True`, which would fabricate a zero-capacity rep | ≤ $0.01 max abs diff | **$0.00**, with not-yet-computable rows counted separately rather than misreported as mismatches |
| `ramp_mechanism_is_real_not_vacuous` | **The check that makes the 0.50 discount more than an assertion.** Ramping reps must genuinely produce less per capacity-day than ramped reps in the real generated data, for every team and pooled — otherwise the whole capacity model is applying a haircut to an effect that does not exist. Mirrors QA plan Test C ("ramping reps show measurably lower win rate than ramped reps, but not zero") | ratio strictly > 0 **and** ≤ 0.80. Deliberately loose: it asks only for a ≥20% observed penalty, far short of the 50% the rule assumes, so it fails on a vacuous mechanism without covertly re-testing the discount itself | **Pass** — AE 0.529, ISR 0.280, pooled 0.488 |
| `ramp_discount_reconciles_to_observed` | The applied 0.50 discount is consistent with what the data actually produced, rather than a third number invented alongside the build spec's ~50% and the generator's 0.55 | pooled empirical ratio within **±0.10** of 0.50. ±0.10 spans both the build spec's assumption and the generator's factor, so the check confirms consistency without claiming more precision than ~105 ramping rep-quarters supports | **Pass** — 0.488 observed (abs diff 0.012); 0.515 at the 2025-06-30 checkpoint |

**Second checkpoint, `as_of_date` 2025-06-30**: 44 team-quarters evaluated, same 13 ramp-mix breaches, 5 of 5 checks pass, pooled ramp ratio 0.515. Quota coverage runs 0.540–0.918 (median 0.736) with 37 of 44 team-quarters over the margin, and company-wide attainment is 79.3% across all quarters / 89.5% over the fully-supplied window — the same shape as the later checkpoint, read four quarters earlier off a baseline that has seen less history. Confirming the artifact is not tuned to one period — the same treatment the Weekly executive readout entry's second checkpoint gives.

**Sample sizes** (2025-12-31): 673 rep-quarters total across 44 quota-bearing reps — 568 ramped (AE 268, ISR 300) and 105 ramping (AE 57, ISR 48); 28 quarters spanned, giving 56 team-quarters of which 48 have genuine opportunity supply; 478 closed-won new-business deals totalling $37.2M ARR.

### Target and drift threshold

- **Target**: no accuracy/error target applies (structural/descriptive). The five checks above stand in its place, and the three exact tie-outs are stated as **hard invariants with a floating-point tolerance rather than a band** — the same reasoning the Weekly executive readout entry gives for stating exact equality: deterministic arithmetic has no sampling variance, and a tolerance would imply an error source that does not exist.
- **Drift threshold**: per the doc's general rule, 2 consecutive monthly checkpoints failing any of the five checks → flagged for review. In practice the three tie-outs are expected never to breach (structural invariants — a breach is a defect to fix, not a trend to watch). Two are genuinely worth watching in `fact_model_performance_history`. **`empirical_ramp_ratio_pooled`**: if it drifted outside 0.40–0.60, the 0.50 discount would no longer describe the data and the capacity model would need re-grounding rather than re-running. **`ramped_baseline_attainment_pooled`**: now that quota is back-solved from deal supply, this figure should sit near 1.0 (0.9245 at 2025-12-31); drifting outside 0.70–1.30 means quota and the supply that fills it have decoupled again and `_QUOTA_BASE_RANGE` needs re-deriving at the generator, not compensating for downstream. Both are natural `drift-monitor` hooks for this artifact.
- **Persistence**: scalar time-series metrics logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `log_performance()` — `structural_checks_total`/`_passed`, `ramp_capacity_discount_applied`, `achievability_margin`, `empirical_ramp_ratio_pooled`/`_ae`/`_isr`, `ramped_baseline_attainment_pooled`/`_ae`/`_isr`, `ramped_rep_quarters_pooled`, `ramping_rep_quarters_pooled`, `team_quarters_evaluated`, `team_quarters_quota_not_achievable`, `team_quarters_ramp_mix_breaches_margin`, `max_ramp_attributable_gap_pct`, `company_quota_coverage_ratio_latest_quarter`, `company_ramp_capacity_haircut_latest_quarter`, `company_attainment_latest_quarter`, and both reconciliation max-abs-diffs. The variable-width tables (attainment by team-quarter, achievability detail, the ramp-effect breakdown) do not fit that log's flat scalar grain, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' Persistence note, and are the structured detail recorded in this entry instead.

### Known limitations

- **The absolute achievability verdict now rests on the 2023+ window's deal supply being representative of the whole panel.** `_QUOTA_BASE_RANGE` is derived from the fully-supplied window, but the same quota level is applied to the 2020–2022 build-out era, whose opportunity supply had not reached steady state. That is why 22 of 24 pre-2023 team-quarters exceed the margin against 12 of 24 from 2023 on. The residual is a real capacity reading, not a units mismatch, but it is a reading of a data window as much as of a roster. The two ratios are not independent by construction either — quota is calibrated so a ramped rep lands near 100%, so `quota_coverage_ratio` will always sit near `baseline × coverage × ramp_factor`. That is the intended design (a quota *should* be set at capacity), not a hidden circularity, but it means the absolute basis answers "how far is this team from a correctly-set quota," not "is the quota level itself right" — the level is right by derivation. `ramp_attributable_gap_pct` remains the basis that carries information the calibration does not put there.
- **This artifact does not read rep cost.** The tree's "Rep fully-loaded cost, incl. ramp" leaf is computed in `mart_efficiency` (`rep_fully_loaded_cost`, with `rep_ramp_cost` isolating the ramp window) from `fact_rep_monthly_cost`, not here; this artifact contributes the ramp-capacity half (how much production a ramping rep gives up), and the two are not joined into a cost-per-unit-of-capacity figure.
- **ISR's empirical ramp ratio (0.280) sits well below the AE figure (0.529) and below the 0.50 rule.** With 46–48 ramping ISR rep-quarters against a low-count, lumpy outcome, the module applies the pooled-reconciled 0.50 to both teams rather than fitting a per-team discount off that sample. A per-team discount becomes defensible if more ramping ISR history accumulates; there is no basis for it on this snapshot.
- **No forward-looking capacity projection.** This artifact assesses quarters that have closed. Projecting next quarter's capacity from a planned hiring ramp is a natural extension the `prior_quarters_only` baseline mode already provides the seam for, but it is not built here — it would need a hiring plan, which is not in any source.
- **No mart exposes the rep-quarter panel.** Every consumer that wants it currently has to re-derive it through this module. `mart_rep_capacity` is the Phase 2 change worth making before a second consumer appears.

## Pipeline coverage

- **Built**: `analytics/pipeline_coverage.py`. Grain: one coverage reading per segment (Commercial, Enterprise) for the quarter containing an evaluation date, plus a next-quarter block per segment. Source marts: `fact_opportunities` (open and closed ISR/AE-owned new-business deals) and `dim_reps` (quota-bearing capacity periods, read through `capacity_planning.load_rep_capacity_periods` so the two artifacts read quota identically), reconciled against `analytics/forecast.py`'s bottoms-up manager lens and `analytics/capacity_planning.py`'s rep-quarter panel. No dbt model was added or changed for this artifact and no Phase 1 generator was touched.
- **What it is**: the Wave 10 "Pipeline coverage artifact — by segment, against realized conversion, gap to quota in dollars" of the post-Wave-8 improvement plan: the CRO's recurring "is there enough pipeline to make the number" question. **It is not one of the build spec's 22 Section 8 artifacts**; it is a deliberate scope addition made after that order closed, and the portfolio carries 22 `analytics/*.py` artifacts plus the executive summary step (the 22nd is the LTV model, below). It reads as a **coverage reading, not a forecast**: `analytics/forecast.py` remains the only owner of "what will close", this artifact never modifies or competes with its lenses, and the two are reconciled and reported side by side, never merged (every output field is named so it cannot be mistaken for a forecast, e.g. `conversion_implied_expected_close_usd`).
- **The metric tree defines no coverage metric**, and this artifact redefines none of the tree's. The definitions below are its own and are all **PROPOSED, not yet confirmed**.
- **Shape decision — structural/logic artifact, not a fitted model.** Every figure is a sum, ratio or difference of mart rows; the one estimated quantity is the realized conversion, a pooled dollar-weighted rate. Per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' **"Structural/logic artifacts"** category there is no coefficient table, no AUC, no confusion matrix and no R², and their absence is deliberate, not pending.
- **Model type selection and rationale (why not a fitted model)**: a pipeline-to-bookings regression (quarter bookings ~ open pipeline × stage mix) was the credible alternative and was rejected on three grounds. (1) It would be fitted on about 11 quarterly points per segment. (2) **Stage-conditional win rates are flat in this data**: every new-business deal logs every stage, won or lost (`generators/opportunities.py`), so stage carries no information about the outcome and a stage-weighted pipeline would add parameters and no signal. The one point-in-time conditioner that does move a win rate is Enterprise POC outcome (pass wins about 42%, fail about 8% of closed dollars in the trailing window), shown as a clearly labelled indicative view rather than folded into the coverage arithmetic.
