(3) The quantity a RevOps owner needs is the coverage identity itself, which a fitted estimate would only blur. A per-deal win-probability model exists and is the forecast artifact.

### Definitions — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Population | `opportunity_type = 'new_business'`, owned by an ISR (Commercial) or an AE (Enterprise). SMB has no rep, no pipeline and no quota. Renewals and expansions are excluded because quota does not cover them (see "The units trap" below) |
| Open at `t` | `created_date <= t < close_date`, the definition `forecast.open_period_population`, `deal_diagnostics` and `territory_coverage` already use |
| `open_pipeline` | Open deals whose CRM `close_date` falls in the evaluation date's quarter. `close_date` stands in for an expected-close field Phase 1 does not generate, exactly as the forecast scopes its own quarter; deals open but closing in later quarters are reported separately and not counted |
| `quota` | Sum of stated quota for the quarter over every ISR (Commercial) or AE (Enterprise) with at least one active day in [quarter start, `t`]. Quota is per rep per quarter, constant within a quarter (checked over 475 rep-quarters), never pro-rated, and joined 1:1 ISR↔Commercial, AE↔Enterprise (checked on all 1,669 new-business deals) |
| `won_to_date` | New-business closed-won `amount` with `close_date` in [quarter start, `t`] |
| `remaining_quota` | `max(0, quota − won_to_date)` |
| `realized_conversion` | Dollar-weighted new-business win rate (won amount ÷ closed amount) of deals closed in the trailing 365 days, never reaching before 2023-01-01, by segment; unavailable below 30 closed deals in the window |
| `required_pipeline_multiple` | `1 ÷ realized_conversion` |
| `pipeline_coverage_ratio` | `open_pipeline ÷ remaining_quota` (named so it cannot be confused with `capacity_planning`'s `quota_coverage_ratio`, which is expected capacity ÷ stated quota, a supply-side quantity) |
| `coverage_vs_required` | `pipeline_coverage_ratio ÷ required_pipeline_multiple` = `open_pipeline × realized_conversion ÷ remaining_quota` |
| `conversion_implied_gap` | `remaining_quota − open_pipeline × realized_conversion`, dollars; positive = shortfall |
| `coverage_status` | `covered` if `coverage_vs_required >= 1.0`; `thin` if `0.75 <= coverage_vs_required < 1.0`; `shortfall` below 0.75; `quota_met` when `remaining_quota` is 0 (the coverage ratio is then undefined, reported as null, not zero or infinite). The bands are **PROPOSED, not yet confirmed**: 1.0 is the break-even by the definition itself, and 0.75 is where a quarter of the required level is inside what pipeline still to be created can plausibly add for Commercial (see "Findings") |

**Why the conversion window starts in 2023.** No lost new-business deal is logged before 2023 (the first closes 2023-01-03), so every one of the 91 ISR/AE new-business deals closed before 2023-01-01 is won: any win rate measured there is a structural 100%, not a conversion. This is a validation check (`pre_2023_closed_deals_are_all_won`), not an assumption. It is also the start of the window in which quota is a steady-state target (Capacity planning's "fully supplied" window), so readings are evaluated **from 2023 onward**; an earlier evaluation date returns `unavailable` with that reason.

**Why trailing 365 days, not an expanding window.** An expanding window from 2023-01 anchors the rate on a period (2023: Commercial 18.6%, Enterprise 14.1% dollar-weighted) that is not representative of the quarter being read; the trailing window follows the rate the team is realizing now. The choice was made on that reasoning, not by comparing error: the sensitivity (same backtest, expanding window) is reported in "Backtest" and does not change any conclusion.

### The units trap, and the reconciliation to the forecast artifact

`forecast.open_pipeline_amount` mixes new business, renewal and expansion opportunities (quota covers ISR/AE new business only). At 2025-11-14 the forecast's all-type open pipeline is Commercial $2.06M across 113 deals and Enterprise $9.19M across 33; restricted to new business it is $725.9K / 34 and $5.42M / 20. Using the all-type figure as a coverage numerator would overstate Commercial coverage by about 2.8x and Enterprise by about 1.7x, so every coverage figure is restricted to new business and the difference is carried in the reconciliation block.

`reconcile_to_forecast()` filters the forecast's manager-lens deal frame (`bottoms_up_rollup(...)["deals"]`) to new business at the same evaluation date and reports, per segment: the two open pipelines and deal counts (**they tie exactly: $0.00, same deal counts, at both checkpoints and at all 21 backtest rows**), the coverage reading's conversion-implied expected close, and the forecast manager lens's weighted value for the same deals. The pricing differs by design (one realized conversion per segment versus a per-deal category weight), and the difference is reported, not corrected: at 2025-11-14 Commercial $184.9K vs the forecast's $163.5K (+13.1%), Enterprise $1.27M vs $1.12M (+13.4%); at 2025-08-15 Commercial $257.7K vs $197.2K (+30.7%), Enterprise $613.5K vs $590.5K (+3.9%). Across the 21 backtest rows the ratio of the coverage reading's open-pipeline expectation to the forecast manager lens has median 1.12, range 0.97 to 1.45.

### Inputs and point-in-time design

| Source mart | Columns used | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `fact_opportunities` | `opportunity_id`, `segment`, `opportunity_type`, `owner_role`, `rep_id`, `is_won`, `amount`, `poc_outcome`, `created_date`, `close_date` | Open and closed new-business deals; the conversion window; won to date |
| `dim_reps` | `rep_id`, `rep_type`, `segment`, `hire_date`, `period_start_date`, `period_end_date`, `quota_amount`, `rep_status` | Stated quota and active status per capacity period; ramp status by `capacity_planning`'s rule |

Every function takes `as_of_date` (the evaluation date). Deals are filtered to `created_date <= as_of_date` and **a deal not closed by it carries no outcome**: `known_at()` masks `is_won` and `poc_outcome` to missing for every open deal, so a later outcome cannot reach a figure even by accident; `close_date` is kept for open deals and used only to scope a deal to a quarter. Quota periods are filtered to `period_start_date <= as_of_date`. `validation_no_lookahead` proves the point end to end: on an in-memory copy of the two source tables in which every outcome after the evaluation date is flipped, every deal created after it dropped and every quota period starting after it altered, the reading is identical to the reading on the untouched marts (checked at the checkpoint and at one earlier mid-quarter date, 2024-05-10).

**Evaluation point.** Commercial cycles are 14–45 days, so most Commercial pipeline is created and closed inside a quarter; a fixed relative point makes quarters comparable. The canonical point is `forecast._backtest_eval_date`'s mid-quarter Friday (the last Friday on or before the quarter's midpoint); `as_of_date` is any date, and the quarter is the calendar quarter containing it.

**How a rep hired or departed mid-quarter is treated.** Quota is the full stated quota of every rep with at least one active day between the quarter start and the evaluation date — `capacity_planning`'s rule, under which stated quota is never pro-rated (the generator does not reduce it). A rep hired **after** the evaluation date is not yet knowable and is not counted; a rep who departed before it keeps their stated quota in the sum (reported as `quota_reps_departed_in_quarter`), as in the capacity panel. At each quarter's last day this rule reproduces `capacity_planning`'s stated quota and rep count exactly (checked on all 22 segment-quarters from 2023-Q1). Quota counts ramping reps at full stated quota (Commercial: 1 of 18 ISRs ramping, 4.6% of quota, at 2025-11-14; Enterprise: 0 of 21 AEs); the ramp haircut is `capacity_planning`'s concern, reported here as `quota_reps_ramping` and `ramping_quota_share` so the reader sees how much of the quota sits with unramped reps.

**Next quarter and the data window.** The next-quarter block counts open deals whose `close_date` falls in the following quarter against a **carried-forward** quota (the current quarter's stated quota of the reps active on the evaluation date, since next quarter's quota is not set yet) and carries **no status verdict** (`not_assessed`): at mid-quarter Commercial next-quarter pipeline is largely not created yet, so a low reading there is structural. The data ends 2025-12-28 (the last opportunity close; submissions end 2025-12-26), so at 2025-11-14 next-quarter coverage is structurally unavailable and is reported `unavailable` with `reason_code = beyond_data_window`, **never as zero coverage**; an evaluation date on or after 2025-12-28 returns `unavailable` (`after_data_window`) for the current quarter too, since no deal can be open. Inside 2025-Q4 every open deal scopes into the current quarter by construction (no deal closes beyond the window).

### Output contract

`run_pipeline_coverage(as_of_date)` returns a JSON-safe dict the dashboard renders verbatim: `label` ("coverage reading, not a forecast"), `as_of_date`, `period`, `quarter_start`/`quarter_end`, `days_to_quarter_end`, `segments` (one per segment: `status` `present`/`unavailable` with `reason_code`/`reason`, `quota_usd`, `quota_reps`, `quota_reps_ramping`, `won_to_date_usd`, `attainment_to_date`, `remaining_quota_usd`, `open_pipeline_usd`/`_deals`, `open_pipeline_after_quarter_usd`/`_deals`, `pipeline_coverage_ratio`, `realized_conversion` and its `conversion_closed_deals`/`conversion_won_deals`/window, `required_pipeline_multiple`, `coverage_vs_required`, `conversion_implied_expected_close_usd`, `conversion_implied_gap_usd`, `coverage_status`, `poc_view` for Enterprise, `caveats` and a `display` block of plain-language strings including a one-sentence `summary`), `next_quarter`, `reconciliation`, `status_rule`, `definitions`, `caveats` and `data_window`. A segment whose conversion window holds fewer than 30 closed deals, with no quota, or past the data window is `unavailable` with its reason and null judgment fields rather than a zero.

### Findings — two checkpoints (mid-quarter Fridays)

| | 2025-08-15, Commercial | 2025-08-15, Enterprise | 2025-11-14, Commercial | 2025-11-14, Enterprise |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Quota (reps) | $683.3K (19) | $2.27M (21) | $648.5K (18) | $2.30M (21) |
| Won to date | $361.3K (52.9%) | $1.60M (70.8%) | $281.9K (43.5%) | $2.67M (115.8%) |
| Remaining quota | $322.0K | $661.0K | $366.6K | $0 (quota met) |
| Open pipeline (this quarter) | $910.3K / 34 deals | $2.96M / 13 | $725.9K / 34 | $5.42M / 20 |
| Open, closing after quarter end | $0 | $8.13M / 31 | $0 | $0 (window end) |
| Coverage ratio | 2.83x | 4.47x | 1.98x | n/a (quota met) |
| Realized conversion (closed deals in window) | 28.3% (411) | 20.7% (147) | 25.5% (442) | 23.5% (137) |
| Required multiple | 3.53x | 4.82x | 3.93x | 4.26x |
| Coverage vs required | 0.80x | 0.93x | 0.50x | n/a |
| Conversion-implied gap | $64.3K short | $47.6K short | $181.7K short | $1.27M ahead |
| Status (proposed rule) | thin | thin | shortfall | quota met |

Realized required multiples run 3.5x–4.8x across the two checkpoints, because the trailing window follows each segment's recent rate. The Enterprise POC-conditioned view (indicative; not backtested) prices open deals whose POC has concluded (forecast's reveal gate) at the closed-deal pass or fail win rate of the same window and the rest at the overall rate: at 2025-11-14, pass 42.1% of closed dollars (59 closed deals, 26 won), fail 9.5% (78 closed, 7 won); expected close $1.16M against $1.27M unconditioned; at 2025-08-15, pass 41.9% (60 / 25), fail 6.5% (87 / 5); $552.0K against $613.5K. The fail class's win count is thin, so its rate carries wide uncertainty.

### Validation — structural and reconciliation checks, a backtest, and a pre-registered relative target

`run_build_time_validation()` runs 12 structural checks, 1 target check and 1 reported comparison (`[INFO]`, gating nothing); **all 12 structural checks pass at both checkpoints** (`[FAIL]` is printed only for a structural failure; an unmet target prints `[MISS]`).

| Check | What it pins down | Tolerance | Result (2025-11-14) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `open_pipeline_ties_to_independent_sql` | The open pipeline computed from frames equals a direct SQL aggregate over `fact_opportunities`, per segment, at every mid-quarter evaluation date from 2023-Q1 and the checkpoint | $0.01; counts exact | **$0.00**, 24 segment-dates, 0 count mismatches |
| `quota_ties_to_capacity_planning_panel` | At each quarter's last day, the quota rule equals `capacity_planning`'s stated quota and rep count per segment-quarter | $0.01; counts exact | **$0.00**, 22 segment-quarters, 0 rep-count mismatches |
| `won_to_date_ties_to_capacity_planning_wins` | At each quarter's last day, won to date equals the panel's won ARR | $0.01 | **$0.00**, 22 segment-quarters |
| `quota_constant_within_quarter` | The one-value-per-rep-per-quarter quota rule only holds if quota never varies inside a quarter | exact | 475 rep-quarters, 0 varying |
| `segment_owner_role_one_to_one` | ISR owns only Commercial, AE only Enterprise | exact | Commercial/ISR 1,217; Enterprise/AE 452 |
| `pre_2023_closed_deals_are_all_won` | Why the conversion window starts in 2023 | exact | 91 of 91 closed deals before 2023 are won |
| `coverage_definitions_hold_as_identities` | remaining quota, required multiple × conversion = 1, coverage vs required = coverage × conversion, gap = remaining − open × conversion, and status agreeing with the gap's sign, over the live readings and every backtest row | 1e-6 ratios; $0.05 dollars | 24 readings, 0 violations |
| `conversion_window_never_reaches_before_2023` | No conversion window starts before 2023-01-01 | exact | earliest start 2023-01-01 |
| `open_pipeline_ties_to_forecast_new_business_rollup` | The open new-business pipeline equals the forecast manager-lens frame filtered to new business | $0.01; counts exact | **$0.00** live and across 21 backtest rows |
| `no_lookahead_reading_unchanged_by_post_<date>_data` (×2) | The perturbed-copy test above, at the checkpoint and at 2024-05-10 | exact | identical |
| `synthetic_known_answer_scenarios` | 14 scenarios with hand-computed answers through the same compute functions: covered / thin / shortfall at 1.2x / 0.9x / 0.5x of required, zero remaining quota (`quota_met`, coverage undefined), no open deals (coverage a real 0.0), no quota (`unavailable`, not a divide by zero), no wins yet, a deal created after the evaluation date not counted, a deal closed before it not open, the quarter boundary (a win on the first day counts, on the previous quarter's last day does not), a deal closing after the quarter end not counted, too few closed deals (`unavailable`), quota already won with no realized conversion (still `quota_met`), pre-2023 deals excluded from conversion | exact | 14 of 14 |

**Sample sizes (2025-11-14).** Conversion windows: 442 Commercial closed deals (130 won) and 137 Enterprise (33 won); 22 segment-quarters in the backtest, 21 evaluated (Enterprise 2023-Q1 has 15 closed deals in its window, below the 30-deal floor, and is reported unavailable). At 2025-08-15: 20 segment-quarters, 19 evaluated.

### Backtest of the coverage reading

For every complete quarter from 2023-Q1, the reading is taken at that quarter's mid-quarter Friday using only what was known then, and the coverage-implied expected bookings (`won_to_date + open_pipeline × realized_conversion`) are compared with the actual quarter-end new-business bookings. This is the artifact's validation target. Result at 2025-11-14 (n = 21 evaluated segment-quarters):

| | n | MAPE | Median APE | P90 APE | Max APE | Aggregate bias |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pooled | 21 | **29.6%** | 15.5% | 53.6% | 185.7% | −4.0% |
| Commercial | 11 | 19.9% | 15.2% | 43.6% | 53.6% | **−15.5%** |
| Enterprise | 10 | 40.3% | 17.5% | 85.9% | 185.7% | −0.1% |

Against the comparators (MAPE over the same 21 rows; the first two are the target's naive baselines, the last two were added so the target is not read as stronger than it is):

| | Coverage-implied (module) | Won to date alone | Prior-4-quarter mean | Constant rate: won + 0.25 × open | Pace: won ÷ fraction of quarter elapsed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pooled | 29.6% | 47.7% | 50.5% | **26.1%** | 32.8% |
| Commercial | 19.9% | 49.7% | 29.7% | **15.0%** | 26.4% |
| Enterprise | 40.3% | 45.6% | 73.5% | 38.3% | **39.8%** |

The constant rate is a fixed comparator stated in advance and not fitted to these quarters: 0.25 is the midpoint of the QA plan's benchmark new-business win-rate range for Enterprise (20–30%) and the floor of its Commercial range (25–35%). The pace comparator extrapolates won to date by the fraction of the quarter elapsed at the evaluation date (inclusive of that day). **The module does not beat the constant-rate comparator (pooled 29.6% against 26.1%; Commercial 19.9% against 15.0%; Enterprise 40.3% against 38.3%), and its Enterprise margin over the pace comparator is negative (40.3% against 39.8%)**; an independent recomputation with a slightly different pace-fraction convention found the same ordering (pooled 33.5%, Commercial 27.1%, Enterprise 40.5%). What the module's realized conversion adds over a constant rate is not accuracy on this backtest; see "Target" below for what it does add.

The pooled MAPE ratio against the better of the two target baselines is 0.621 (0.651 at 2025-08-15). With an expanding conversion window from 2023-01 instead of the trailing 365 days the pooled MAPE is 27.1% (Commercial 20.6%, Enterprise 34.2%), so the window choice does not change any conclusion.

**Scoping scenarios — does the eventual close date flatter the reading?** The reading counts an open deal toward the quarter by its eventual CRM `close_date`. Two alternative scopings computed by the module (`mape_hindsight_free_scoping`, `mape_no_scoping`) bound that effect on the same 21 rows. *Hindsight-free scoping* replaces the eventual `close_date` with an expected close of `created_date` plus the trailing median created-to-close cycle of deals already closed in the conversion window (mean over quarters: 28.3 days Commercial, 119.3 days Enterprise): pooled MAPE **27.9%** (Commercial 19.9%, Enterprise 36.7%), no worse than the module's 29.6% / 19.9% / 40.3%. *No scoping* counts every deal open at the evaluation date whatever its eventual close: pooled MAPE **70.1%** (Commercial 19.9%, because every open Commercial deal closes inside the quarter at that point; Enterprise 125.4%). So the eventual close date does select which open deals count toward the quarter, and without any scoping Enterprise would be badly over-read, but a scoping that uses no hindsight loses nothing here. (At 2025-08-15: hindsight-free 29.4% pooled against 31.5%; no scoping 72.3%.)

Three honest readings of that distribution. (1) **Commercial is systematically under-read by about 15%, and the cause is structural, not conversion.** 23.4% of Commercial quarter-end bookings (by quarter: 11% to 48%) come from deals created after the mid-quarter evaluation date — Commercial cycles are 14–45 days, so those deals did not exist to be counted — against 0.0% for Enterprise. The late-created share alone would imply about −23%; the net aggregate bias is −15.5% because the realized conversion over-prices the Commercial pipeline that is visible: over the backtest the module's expected bookings from visible deals (won to date + open × conversion) run **+10.4%** above what the visible deals actually delivered (won to date + wins from deals open at the evaluation date), and open × conversion alone is $1.92M against $1.42M won from those open deals (+34.8%). The two errors partly offset (the over-pricing is +8.0% of actual bookings, the late creation −23.4%). The reading cannot see pipeline that has not been created yet; it is a floor on Commercial bookings, not an estimate of them, and this is why a Commercial `shortfall` at mid-quarter is less alarming than the same label on Enterprise. (2) **Enterprise is unbiased in aggregate but lumpy:** median APE 17.5%, mean APE 40.3%, driven by a few large misses (2025-Q1: $1.50M expected from $3.84M open against $525K booked, +185.7%; 2023-Q2 +74.8%; 2023-Q3 −50.6%) — a handful of large deals, not a model error. (3) Every quarter's open pipeline ties to the forecast's new-business rollup exactly, so the dispersion is entirely in pricing the open dollars at one rate.

**Coverage versus attainment — a relationship exists, and it is partly mechanical.** Spearman rank correlations over the backtest (seeded permutation p-values, 10,000 shuffles, seed 42; n is about two dozen segment-quarters and quarters are not independent, so no confidence interval is claimed): `coverage_vs_required` against final quarter attainment, pooled n = 18, ρ = 0.61 (p = 0.009; Commercial n = 9, ρ = 0.77, p = 0.021; Enterprise n = 9, ρ = 0.67, p = 0.057); `pipeline_coverage_ratio` against final attainment ρ = 0.55 (p = 0.021). **Won-to-date attainment alone predicts final attainment better than any coverage measure (ρ = 0.80, n = 22)**, because won to date is part of both sides of the comparison; the coverage ratio's own contribution is the rest of the quarter: open pipeline × conversion against the bookings that arrived after the evaluation date, n = 21, ρ = 0.63 (p = 0.003) (the clean figure, since neither side contains won to date). `coverage_vs_required` against rest-of-quarter bookings as a share of remaining quota, n = 18, ρ = 0.60 (p = 0.009), is reported but is partly mechanical: both sides divide by remaining quota, so it shares a denominator with the coverage ratio and should not be read as independent confirmation. So coverage is informative about what is left to book, as designed, but it is not an independent oracle of attainment. Status outcomes (mid-quarter status against final attainment): `covered` n = 3, 2 reached full quota, median attainment 113.7%; `thin` n = 3, 1 reached, median 97.7%; `shortfall` n = 12, 0 reached, median 70.9%; `quota_met` n = 3, 3 reached, median 149.0%. The ordering is monotone, but 3-3-12-3 is too few to calibrate the status bands, which stay proposed.

### Target and drift threshold — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed

- **Target (PROPOSED, not yet confirmed).** No upstream document states an error bound for a coverage reading, so the target is relative: the coverage-implied expected bookings must beat **both** naive baselines — won to date alone, and the mean of the prior four quarters' bookings — on pooled MAPE and on each segment's MAPE, over the 2023-onward backtest. The form was chosen after a first look at the scale of the errors, and the achieved numbers are reported as they came out, not tuned. **Met at both checkpoints** (pooled 29.6% against 47.7% and 50.5%; Commercial 19.9% against 49.7% and 29.7%; Enterprise 40.3% against 45.6% and 73.5%).
  **This is a weak target and should be read as one.** Beating those two baselines shows that including open pipeline helps; it does not show that the realized conversion earns its keep. A fixed constant rate (won + 0.25 × open, stated in advance, not fitted) beats the module (pooled 26.1%, Commercial 15.0%, Enterprise 38.3%), and a pace comparator (won ÷ fraction of quarter elapsed) is level with it for Enterprise (39.8% against 40.3%). Both comparators are reported in the backtest, in the logged scalars and in the console output, and gate nothing (printed `[INFO]`). The module's value-add is therefore not predictive accuracy over a constant rate. It is (1) the coverage and gap framing: how many dollars of open pipeline have been needed per dollar booked, how many are there, and what the dollar gap is, per segment; (2) a per-segment required multiple that follows each segment's recent realized rate (3.5x–4.8x across the two checkpoints, where one constant would hide that Enterprise needs more pipeline per dollar than Commercial); (3) explicit unavailable states instead of a number where the evidence is missing; and (4) reconciliation to the forecast and to capacity planning. Structural components carry no accuracy target: the 12 checks are hard invariants with a tolerance, not a band.
- **Drift threshold (PROPOSED, not yet confirmed).** Per the general rule above, flag for review on **2 consecutive checkpoints** on which `backtest_mape_ratio_vs_naive_baseline` is above **1.0** (the coverage-implied bookings no longer beat the better of the two target baselines; achieved 0.621 and 0.651), or where any of the 12 structural checks fails (a defect to fix, not a trend to watch). **Its limits:** the rule is only as strong as the target it monitors, which a constant rate already beats, so a ratio below 1.0 means the reading still beats won-to-date and last-four-quarters, not that it is accurate; and with 2 checkpoints logged it has never been exercised. The constant-rate and pace MAPEs are logged (below) and worth watching alongside it. `backtest_aggregate_bias_commercial` is also worth watching (−0.155 now): a drift toward 0 would mean more Commercial pipeline is arriving before mid-quarter, i.e. a change in cycle length.
- **Persistence**: scalar metrics are logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `log_performance()` under `model_name = 'pipeline_coverage'` — per segment `quota_usd`, `won_to_date_usd`, `remaining_quota_usd`, `open_pipeline_usd`, `pipeline_coverage_ratio`, `realized_conversion`, `required_pipeline_multiple`, `coverage_vs_required`, `conversion_implied_gap_usd` (where defined), `conversion_implied_vs_forecast_manager_lens_pct_<segment>`; and `structural_checks_total`/`_passed`, `synthetic_scenarios_total`/`_passed`, `backtest_segment_quarters_evaluated`, `backtest_mape_coverage_implied_pooled`/`_commercial`/`_enterprise`, `backtest_aggregate_bias_commercial`/`_enterprise`, `backtest_mape_ratio_vs_naive_baseline`, `backtest_mape_constant_rate_025_<pooled|commercial|enterprise>`, `backtest_mape_pace_<…>`, `backtest_mape_hindsight_free_scoping_<…>`, `backtest_mape_no_scoping_<…>`, `rank_corr_coverage_vs_required_to_attainment`. The per-quarter backtest table and the scenarios are recorded in the dated report (`analytics/outputs/pipeline_coverage_<date>.json` and `.md`) rather than in the log's flat grain. Logging is an upsert: re-running a checkpoint leaves the log byte-identical.

### Known limitations

- **New business only.** Renewal and expansion pipeline, and the AM-owned book, are out of scope because quota does not cover them; SMB has no pipeline and no quota.
- **`close_date` is a stand-in for an expected-close date.** The deal's eventual `close_date` decides which open deals count toward the quarter. That is the forecast's convention and does not reveal a deal's outcome (a `close_date` says when it resolved, not how), but it is an information-set difference from live use, where only an expected-close field entered by a rep would be available, and the no-look-ahead perturbation test does not perturb it (it corrupts outcomes, later deals and later quota, not `close_date` of open deals). The mitigating, measured result: a hindsight-free scoping (`created_date` plus the trailing median cycle) gives pooled MAPE 27.9% (Commercial 19.9%, Enterprise 36.7%), no worse than the module's 29.6% / 19.9% / 40.3%, whereas no scoping at all would give 70.1% pooled (Enterprise 125.4%). Pipeline is only ever counted against the quarter in which it resolves.
- **Flat stage conversion.** No stage adjustment exists in this data (every deal logs every stage); the only conditioner is Enterprise POC outcome, shown as an indicative view and not backtested.
- **Thin Enterprise evidence.** 137 closed Enterprise deals (33 won) in the conversion window, a POC-fail class with 7 wins, and 10 evaluated Enterprise backtest quarters; its rate and its error are far noisier than Commercial's.
- **Commercial is read as a floor at mid-quarter** (23.4% of bookings arrive from pipeline created after it).
- **Data-window end.** Next-quarter coverage is structurally unavailable at 2025-11-14 and the current quarter is unavailable on or after 2025-12-28.
- **Quota is not a steady-state target before 2023**, and no conversion exists before it; readings start in 2023-Q1 (Commercial) and 2023-Q2 (Enterprise).
- **Ramp.** Stated quota is counted in full for ramping reps; `capacity_planning` owns the ramp haircut.
- **Next-quarter quota is carried forward**, not set; the next-quarter block is indicative and carries no verdict.
- **Only mid-quarter checkpoints are validated.** The weekly use the CRO makes of it (any date in the quarter) is computed by the same functions and passes the same point-in-time perturbation test, but no accuracy claim is made for a late-quarter reading, where few deals remain open.
- **The status bands and the target form are proposals**, supported by 22 segment-quarters. The status boundaries use a 1e-9 relative tolerance, so a reading exactly on 1.0x or 0.75x takes the upper band and the status always agrees with the sign of the conversion-implied gap.
- **The in-process call is total.** `run_pipeline_coverage(as_of_date)` returns a reading (per segment `present`, or `unavailable` with a reason code) for any date from 2020-01-01 to 2025-12-31 under both the root interpreter and `dashboard/.venv` (Python 3.12, pandas 3). A sweep of 150 dates (every month end, every mid-quarter Friday, every quarter start, end and day after, and the window-end dates) gives, by segment reading: `before_conversion_window` 135, `insufficient_closed_deals` 10, `after_data_window` 6, and present 149 (`shortfall` 88, `quota_met` 27, `thin` 17, `covered` 17), out of 300 segment readings. The coverage reading depends only on this module; only the reconciliation block and the Enterprise POC view use the forecast's point-in-time feature builder, and a failure there yields reconciliation `unavailable` (`forecast_side_unavailable`) rather than an exception. The forecast's point-in-time feature builder aligns the key dtype of each frame it joins as-of (submissions, stage history, usage trend) to the evaluation frame's before merging, so it runs on dates before the first forecast submission, where the submissions frame is empty, under pandas 3 as under pandas 2. In a 148-date sweep (every month end 2020-2025, the first and third of each quarter's opening month, the second Friday of each quarter's middle month, and the window-end dates) the reconciliation block is present on all 146 dates inside the data window under both interpreters, with identical values to 1e-9, and unavailable only on the two dates past the window (`not_requested_or_after_data_window`). Disabling the alignment reproduces the failure in `dashboard/.venv` on 60 of those dates (`forecast_side_unavailable`).
- **Quota met needs no conversion.** When the quota is already won the status is `quota_met` even where the realized conversion is unavailable; the conversion-implied fields are then null.

## Data quality / metric governance

- **Built**: `analytics/data_quality_governance.py`. Grain: one governance run per `as_of_date`, three independent check families each at their own natural grain (one row per metric-tree edge; one row per mart/column/table for the data-quality checks; one row per invariant rule) — everything the module publishes is one of those three families plus a synthetic self-check. Report written to `analytics/outputs/data_quality_governance_<as_of_date>.json` / `.md` (version-controlled, matching the Weekly executive readout's precedent for a real-data document being inspectable without rebuilding the pipeline). No dbt model was added or changed for this artifact, and no Phase 1 generator or raw table was added.
- **What it is**: build spec item #18, Wave 3 ("infrastructure before more gets built on ungoverned data"). Three distinct jobs, none of which any other artifact does:
  1. **Metric-tree mathematical integrity.** For every parent node in `docs/acme-corp-gtm-metric-tree.md` that states a sum/product/ratio formula over its children, computationally verify the built marts actually satisfy that equation, in real values, at `as_of_date`. This operationalizes `CLAUDE.md`'s invariant ("every parent metric must be the actual mathematical result of its children... never a 'related metrics' grouping") as a runnable check instead of a design promise.
  2. **Marts-layer data quality.** `docs/acme-corp-phase1-data-qa-plan.md`'s test suite (referential integrity, distributional realism, volume sufficiency) covers raw generator output only. This artifact runs the analogous check against the dbt-*built* marts specifically, since a dbt bug could still corrupt the data after raw generation passes clean.
  3. **Invariant governance.** `CLAUDE.md`'s "Non-negotiable invariants" section is a set of design promises (segment terminology, no downgrade path, USD only, channel/segment orthogonality, the four `trigger_reason` values, no independently-random risk columns). This mechanizes each one into a check against the live built data, including three data-grounded spot-checks — not a generic assertion — that win probability, churn probability and usage growth each carry a real relationship to a stated driver.
- **Explicitly not a duplicate of `drift-monitor`**: `drift-monitor` is recurring *production* monitoring of a *fitted* model's calibration drift and Layer-3-to-Layer-2 proxy-metric decoupling (per this doc's "Proxy-metric decoupling — general rule"), checked against a stated drift threshold over time. Nothing in this artifact is fitted, and nothing here tracks decay over a time series — every check is a one-shot, deterministic comparison against already-materialized values or a fixed rule, re-run fresh at whatever `as_of_date` is asked for. The overlap that might look like duplication and isn't: `drift-monitor` asks "has this model's accuracy degraded since it was built," while this artifact asks "does the built data actually satisfy the arithmetic/structural contract it always claimed to." A metric-tree edge that fails here is a correctness bug (something to fix), not a drift event (something to watch trend on) — the distinction the Variance-diagnostic engine entry draws between a deterministic tree walk and a fitted attribution model applies here for the identical reason.
- **Shape decision — structural/logic artifact, not predictive**: every check is a deterministic comparison — either an exact arithmetic identity (tolerance is floating-point noise, not a statistical band), a referential-integrity anti-join, a null-rate threshold, or a two-sample comparison with a stated, PROPOSED floor. Nothing is fitted; there is no train/test split, no estimated parameter, no probability model. Per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' **"Structural/logic artifacts"** category — the same reasoning the Variance-diagnostic engine, Segment migration analysis, Capacity planning and Marketing attribution entries all use — **there is no coefficient table, no AUC, no confusion matrix, no R²/RMSE and no calibration note here, and their absence is deliberate, not pending.**
- **Model type selection and rationale (why not a fitted anomaly/drift-detection model)**: a statistical anomaly detector (e.g., flagging a mart value as suspect when it falls outside its own historical distribution) was the credible alternative and was rejected on two grounds specific to this artifact's job. (1) **Most of what this artifact checks is a known, exact identity, not an unknown distribution.** The metric tree's parent-child formulas are stated exactly (sum/product/ratio); CLAUDE.md's invariants are stated exactly (three segment values, two allowed migration directions, four trigger reasons). Fitting a distribution to detect deviation from a quantity that has an exact, already-known correct answer would replace a certain check with an uncertain one — the same argument the Variance-diagnostic engine entry makes for walking a known arithmetic identity rather than fitting coefficients to it, and Capacity planning's entry makes for summing rather than regressing. (2) **A historical-distribution anomaly detector needs history this artifact doesn't have yet** — this is the first checkpoint of a governance run that has never existed before, so there is no trailing distribution of "normal" to compare a new checkpoint against. `fact_model_performance_history` accumulating checkpoints over time is exactly the mechanism that *would* eventually support that kind of check — and is `drift-monitor`'s job, not this artifact's, once enough checkpoints exist.
- **No random seed applies.** No stochastic step exists anywhere in this module — no train/test split, no sampling, no simulation — matching `analytics/segment_migration.py`'s, `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s and `analytics/capacity_planning.py`'s precedent. The one place a seed appears at all is inside `run_synthetic_violation_tests()`'s independently-random negative-control scenario (`np.random.default_rng(42)`), which exists only to construct a synthetic "this column really is random noise" case for the self-check suite — seeded for reproducibility like every other stochastic step in this project, even though it produces no business output.
- **Point-in-time design**: every loader takes `as_of_date` and filters `month <= as_of_date` / `close_date <= as_of_date` / `effective_date <= as_of_date` / `migration_date <= as_of_date` as appropriate to each source mart's own grain; `dim_accounts` and `dim_reps` (current-state / point-in-time-period dimensions) are filtered on `signup_date <= as_of_date` and `period_start_date <= as_of_date` respectively, the same treatment `analytics/capacity_planning.py` gives `dim_reps`. No table here is read without a point-in-time filter where the table's grain supports one.

### 1. Metric-tree mathematical integrity — what's checked, and what isn't

Every parent-child edge in the tree that states a formula is represented below as one of four statuses — **PASS/FAIL** (computed against real marts values), **NOT_COMPUTABLE** (a genuine data gap, with reason), or **VALIDATED_ELSEWHERE** (computable, but already independently validated by another artifact and deliberately not re-derived here to avoid a second, silently-diverging implementation) — never silently omitted, the same "flagged, not dropped" treatment `mart_growth_bridge`'s own header, `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s coverage table and `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s `NOT_COMPUTABLE_LAYER3_LEAVES` all give their own gaps.

**Result at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31** (14 computed edges, all pass; second checkpoint 2025-06-30 also all pass):

| Edge | Formula | Status | Max abs diff |
|---|---|---|---|
| Growth pillar identity | `Starting + New logo + Expansion − Contraction − Churn (± migration, nets to zero)` | **PASS** | $0.00 |
| Growth pillar migration qualifier | company-wide `Σmigration_in == Σmigration_out` per month | **PASS** | $2.9e-11 |
| Win rate (Layer 2) | `Closed won ÷ (won + lost)` | **PASS** | 0.00 |
| Net new ARR (Magic number's numerator, cross-mart) | `(new_logo_mrr + expansion_mrr − contraction_mrr − churn_mrr) × 12` | **PASS** | $1.9e-9 |
| AM expansion ARR (AM efficiency's numerator, cross-mart) | `expansion_mrr × 12` | **PASS** | $1.9e-9 |
| S&M cost (Layer 2, under Magic number) | `Rep fully-loaded cost (incl. ramp) + Marketing spend allocation by channel` | **PASS** | $0.00 |
| Magic number (Layer 1) | `Net new ARR ÷ prior-period S&M cost` (denominator is the prior month's `sm_cost`) | **PASS** | 0.00 |
| AM efficiency (Layer 1) | `Expansion consumption revenue ÷ AM cost` (monthly expansion MRR ÷ same-month AM cost) | **PASS** | 8.9e-16 |
| Consumption payback (Layer 1) | `CAC ÷ utilized-Action margin` | **PASS** | 0.00 |
| Onboarding/CS efficiency (Layer 1) | `AM/CS touchpoints ÷ automated Actions delivered` | **PASS** | 0.00 |
| NRR (Layer 1) | `(Starting − Contraction − Churn + Expansion) ÷ Starting` | **PASS** | 0.00 |
| GRR (Layer 1) | `(Starting − Contraction − Churn) ÷ Starting` | **PASS** | 0.00 |
| Logo retention (Layer 1) | `Retained accounts ÷ Starting accounts` | **PASS** | 0.00 |
| NRR/GRR ↔ Growth cross-mart consistency | `mart_durability`'s dollar bridge == `mart_growth_bridge`'s revenue bridge | **PASS** | $1.9e-9 |
| New logo = Pipeline × Win rate × Avg commitment | product | **NOT_COMPUTABLE** | — |
| Pipeline generated = Σ(channel volume × channel-to-lead × lead-to-PQL) | sum of products | **VALIDATED_ELSEWHERE** | — |
| Expansion = Wallet share progression × Overage realization | product | **NOT_COMPUTABLE** | — |

Reasons, stated rather than left implicit:
- **New logo = Pipeline × Win rate × Avg commitment**: no mart exposes Pipeline generated at New Logo's own population. `mart_growth_bridge`'s `win_rate`/`avg_initial_commitment` are computed over closed SQO-stage opportunities; Pipeline generated (per `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`) is computed over converting *leads* — a different population and a different unit (SMB in particular has no real win-rate concept at all: one Opportunity record, always Closed Won). `mart_growth_bridge`'s own header already states the two New Logo lenses "will NOT match exactly... a normal, real bookings-vs-revenue-recognition gap, not a bug." Multiplying three marts-derived legs that don't share a population would manufacture a false tie-out, not check a real one.
- **Pipeline generated's own internal formula**: genuinely computable from marts (`fact_leads`, `fact_campaign_engagement_events`, `dim_campaign`), and already is — `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s own build-time check (documented above as `pipeline_generated_formula_is_exact`; the function is `reconcile_pipeline_generated_identity`, listed in that module's own checks table as `pipeline_generated_rollup_reconciles_to_source`) validates it exactly (≤1e-9 tolerance, passing at both its checkpoints). That correctness depends on non-trivial point-in-time lead-resolution-horizon logic (open/lapsed/converted state, derived per sub-channel) already owned by that module. Re-implementing it a second time here, reading the same marts but maintained separately, would risk a silently-diverging copy rather than adding governance value — so this edge is reported as validated with a pointer to where the check actually runs, not re-derived and not skipped.
- **Expansion = Wallet share progression × Overage realization**: Wallet share progression (% of an account's total addressable workflow footprint running through Acme Corp) has no source anywhere in the raw data or marts — nothing estimates the non-Acme denominator. Confirmed against `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s own coverage table ("Expansion consumption revenue | 1 of 2": Overage realization is computed as an overage share of MRR, but the product needs both factors). A genuine Phase 1 data gap, not a scope choice a Phase 4 workaround could close.

**Excluded by design (the tree's own explicitly non-additive nodes)**: **Brand & awareness** ("leading indicator, not summed into the pipeline math") and **Marketing–sales handoff quality** ("diagnostic overlay on the above three... not a fourth multiplicative factor" — both quotes are the tree's own text). Both are registered as excluded rather than checked, consistent with `CLAUDE.md`'s invariant, which names both as deliberate non-additive exceptions.

**A genuine cross-mart consistency check worth naming explicitly**: `mart_durability` materializes its own `dollar_bridge` CTE from `int_revenue_movements` independently of `mart_growth_bridge`'s `revenue_bridge` CTE, even though both read the same upstream source and the tree's own text says NRR/GRR "See Growth — expansion, contraction, churn drivers." `check_durability_growth_cross_mart_consistency()` confirms the two marts' `starting_mrr`/`expansion_mrr`/`contraction_mrr`/`churn_mrr` agree to floating-point precision for every segment-month — the same two-marts-share-one-upstream-source reconciliation pattern the Segment migration analysis entry applies to `mart_growth_bridge` vs. `mart_segment_migration`.

### 2. Marts-layer data quality — what's checked

Four categories, mirroring the Phase 1 QA plan's own structure (referential/structural integrity, distributional realism, volume/sufficiency) but run against the dbt-*built* marts rather than raw generator output — a layer the Phase 1 QA plan's own suite does not cover:

| Category | Checks | Result (2025-12-31) |
|---|---|---|
| Referential integrity | 9 FK pairs across `fact_opportunities`→`dim_accounts`/`dim_reps`, `fact_revenue_monthly`→`dim_accounts`, `fact_account_segment_history`→`dim_accounts`, `mart_segment_migration`→`dim_accounts`, `fact_usage_monthly`→`dim_accounts`, `fact_leads`→`dim_accounts`, `fact_forecast_submissions`→`fact_opportunities`, `fact_committed_vs_utilized_monthly`→`dim_accounts` — every non-null child value must resolve; two pairs (`fact_opportunities.account_id`, `fact_opportunities.rep_id`, `fact_leads.account_id`) explicitly allow nulls where the data model says nulls are legitimate (lost deals with no traceable account, SMB's system-owned deals, non-converting leads) | **9 / 9 pass** |
| Completeness | 13 not-null checks on keys and required columns across `dim_accounts`, `fact_opportunities`, `mart_growth_bridge`, `mart_durability`, `mart_efficiency`, `mart_account_health`, `fact_account_segment_history` | **13 / 13 pass** |
| Distributional sanity | `win_rate` ∈ [0,1]; `nrr` ∈ [−1,10]; `grr` ∈ [−1,2]; `logo_retention_rate` ∈ [0,1]; won deal amount under 2× its segment's documented ACV ceiling (SMB $30K / Commercial $150K / Enterprise $1.5M) — deliberately loose corruption-catching bounds, not the QA plan's tighter business-realism bands, since this module's job is "did dbt corrupt a value," not "is the business shape realistic" (already the QA plan's job upstream) | **5 / 5 pass** |
| Volume sufficiency | Minimum row-count floors on `dim_accounts` (≥4,000), `fact_opportunities` (≥3,000), `mart_growth_bridge`/`mart_durability`/`mart_efficiency` (≥90 each, 3 segments × ≥30 months), `fact_account_segment_history` (≥4,000), `fact_usage_monthly` (≥50,000) | **7 / 7 pass** |

**34 / 34 marts-layer data-quality checks pass at both checkpoints** (2025-12-31: 7,700 accounts, 9,815 opportunities, 142,942 usage-monthly rows, 8,509 segment-history rows; 2025-06-30 on the truncated window, same shape).

### 3. Invariant governance — what's checked

Every `CLAUDE.md` "Non-negotiable invariant" mechanized into a check against live built data, plus three data-grounded not-independently-random spot-checks (deliberately concrete, not a generic assertion, per the task's own instruction):

| Invariant | Check | Result (2025-12-31) |
|---|---|---|
| Segment terminology | Every segment-valued column (`dim_accounts.segment`, `dim_reps.segment`, `fact_account_segment_history.segment`, `mart_segment_migration.from_segment`/`to_segment`) contains only `{SMB, Commercial, Enterprise}` — no "tier," no fourth value | **PASS** (5/5 columns clean) |
| No segment downgrade | Every `(from_segment, to_segment)` pair in `mart_segment_migration` is one of the two allowed forward directions (`SMB→Commercial`, `Commercial→Enterprise`) — never a downgrade, never a skip-level | **PASS** — 809 events, both observed pairs allowed, 0 invalid |
| Currency USD-only | No column anywhere in the `main_marts` schema matches `currency`/`fx_rate`/`exchange_rate` | **PASS** — 0 of 278 columns scanned |
| Channel/segment orthogonality | `self_serve` and `inbound_marketing` each span all 3 segments (real orthogonality, not a partition); `outbound_sdr` — the one documented exception, build spec: "Enterprise-only... not an entry path for the other segments" — appears *only* under Enterprise, confirming the documented exception holds rather than silently passing it | **PASS** |
| `trigger_reason` coverage | All four values (`initial_firmographic`, `initial_default`, `usage_threshold`, `firmographic_rescore`) present in `fact_account_segment_history`, each clearing a floor of 20 rows (**PROPOSED**, see below) | **PASS** — 478 / 7,222 / 564 / 245 |
| Win probability not independently random | Two-proportion z-test: Enterprise POC pass vs. fail win rate (POC pass rate is a stated metric-tree Layer-3 driver of Win rate) | **PASS** — 47.4% vs. 8.5% (gap 38.9pp, z=9.45; floor 15pp @ z≥2.58) |
| Churn probability not independently random | Account-relative: churned accounts' own final-3-months `actions_consumed`/`login_count` vs. their own earlier-month baseline (both stated Account health score inputs) | **PASS** — median relative decline 0.64 (`actions_consumed`) / 0.58 (`login_count`); floor 0.20 |
| Usage growth not independently random | Migrating-accounts' (`trigger_reason='usage_threshold'`) trailing 3-month usage growth rate before `migration_date` vs. same-segment non-migrating population's growth rate over the same window | **PASS** — 4.46 vs. 0.49 (ratio 9.1×; floor 1.5×) |

**8 / 8 invariant checks pass at both checkpoints.**

**A genuine calibration decision worth stating**: the churn-probability spot-check is gated on the **median**, not the mean, relative decline. Confirmed at build time: `actions_consumed`'s median relative decline is 0.64 against a mean of only 0.13, even though 71% of churned accounts individually clear the 0.20 floor. The distribution carries a fat left tail — a minority of accounts with a near-zero usage baseline the whole way through, where a tiny absolute uptick right before churn reads as an enormous negative "decline" (the most extreme synthetic-looking case in the real data: −11.96, i.e. usage roughly 13× its own baseline in the final 3 months) — that drags the mean well below the population's typical behavior. The median is the honest summary of a distribution shaped like this one; gating on the mean here would have been measuring outlier noise, not the real driver relationship the check exists to confirm. `login_count`'s mean and median are much closer together (0.46 vs. 0.58) and both clear the floor regardless of which is used.

### Floors and thresholds — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed

None of these numbers are stated anywhere upstream (build spec, QA plan, metric tree); each is this artifact's own resolved decision at build time, in the same spirit as the Variance-diagnostic engine's ±8% and the Segment migration analysis's 10% firmographic-rescore floor:

- **`trigger_reason` minimum count floor: 20.** Chosen to sit far below every value observed at build time (245–7,222 across the four values), so ordinary future data regeneration can't spuriously trip it, while still being large enough that a single stray row can't fake compliance.
- **ACV outlier ceiling: 2× the segment's documented upper ACV bound.** Deliberately loose (corruption-catching, not business-realism) — the QA plan already owns the tighter distributional-realism band; this only needs to catch a value that's actually broken.
- **Win-probability spot-check: ≥15pp gap at z≥2.58.** The z-threshold matches `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s own holdout-suppression check (99% one-sided confidence), reused here for consistency across the repo's two-proportion comparisons rather than picking a new number.
- **Churn-probability spot-check: ≥0.20 median relative decline.** Conservative — Phase 3's own churn analysis observed usage dropping roughly 66%→23% (a ~65% relative decline) over the run-up to churn, so this floor sits well below the actual effect size and is unlikely to be spuriously tripped by ordinary noise.
- **Usage-growth spot-check: ≥1.5× the non-migrating population's growth rate.** Deliberately modest given the observed effect is far larger (9.1× and 8.5× at the two checkpoints) — the floor exists to catch a real regression, not to sit close to the observed value.

None of these is a validated statistical bound the way the health score's AUC target or capacity planning's achievability margin were checked against sampling noise — they are, honestly, judgment calls sized against the one real dataset this project has. `analytics-model-validator` is asked to sanity-check each against the same reasoning the Segment migration analysis's 10% floor was checked against (does the floor sit on a flat, non-knife-edge part of the observed range).

### Validation — synthetic injected-violation tests, not statistics

Per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' "Structural/logic artifacts" category, this artifact's own correctness claim is "does it actually catch a known-broken case" — checked by `run_synthetic_violation_tests()`, hand-constructed clean and deliberately-broken synthetic inputs run through the *same* checking functions the real marts-backed checks use, each declaring its expected pass/fail outcome. Directly modelled on `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s synthetic scenario suite and `analytics/capacity_planning.py`'s non-vacuousness checks.

**All 10 scenarios pass (match their declared expectation) at both checkpoints:**

| Scenario | Injected violation | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `metric_tree_growth_identity_clean_passes` | none (control) | PASS | **PASS** |
| `metric_tree_growth_identity_injected_break_fails` | `ending_mrr` perturbed by $25,000 | FAIL | **FAIL (caught)** |
| `metric_tree_win_rate_clean_passes` | none (control) | PASS | **PASS** |
| `metric_tree_win_rate_injected_break_fails` | `win_rate` stored as 0.75 against 40 won / 60 lost (implies 0.40) | FAIL | **FAIL (caught)** |
| `segment_terminology_clean_passes` | none (control) | PASS | **PASS** |
| `segment_terminology_fabricated_tier_value_fails` | one account's segment replaced with `"Tier 2"` | FAIL | **FAIL (caught)** |
| `no_segment_downgrade_clean_passes` | none (control) | PASS | **PASS** |
| `no_segment_downgrade_fabricated_path_fails` | a fabricated `Enterprise→SMB` migration row injected | FAIL | **FAIL (caught)** |
| `win_probability_real_driver_relationship_passes` | none (control — real driver relationship) | PASS | **PASS** |
| `win_probability_independently_random_column_fails` | `is_won` drawn independently of `poc_outcome` (seeded `np.random.default_rng(42)`) | FAIL | **FAIL (caught)** |

This is the artifact's stated correctness bar, not a supplementary nice-to-have: a governance checker that cannot be shown to catch a parent-doesn't-sum-to-children case, a fabricated "tier" value, a fabricated downgrade path, and a genuinely-random risk column is not demonstrated to work at all, regardless of how clean the real data happens to look.

### Persistence

Scalar summary counts (`checks_total`/`_passed`/`_failed`, `metric_tree_edges_checked`/`_passed`/`_not_computable`, `referential_integrity_checks_total`/`_passed`, `completeness_checks_passed`, `distributional_sanity_checks_passed`, `volume_sufficiency_checks_passed`, `invariant_checks_total`/`_passed`, `synthetic_violation_scenarios_passed`/`_total`) are logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `log_performance()`. The variable-width detail (per-edge results, per-FK-pair/per-column results, per-invariant detail, the full synthetic-scenario table) does not fit that log's flat scalar grain and is the structured detail recorded in `analytics/outputs/data_quality_governance_<as_of_date>.json`/`.md` and in this entry instead, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' Persistence note.

### Target and drift threshold

- **Target**: no accuracy/error target applies (structural/logic artifact). The three check families above (56 checks total at 2025-12-31: 14 metric-tree edges + 34 marts-layer data-quality checks + 8 invariant checks) plus the 10-scenario synthetic self-check stand in its place — **all 56 real-data checks and all 10 synthetic scenarios pass at both checkpoints.**
- **Drift review trigger**: per this doc's general rule, 2 consecutive monthly checkpoints with any real-data check failing → flagged for review. In practice most of these checks are structural invariants expected to never fail on correctly-built data (a failure is a defect to fix, not a trend to watch, the same treatment Capacity planning's exact tie-outs get) — the two genuinely worth watching in `fact_model_performance_history` over time are `metric_tree_edges_not_computable` (should only ever *decrease*, as Phase 1/2 gaps close — an increase would mean a mart regressed) and the three not-independently-random spot-check margins (`win_probability` gap/z, `churn_probability` median decline, `usage_growth` ratio) — a real narrowing of any of those margins over time, even while still technically clearing its floor, would be the leading indicator that a generator or dbt change had quietly weakened a causal relationship this project's other artifacts depend on existing. This is a `drift-monitor` hook, not something this build-time run itself tracks over time.

### Known limitations

- **The `trigger_reason` floor, ACV ceiling, and the three spot-check floors are all first-checkpoint judgment calls**, not statistically derived the way the health score's AUC target or capacity planning's achievability margin were. They are stated explicitly as PROPOSED and handed to `analytics-model-validator` rather than treated as settled.
- **The metric-tree integrity checker covers every edge that has a stated formula in the tree text, but the tree also contains many Layer-2/Layer-3 nodes that are single measurements, not decompositions of listed children** (e.g. Avg initial commitment, Activation's own TTFA figure) — those have nothing to mathematically tie out and are correctly absent from the edge list, not silently skipped; `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s own coverage table is the fuller inventory of which Layer-2/Layer-3 leaves are computable at all versus which have a real formula to check.
- **This is a first build-time checkpoint, not a monitored series yet.** `fact_model_performance_history` now carries two checkpoints (2025-12-31, 2025-06-30) for this artifact; a governance check that has only ever been run twice on a green build has not yet been tested against a real regression. The synthetic injected-violation suite is the substitute evidence that the mechanism works, per the shape decision above — it is not a substitute for eventually seeing this artifact catch something real.
- **No check here re-validates the Phase 1 QA plan's own test suite** (raw-generator-layer checks) — this module starts at the dbt-built marts layer, deliberately, since the QA plan already owns raw-layer correctness and re-running it here would be duplicative, not additive governance.

## Automated playbook triggers

- **Built**: `analytics/playbook_triggers.py`. Grain: one row per trigger firing (`rule_id`, `account_id`, `timestamp`, `resulting_action`, `outcome`) — the exact operational-log grain build spec line 118 states — with outcome-capture, owner, SLA and escalation columns appended after those five. Reads `fact_workflow_chain_events`, `fact_opportunities`, `fact_committed_vs_utilized_monthly`, `fact_am_activity`, `fact_subscriptions`, `dim_reps`, `dim_accounts`, `fact_account_segment_history` (`main_marts` only). Logged to `fact_playbook_triggers`, backed by `data/playbook_triggers.csv` (version-controlled, append-only, upsert-safe), and wired into `analytics/weekly_readout.py`'s "Automated playbook triggers" section. `dbt/models/staging/stg_playbook_triggers.sql` and `dbt/models/marts/facts/fact_playbook_triggers.sql` expose the table, mirroring `fact_model_performance_history`'s exact CSV-source-external-table pattern.
- **What it is**: build spec item #10, Wave 4 ("needs Wave 1's thresholds validated against real data first" — satisfied: the variance-diagnostic engine and weekly executive readout, Wave 1's fourth and fifth artifacts, are both built and validated). Build spec Section 5: "binary threshold rules… thresholds stored as configurable rules, not hardcoded into a narrative prompt, with a log of which triggers fired and what action resulted." Both `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py` and `analytics/weekly_readout.py` record the resolved scope decision that playbook triggers are deferred to their own Wave-4 build — this is that build.
- **Shape decision — structural/logic, not predictive**: every rule is a deterministic threshold comparison against already-materialized mart values. Per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' "Structural/logic artifacts" category (the same category the variance-diagnostic engine, segment migration analysis, capacity planning, marketing attribution and data-quality-governance entries all use): **no coefficient table, no AUC, no confusion matrix, no R²/RMSE and no calibration note applies here, and their absence is deliberate, not pending.** The correctness claim is narrower and fully checkable: does each rule fire on a hand-constructed case it should fire on, and not fire on one it shouldn't — `run_synthetic_scenarios()`.
- **Model type selection and rationale (why not a fitted model)**: a propensity-style classifier (e.g., "predict which accounts will need a workflow-chain escalation") was the credible alternative and was rejected on the same grounds the segment-migration and marketing-attribution entries reject a propensity classifier for their own rule-governed mechanisms: build spec Section 5 specifies these as **binary threshold rules by design** — "configurable rules, not hardcoded into a narrative prompt" — not a prediction task. A fitted model would replace an auditable, stored threshold with an opaque score, undermining the entire reason build spec Section 5 asks for rules over a narrative prompt in the first place: a rule a CRO can read, question, and retune is the deliverable, not a probability nobody can inspect.
- **Determinism / seeding**: no stochastic step exists anywhere in this module — no sampling, no simulation, no train/test split. Every rule is a deterministic threshold comparison over already-materialized mart values, so no random seed applies, matching `analytics/segment_migration.py`'s and `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s precedent.
- **Point-in-time design**: every loader filters `month <= as_of_date` / `close_date <= as_of_date`. Each rule's own trailing-window logic (the ingestion-stall streak, the POC cohort pass rate) only ever looks backward from the row being evaluated, never at a later-dated value, so no rule can leak a future month into a trigger timestamped earlier.

### Why these three rules, and why their thresholds depart from the build spec's own illustrative numbers

Build spec Section 5 offers three rules as worked examples ("e.g."), not a fixed spec: *"14+ days of ingestion-without-completion," "POC pass rate below 60%," "30 days post-close under 50% committed-Action utilization."* All three are kept as the starting set — each maps cleanly onto a real mart (`fact_workflow_chain_events`, `fact_opportunities.poc_outcome`, `fact_committed_vs_utilized_monthly`) and the build spec itself frames them as this artifact's working examples. Two translations were necessary and are stated rather than silently applied:

1. **Day-grain → month-grain.** None of the three source marts carries day-level ingestion or utilization events — every one is account-per-**month** (`fact_workflow_chain_events`, `fact_committed_vs_utilized_monthly` are both monthly; POC resolution is a single close-dated event, not a daily series). "14+ days" and "30 days" become "2+ consecutive months" and "the first full calendar month post-close" respectively — the same day-to-month translation Activation's trailing-baseline treatment and the account-health score's "N consecutive months" churn language already establish elsewhere in this project.
2. **The literal 60%/50% numbers do not survive contact with this data's real distribution**, and keeping them unchanged would have produced rules that fire almost constantly rather than genuine outliers (see the grounding below for each). Both are lowered to sit at a real, non-trivial minority of the observed distribution instead — the same discipline the data-quality-governance entry's PROPOSED floors and the segment-migration entry's 10% firmographic-rescore floor both apply: a threshold that fires on the overwhelming majority of cases is not a trigger, it is a constant.

### Rule 1 — Ingestion-without-completion (`ingestion_without_completion`)

**Rule**: `full_chain_completion_rate < 0.70` sustained for **2+ consecutive months** — fires once, at the month the streak first reaches that length (not once per month the episode continues). Source: `fact_workflow_chain_events` (`account_id`, `month`, `full_chain_completion_rate` = `downstream_actions / upstream_actions`). Action: *"Escalate to CS for workflow-chain diagnostic outreach."*

**Grounding (PROPOSED, not yet confirmed)**: `full_chain_completion_rate` across all 142,942 account-months is genuinely **bimodal with an empty gap** — a histogram in 0.021-wide bins shows roughly 470–530 account-months per bin from 0.349 up to 0.644, then **zero account-months in every bin from 0.644 to 0.834**, then 5,000–22,000 per bin from 0.834 to 0.981. **0.70 sits in the middle of that empty gap** — there is no distributional knife-edge near it in either direction, since nothing in the real data lies between 0.644 and 0.834 at all. This is a real, designed subpopulation, not sampling noise: **every observed below-0.70 episode is 3–5 consecutive months long** (1,461 episodes, minimum run length 3, none of length 1 or 2) — a **2-consecutive-month** persistence gate is chosen deliberately below that observed minimum, so it is guaranteed to catch every real episode (since none is shorter than 3 months) while still being unable to fire on an isolated single bad month, because no such case exists anywhere in this data to begin with.

### Rule 2 — POC pass rate below threshold (`poc_pass_rate_below_threshold`)

**Rule**: fires for a specific Enterprise opportunity when **its own POC resolves `fail` AND the trailing 90-day cohort POC pass rate** (every other Enterprise POC that closed strictly before this one, within the trailing window, cohort size ≥ 5) **sits below 30%**. Source: `fact_opportunities` (`account_id`, `opportunity_id`, `close_date`, `poc_outcome`, `segment = 'Enterprise'`). Action: *"Escalate to Sales Engineering leadership for POC process review."*

**Grounding (PROPOSED, not yet confirmed)**: overall Enterprise POC pass rate is **42.5%** (192 of 452, at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31) — the build spec's illustrative 60% floor sits *above* the population mean, and a trailing-90-day rolling pass rate computed per-POC (n=424 resolved rows with a full window) has **median 40.0%, IQR [34.9%, 48.1%]**; a 60% floor would fire on **99.8%** of resolved POCs — not a trigger, a constant. **30% sits close to the 10th percentile (28.7%)** of that same trailing-rate distribution — comfortably below the median so it does not fire near-continuously, while still catching a real, non-trivial minority (12.97% of trailing-rate observations sit below 0.30) of genuinely underperforming windows.

**A structural limitation, found at build time, not glossed over**: `fact_opportunities.account_id` is **NULL on every lost opportunity** — the identical gap `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s "Measure caveat" already documents for lost new-business deals, confirmed here to hold for lost Enterprise/POC-stage opportunities generally. Of the 452 Enterprise POCs at 2025-12-31, only the 113 that closed **Won** carry a traceable `account_id` (91 pass, 22 fail-but-still-won); the other 339 (101 pass-but-lost, 238 fail-and-lost) have no account this operational log's `(rule_id, account, timestamp, …)` schema can name. This module handles it in the only honest way available: **the trailing cohort pass rate is computed over every resolved POC in the window regardless of whether its own account is nameable** (dropping the null-`account_id` rows before computing the cohort rate would compute it over a Won-only, survivorship-biased subset — 91 of 113 Won POCs passed, 80.5%, against the true population rate of 42.5% — which would make the rule almost never fire for the wrong reason); **only the row that would actually be logged requires a real `account_id`**, and that filter is applied after the cohort rate, not before it. The real consequence: the systemic condition (a fail during a low-cohort-rate window) fires **30 times** at `as_of_date` 2025-11-30, but only **1 of those 30** has a traceable account to log. This is a real Phase 1/Phase 2 data gap (account_id on lost opportunities), not a Phase 4 workaround — flagged, not fabricated, the same treatment the marketing-attribution entry gives the identical gap.

### Rule 3 — Post-close committed-Action utilization (`post_close_underutilization`)

**Rule**: fires when a Commercial/Enterprise new-business account's **first full calendar month post-close** (the monthly-grain translation of "30 days post-close") carries committed-Action `utilization_rate < 0.30`. Source: `fact_opportunities` (`account_id`, `close_date`, `segment`, `opportunity_type = 'new_business'`, `is_won = true`) joined to `fact_committed_vs_utilized_monthly` (`account_id`, `month`, `utilization_rate = utilized_actions_monthly / committed_actions_monthly`) on the post-close month. A row with no committed-vs-utilized entry for that month (no commitment on file yet) is excluded, not treated as zero utilization — a missing commitment and a genuinely-unused one are different facts. Action: *"Trigger AM proactive onboarding check-in."*

**Grounding (PROPOSED, not yet confirmed)**: `committed_actions_monthly` is populated **only for Commercial (14,609 of 15,859 account-months) and Enterprise (3,265 of 3,375)** — SMB carries zero, exactly matching the build spec's own company model ("SMB: metered, month-to-month, no contract" — no commitment to have a rate against). Restricting to won new-business Commercial/Enterprise accounts' first full post-close month (432 matched rows at 2025-12-31) gives **median utilization 42.2%, IQR [35.3%, 46.5%]** — the build spec's illustrative 50% floor sits *above* the median and would fire on **85.9%** of newly-closed accounts, which is normal early-ramp behavior, not a risk signal. **30% sits near the 20th–22nd percentile** (10th percentile 23.7%, 25th percentile 35.3%; 22.2% of rows fall below 0.30) — comfortably below the typical ramp pattern, catching a genuine underutilizing minority rather than the ordinary "just closed, not yet fully onboarded" majority.

**A note on what this threshold is not shown to predict**: a same-cohort comparison of eventual `customer_status` shows low-post-close-utilization accounts (< 0.30) churning at 5.2% against 13.1% for accounts above the floor, but that gap is a segment-composition effect, not a signal. All 96 flagged accounts are Enterprise; the 336 unflagged accounts are 319 Commercial and 17 Enterprise. Eventual churn is 5 of 96 for flagged Enterprise, 0 of 17 for unflagged Enterprise, and 44 of 319 for Commercial (no Commercial account is flagged); the 13.1% is the Commercial rate diluted by 17 Enterprise accounts. Enterprise median first-month utilization is 0.25 against 0.44 for Commercial, so a 0.30 floor mostly marks segment membership (96 of 113 Enterprise new-business rows, 85%, are flagged). Within Enterprise the comparison has 17 unflagged accounts and no churn among them, too few to read. No churn signal for this rule can be assessed from this data. Per the shape decision above, the rule does not need a predictive correlation to be valid — it is a stored, inspectable operational threshold, not a fitted model — but it should not be read as a churn warning.

### Outcome capture, ownership and SLA

Every value in this section is **PROPOSED, not yet confirmed**: windows, recovery thresholds, SLA lengths and the owner bases are first-build judgement calls held as configuration (`OUTCOME_CRITERIA`, `OWNER_RULES`, `SLA_CONFIG` in `analytics/playbook_triggers.py`), alongside the rule thresholds above, and are handed to `analytics-model-validator` as such.

**Operational-log schema.** `data/playbook_triggers.csv` keeps the five build-spec columns first, then appends `outcome_date`, `outcome_metric_value`, `outcome_reason`, `outcome_window_end`, `outcome_evaluated_as_of`, `outcome_source`, `owner_rep_id`, `owner_role`, `owner_unresolved_reason`, `account_territory`, `sla_hours`, `sla_due_at`, `escalation_to`, `escalation_reason`, `closed_at`. `fact_playbook_triggers` exposes all of them and computes `sla_status` and `escalation_required` as of `var('analysis_as_of_date')` (2025-12-31), so the stored log never goes stale.

**Outcome vocabulary.** `resolved` (the rule's success criterion was met inside its window), `unresolved` (it was not, including the account churning first), `pending` (the window has not elapsed as of the evaluation date), `not_evaluable` (the window elapsed but the data cannot decide it). `pending` is structural: any automated outcome other than `pending` requires `outcome_window_end <= outcome_evaluated_as_of`, even when a recovery is already visible in data dated before the window closes; evidence is only read from after the trigger and up to the evaluation date. `outcome_date` is the date of the deciding evidence, `outcome_metric_value` the value that decided it, `outcome_reason` why.

| Rule | Success criterion (PROPOSED) | Window | Why this criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ingestion_without_completion` | `full_chain_completion_rate` >= 0.70 for 2 consecutive calendar months, inside the window | 6 months after the trigger month | The rule's own bar (0.70) and persistence (2 months), reversed: the stall is over when the account is back above the bar for as long as it took to count as a stall. 6 months comfortably exceeds the 3–5-month episodes observed in the data. |
| `poc_pass_rate_below_threshold` | Enterprise POC pass rate over the 90 days after the trigger >= 0.30 (>= 5 resolved POCs), every resolved POC counted whether its account is nameable or not | 90 days | The rule fires on a systemic low pass rate, so success is that condition clearing, measured on the same population the rule's own cohort rate uses. |
| `post_close_underutilization` | `utilization_rate` >= 0.50 for 2 consecutive months, inside the window | 3 months after the post-close month | The build spec's own illustrative 50% floor, held as the level a healthy ramp reaches; the trigger's 0.30 is the alarm level, not the recovery level. |

**Backfill, evaluated as of 2025-12-31 (the latest `as_of_date` the repo uses) over the 1,558 logged triggers:**

| Rule | Logged | resolved | unresolved | pending | not_evaluable |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ingestion_without_completion` | 1,461 | 0 | 1,319 (all `churned_before_recovery`) | 142 | 0 |
| `poc_pass_rate_below_threshold` | 1 | 1 (forward pass rate 54.1%) | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| `post_close_underutilization` | 96 | 96 (median deciding utilization 135.9%) | 0 | 0 | 0 |

**Are the alerts informative? Flagged versus comparable non-flagged (`evaluate_rule_informativeness(2025-12-31)`; descriptive, no inference):**

- **Rule 1 — strongly informative as a churn signal, and the outcome is not recoverable.** 100% of the 1,319 flagged accounts with a closed 6-month horizon churned within it (all 1,461 logged accounts have churned by 2025-12-31), against **3.3%** of comparable unflagged accounts (same segment as of the trigger month, completion >= 0.70 this and last month, same trigger months, weighted to the flagged mix; 92,515 account-months). The median gap from the trigger month to the churn date is 3.4 months (range 1.0–4.0). 1,461 of the 1,837 churned accounts in the data (79.5%) carry this alert. The alert is an early pre-churn signature with roughly one to four months of lead; since no flagged account ever recovered, the data cannot say whether outreach could have changed that.
- **Rule 2 — the condition clears on its own; not shown to be informative.** Across all 30 failed POCs that fired the systemic condition (traceable account or not; 1 is logged), 30 of 30 (100%) saw the forward 90-day pass rate recover to >= 30% (mean 43.9%), against 88.5% (177 of 200 decidable) after failures in a healthy window (mean 40.6%). Two qualifications apply. The 30 firings fall in about four clusters of overlapping trailing windows (Jul–Sep 2023, Aug–Sep 2024, Dec 2024, Jun–Jul 2025), so the effective sample is about 4, not 30. And the recovery test uses the same 0.30 bar that fires the rule, so recovery by mean reversion after a low window is close to built in. A low trailing window is followed by a pass rate no lower than after a healthy one, consistent with a noisy cohort rate regressing to its mean rather than a persistent process problem. The single logged outcome is `resolved`; n = 1 supports no rule-level conclusion.
- **Rule 3 — no signal can be assessed, and the comparison is confounded by segment.** Recovery by the criterion above is 96 of 96 (100%) for flagged accounts and 285 of 285 (100%) for unflagged ones that have a closed window (51 unflagged are still pending), so the outcome does not separate them and is uninformative. The churn comparison (churn within 12 months of the post-close month: 0 of 76 flagged against 30 of 198 unflagged) reproduces but is a segment effect. All 96 flagged accounts are Enterprise; the unflagged group is 319 Commercial and 17 Enterprise, and all 30 churners are Commercial, where no account is flagged. Within Enterprise the comparison is 0 of 76 flagged against 0 of 3 unflagged. Enterprise first-month utilization is structurally lower (median 0.25 against 0.44 for Commercial), so the 0.30 threshold mostly marks segment membership: 96 of 113 Enterprise new-business rows (85%) are flagged, and the rule is effectively an Enterprise flag. The same composition explains the eventual-churn figures under Rule 3 above (5.2% against 13.1%). `evaluate_rule_informativeness()` does not stratify by segment for this rule; the within-Enterprise figures come from splitting its population by segment. No churn signal, in either direction, can be assessed from this data, and the rule's alerts are not supported as a churn or recovery signal.

**Manual / external outcomes.** `record_outcome(rule_id, account_id, timestamp, outcome=None, outcome_date=None, outcome_metric_value=None, outcome_reason=None, closed_at=None, source='manual')` updates an already-logged trigger (it never creates one), validates the vocabulary, requires an `outcome_date` for any non-pending outcome, and stamps `outcome_source` `manual` or `crm`. This is the seam a CRM round-trip calls. The upsert in `log_triggers()` never lets the automated resolver overwrite a logged outcome whose source is not `automated`, never replaces a later automated evaluation with an earlier one, and never wipes a logged outcome or owner when a caller re-logs a bare five-column frame. Re-running the build is idempotent: the log is byte-identical on a repeated run.

**Owner (resolved from real assignment data; null with an explicit reason otherwise).** The data has no account-to-AM assignment table: an account is tied to an AM only through `fact_am_activity`, so the AM of record is the rep of the account's most recent AM activity on or before the SLA clock start, required to be `active` in `dim_reps` at that date. SMB is no-touch by design and has no AM.

| Rule | Owner basis | Resolved | Unresolved reasons |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ingestion_without_completion` | AM of record (`AM-Commercial` 70, `AM-Enterprise` 4) | 74 of 1,461 (5.1%) | `smb_no_touch_no_human_owner` 1,387 |
