Discount realization, discount-vs-outcome, discount-vs-competitive-pressure and packaging calibration are computed **all-time-to-date** as of `as_of_date` — the same choice the Segment migration entry makes for its trigger-reason-mix and time-in-prior-segment reads, since these are level/distributional statistics, not flows, and an all-time-to-date view avoids thin-sample noise in any single trailing window. Deal-size trend is the one genuinely time-series read and uses matched trailing-12-month windows (current vs. immediately prior), both strictly `<= as_of_date`, so no leakage.
- **Determinism**: the only stochastic step is the two permutation tests' label-shuffle draws, seeded via `_RANDOM_SEED = 42` through a dedicated `np.random.default_rng` stream, reproducible and isolated from every other module's randomness. Everything else (discount realization, packaging calibration, deal-size trend, the reconciliation and band checks) is a direct deterministic aggregation with no random step.

### Findings (computed `as_of_date` 2025-11-30, second checkpoint 2025-06-30)

**1. Discount realization by segment/opportunity_type — matches this project's stated pricing mechanics.** Average realized discount: **SMB 2.46%**, **Commercial 12.3–12.8%** (new business/renewal/expansion all cluster tightly together), **Enterprise 20.1–21.1%**. All seven segment×opportunity_type combinations fall inside `generators/config.py`'s `DISCOUNT_RATE_RANGE` band for that segment (SMB 0–5%, Commercial 5–20%, Enterprise 10–30%) — consistent with the build spec's stated mechanics (SMB metered/no-negotiation runs near-zero discount; Commercial's negotiated annual contract and Enterprise's negotiated multi-year graduated commitment both run materially deeper, with Enterprise deepest). `recomputed_discount` (`1 - amount/list_price`) agrees with the stored `discount_rate` for every row (see reconciliation check below), so this reading is not sensitive to which of the two columns is used.
- **Discount depth does not differ meaningfully by `opportunity_type` within a segment** (Commercial: new business 12.3%, renewal 12.8%, expansion 12.5%; Enterprise: new business 20.2%, renewal 20.7%, expansion 21.1%) — a genuinely flat read, not an artifact of small samples in the smaller legs (renewal/expansion each carry 84–837 opportunities at 2025-11-30).

**2. Discount depth vs. outcome — no real relationship found, and the test methodology is shown to have power to detect one if it existed.** Won vs. lost new-business discount, by segment, permutation test (2,000 shuffles, seeded):

| Segment | avg discount (won) | avg discount (lost) | diff | Pearson r | permutation p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Commercial (2025-11-30) | 12.06% | 12.44% | −0.37pp | −0.040 | 0.178 |
| Enterprise (2025-11-30) | 20.12% | 20.22% | −0.10pp | −0.008 | 0.861 |
| Commercial (2025-06-30) | 12.03% | 12.51% | −0.48pp | −0.052 | 0.100 |
| Enterprise (2025-06-30) | 19.79% | 20.17% | −0.38pp | −0.028 | 0.583 |

None of the four checkpoints clears conventional significance (all p > 0.05); the Commercial legs run closest (p ≈ 0.10–0.18) but the effect size is under half a percentage point of discount in both cases — not a leadership-actionable relationship even where borderline. **Discount depth vs. competitive-loss pressure** (competitive losses vs. all other lost-deal reasons, same permutation test): Commercial diff −0.06pp (p=0.86), Enterprise diff +0.36pp (p=0.56) at 2025-11-30 — likewise no real signal. **Positive control**: `forecast_category` vs. win rate shows a 98.2pp spread (Commit 98.6% won, Omitted 0.5% won) at 2025-11-30 (98.2pp at 2025-06-30) — the same test methodology finds an enormous, obviously-real effect when one exists, which is what makes the discount-outcome null result a genuine finding rather than a sign the tests lack power. **Grounded in the generator**: `discount_rate` is drawn `rng.uniform(*DISCOUNT_RATE_RANGE[segment])` independently of `is_won`/`loss_reason`/`amount` in `generators/opportunities.py` — this data-generating design is exactly what the empirical null result reflects. **Actionable read for leadership**: in this data, deeper discounting does not correlate with winning more often or with avoiding competitive losses — reps who discount are not buying measurably better outcomes, so a discount-approval policy tightened toward the low end of each segment's band would not, on this evidence, cost win rate.

**3. Packaging/committed-usage calibration — commitments run structurally below usage, by design, and the diagnostic confirms the design is landing close to its own target, not that it is "well-calibrated" in a naive centered-on-100%-sense.** Committed-minimum Action volume is deliberately set at `generators/config.py`'s `COMMITTED_VOLUME_FACTOR = 0.65` of an account's baseline usage — the stated design reason is to make overage billing "a common, growing event," the main NRR driver for multi-year Enterprise contracts specifically. That implies a **steady-state expected utilization of 1/0.65 ≈ 1.5385** (154% of committed minimum), not 1.0. Observed (2025-11-30): Commercial median 1.488 (mean 1.512), Enterprise median 1.553 (mean 1.541) — both within 5 percentage points of the 1.5385 design target (diff from expected: Commercial −5.1pp, Enterprise +1.4pp). Only **9.9% (Commercial) / 5.8% (Enterprise)** of account-months land in a naive "well-calibrated around 100%" band (0.8–1.2×); **85.6% / 85.1%** run above 1.2× — i.e. the large majority of account-months rely on overage billing beyond the committed minimum, which is the intended mechanic, not a sizing defect. The yearly trend shows this converging toward the design target as the book seasons: Commercial median utilization rises from 1.338 (2020) to 1.529 (2025); Enterprise from 0.423 (2020, thin/back-dated-era sample — same caveat the Capacity planning entry documents for pre-2023 data) to 1.624 (2025), consistent with the 1.5385 target rather than drifting away from it. **Actionable read for leadership**: committed minimums are not miscalibrated relative to actual usage in the "off by an unpredictable amount" sense — they track a consistent ~65%-of-usage target across both segments and over time — but the practical consequence is that the large majority of Commercial/Enterprise revenue realization depends on overage billing rather than the committed-contract line itself, which is a packaging-strategy question (is that the intended revenue mix) more than a calibration defect.

**4. Deal-size trend within segment band — stable, no material drift.** Trailing-12-month mean new-business deal size vs. the immediately prior 12-month window, at 2025-11-30: SMB −2.1%, Commercial −2.8%, Enterprise +1.6%; at 2025-06-30: SMB −3.1%, Commercial +2.0%, Enterprise +2.1%. All six readings sit well under the proposed ±15% drift threshold (see below), and `check_deal_size_within_band()` confirms every single new-business opportunity's `amount` falls inside its segment's ACV range (100% for all three segments, both checkpoints) — a hard structural invariant since `generators/opportunities.py` clips every amount to `config.ACV_RANGES` at generation time.

### Validation — structural tie-outs and non-vacuousness checks, not statistics

In place of an accuracy/error target (none applies — see the shape decision), four checks. All pass at both checkpoints (2025-11-30 and 2025-06-30).

| Check | What it pins down | Floor / tolerance | Result (2025-11-30) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `check_discount_reconciliation` | Discount realization computed two ways — stored `discount_rate` vs. `1 - amount/list_price` recomputed per opportunity — ties out, since both derive from the same generation-time draw | max abs diff ≤ 0.001 | **6.46e-05** (rounding noise only), 9,413 opportunities |
| `check_deal_size_within_band` | Every new-business `amount` falls inside its segment's `ACV_RANGES` band — a hard structural invariant, not a statistical range | 100% within band, all 3 segments | **100.0%** all 3 segments (SMB n=6,231, Commercial n=1,183, Enterprise n=440) |
| Positive-control non-vacuousness (`compute_positive_control_forecast_category`) | Confirms the permutation-test methodology has real power to detect a relationship when one exists, so the discount-outcome null result is a genuine finding and not a sign the tests can't detect signal | win-rate spread across `forecast_category` > 0.80 | **Pass** — 0.9815 spread (Commit 98.6% vs. Omitted 0.5%) |
| Discount-vs-outcome permutation tests | Non-vacuousness in the other direction: confirms no spurious low p-value appears where none should — all four segment×checkpoint readings land p > 0.05 | — (reported, not gated) | All 4 readings p > 0.05 (0.10–0.86) |

**Sample sizes** (2025-11-30): 9,413 opportunities total (6,231 SMB / 1,183 Commercial new-business / 440 Enterprise new-business, plus renewal/expansion legs); discount-vs-win-rate n = 357 won / 826 lost (Commercial), 113 won / 327 lost (Enterprise); discount-vs-competitive n = 281 competitive / 545 other (Commercial), 108 competitive / 219 other (Enterprise); packaging calibration n = 13,702 account-months (Commercial), 3,070 (Enterprise).

### Target and drift threshold

- **Target**: no accuracy/error target applies (structural/descriptive, with two seeded non-parametric tests, not a fitted model). The four checks above stand in its place.
- **Deal-size drift threshold: ±15% relative change in trailing-12-month mean deal size vs. the prior trailing-12-month window, within a segment — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed.** Nothing upstream (build spec, QA plan, metric tree) states a number for this leaf. Grounded the same way the variance-diagnostic engine's ±8% Layer-1 threshold and Capacity planning's 15% achievability margin are grounded: proposed at build time against this artifact's own observed range. ±15% (roughly double the variance engine's Layer-1 threshold) is deliberately wider because this is a Layer-3 leaf on a much thinner sample per window (as few as 84–151 Enterprise deals per trailing-12-month window, vs. company-wide Layer-1 aggregates) — a tighter band would flag ordinary quarter-to-quarter sampling noise on the smallest segment, the same "a threshold that fires constantly is not a trigger" reasoning the Playbook triggers entry gives for rejecting the build spec's own illustrative 50%/60% example numbers. All 6 observed readings (3 segments × 2 checkpoints) land well inside this band (max abs move 3.1%), so the threshold is not yet exercised by a real breach in this data — expected, since deal size is clipped to a stable band by construction and no generator mechanism drives it to trend. **This band is grounded against the mature (2024+) era only** — the same pre-2023 back-dated-tail caveat the Capacity planning entry documents applies here too: trailing-12-month windows anchored before 2023 swing as far as ±42.7% (Commercial) and ±34.2% (Enterprise), well past this threshold, reflecting the thin early book rather than a real drift signal. Both validated checkpoints (2025-11-30, 2025-06-30) are mature-era, so this doesn't affect the reported results, but a future `drift-monitor` run must not apply this threshold to an `as_of_date` in the pre-2023 window.
- **Packaging calibration reference: median utilization within ±10 percentage points of `EXPECTED_STEADY_STATE_UTILIZATION` (1/`COMMITTED_VOLUME_FACTOR` ≈ 1.5385) — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed.** Grounded directly in `generators/config.py`'s stated design constant rather than an assumed 1.0 target, per the finding above. Observed diffs (−5.1pp to +1.4pp at 2025-11-30, −6.4pp to −1.1pp at 2025-06-30) sit comfortably inside this band on both checkpoints. **This gated figure is a cumulative-since-2020 median, which dilutes the currently-installed book with the thin, low-utilization early years** — the by-year table above shows Enterprise's own 2025 calendar-year median already at 1.624 (+8.55pp from target, 85% of this band's allowance), well past what the diluted cumulative figure (+1.4pp) suggests. As the book keeps seasoning, the cumulative gate will lag a real breach by multiple checkpoints. A future iteration should either re-gate this check on a trailing-window median (matching deal-size drift's own trailing-12-month treatment above) or track the by-year table alongside the cumulative figure as the earlier-warning signal — not yet done here, flagged for `analytics-model-validator`/`drift-monitor` rather than silently left as a cumulative-only read.
- **Discount-vs-outcome / discount-vs-competitive-pressure: no threshold, by design.** These are reported findings (is there a relationship), not a metric with a target level — there is nothing to set a drift threshold against beyond re-running the permutation test at each future checkpoint and confirming the null result persists (a checkpoint where either p-value drops sharply below 0.05 with a materially larger effect size than observed here would be the signal worth investigating — that would mean the generator's discount/outcome independence assumption no longer describes the data, a generator-level question, not a threshold breach in the usual sense).
- **Drift review trigger**: per the doc's general rule, 2 consecutive monthly checkpoints outside either PROPOSED band above → flagged for review. `check_discount_reconciliation` and `check_deal_size_within_band` are structural invariants and are expected to never breach; a breach in either is a defect to fix, not a trend to watch.
- **Persistence**: scalar time-series metrics (`discount_reconciliation_max_abs_diff`, `deal_size_within_band_all_passed`, `positive_control_win_rate_spread`, `test_methodology_non_vacuous`, `discount_vs_win_diff_<segment>`, `discount_vs_win_permutation_p_<segment>`, `discount_vs_competitive_diff_<segment>`, `packaging_median_utilization_<segment>`, `packaging_share_well_calibrated_<segment>`, `deal_size_pct_change_<segment>`) are logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `log_performance()`. The full discount-realization-by-opportunity_type table and the packaging-trend-by-year table don't fit that log's flat scalar grain and are recorded as structured detail in this entry instead, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' Persistence note.

### Known limitations

- **The discount-outcome null result is a property of this generator's design (independent uniform draw), not an empirically-discovered real-world pattern.** It is reported honestly as what the data shows, with the generating mechanism named directly rather than left to look like a surprising empirical finding — see the model-choice rationale above. If a future generator change wires `discount_rate` to `is_won`/competitive pressure (to make the relationship this artifact currently finds absent become real and testable), this module's permutation tests would detect that change directly; no code change would be needed here, only re-running the build-time checkpoint.
- **Packaging calibration is a population/segment-level read, not per-account.** It does not identify *which* specific accounts are most over- or under-utilized relative to their own commitment (that is closer to `analytics/playbook_triggers.py`'s `post_close_underutilization` rule's job, which this module deliberately does not duplicate — that rule is a binary first-month trigger on new accounts specifically, this is a distributional view of the whole installed base over time).
- **No cost/margin data exists to translate "committed minimums run below usage" into a revenue-mix dollar figure** (e.g. committed-contract revenue vs. overage revenue as a share of total) — `fact_committed_vs_utilized_monthly` carries Action volumes, not billed dollars for each line separately, so the finding above is stated in utilization-ratio terms, not dollar terms. A Phase 2 mart exposing committed vs. overage billed amounts separately (rather than volumes) would let a future iteration state this as a revenue-mix split directly.

## TAM / ICP / opportunity-sizing model

- **Built**: `analytics/tam_icp_sizing.py`. Grain: one row per `company_id` in the `market_universe` population (123,200 companies, customers and non-customers alike). Source marts: `dim_market_universe`, `dim_accounts` (point-in-time customer capture and current segment), `fact_opportunities` (the realized new-business ACV every dollar figure in this artifact is grounded in). No dbt model was added or changed for this artifact, and no Phase 1 generator or raw table was added — `dim_market_universe` and `fact_opportunities` both already exist as real generated/modeled data.
- **What it is**: build spec item #22 (Wave 5). Build spec Section 5, on `market_universe`: "This single table is what makes TAM/ICP sizing and territory whitespace/allocation genuinely computed rather than hand-mocked." This artifact is the #22 half of that sentence; #21 (territory / coverage & routing) is a separate, not-yet-built sibling artifact and is not duplicated or pre-empted here. **A `territory` column now exists on `dim_market_universe`** (added concurrently, for #21's benefit, while this artifact was being built) — deliberately not used anywhere in this module. TAM/ICP sizing is scoped to segment-fit / ICP tier / region / industry / employee-band decomposition, per the build spec's own item #22 wording; territory-level allocation is #21's question to answer, not this one's.
- **Relationship to the metric tree — deliberately outside it, confirmed not assumed**: `acme-corp-gtm-metric-tree.md` was grepped at build time for `TAM`/`SAM`/`ICP` — no match anywhere. This is expected, not a gap: the tree is the weekly-operating-cadence scorecard (Growth/Efficiency/Durability, computed every week against `gtm_plan_targets`), and the build spec's own Wave 5 description places TAM/ICP outside that cadence entirely ("strategic/market-facing, runs off `market_universe`, not part of weekly cadence"). This artifact reports nothing against any tree node, computes no tree metric, and nothing here should be read as a competing or shadow definition of one.
- **Shape decision — structural/logic artifact, not a fitted model**: every number here is a direct aggregation over already-materialized mart rows, or a deterministic recomputation of an existing deterministic function (`fit_tier`), plus one stated dollar assumption grounded in real realized deal data. Nothing is trained; there is no train/test split, no estimated parameter, no probability. Per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' **"Structural/logic artifacts"** category — the same reasoning the Segment migration, Variance-diagnostic engine, Capacity planning and Marketing attribution entries use — **there is no coefficient table, no R²/RMSE, no AUC, no confusion matrix, no calibration note here, and their absence is deliberate, not pending.**
- **ICP tiering — reuses, does not redefine, the existing fit score**. "Tier 1" / "Tier 2" / "Tier 3" are new market-sizing-facing names for the exact same partition `generators/firmographics.py`'s `fit_tier()` already computes for every `market_universe` row and already uses to decide real segment entry (build spec Section 1 and Section 5: `icp_fit_score` is "computed with the same logic as `lead_scoring_history`... applied to the whole universe," and that same score decides entry). **Tier 1 = Enterprise-fit** (`icp_fit_score >= 72`), **Tier 2 = Commercial-fit** (`40 <= icp_fit_score < 72`), **Tier 3 = SMB-fit** (`icp_fit_score < 40`, or the `is_personal_email_domain` hard override to SMB regardless of score). 72 and 40 are **mirrored, not re-derived**, from `generators/firmographics.py`'s `ENTERPRISE_FIT_THRESHOLD`/`COMMERCIAL_FIT_THRESHOLD` constants — the exact cut points already deciding real segment entry in this data, not a new percentile-based split invented for this artifact (this is the resolution to the build spec instructions' explicit warning against "inventing a second, different ICP methodology"). No tier-naming precedent existed anywhere else in the project's docs before this (checked: no "Tier 1/2/3" ICP language in any `docs/` file or dbt model at build time — the only prior "Tier" hits in the repo are a `data_quality_governance.py` test's deliberately-injected forbidden-string violation, unrelated). `dim_market_universe` drops the generation-time `fit_tier` helper column before materializing (`generators/market_universe.py`'s own comment: "dropped before CSV write"), so `icp_tier`/`fit_segment` are **recomputed here** from `icp_fit_score` + `is_personal_email_domain` — a pure, deterministic function with no randomness, confirmed reproducible against the generator's own logic by the entry-tier correspondence check below.
- **Model type selection and rationale (why not a fitted model)**: a whitespace-conversion-propensity classifier (predicting which non-customer companies are most likely to convert) was the credible alternative and was rejected on the same grounds the Segment migration and Marketing attribution entries reject their own analogous classifiers. `generators/accounts.py`'s `_pick_from_tier()` is the actual mechanism deciding which `market_universe` companies become customers — sampled from within-tier pools, weighted mildly toward higher `icp_fit_score` within the tier. A classifier trained on firmographic features to "predict" conversion would mostly be re-deriving that generator's own sampling rule, the same trivial-accuracy failure mode the Account health score's calibration note documents for scoring inside a deterministic decline window. The tier scheme this artifact uses instead is the generator's own real selection mechanism, made explicit and applied to prioritize whitespace, not a fitted approximation of it.
- **Inputs**, all read from the marts layer:
  - `dim_market_universe` (`company_id`, `employee_count_band`, `industry`, `region`, `is_personal_email_domain`, `icp_fit_score`, `account_id`) — the population.
  - `dim_accounts` (`account_id`, `segment`, `signup_date`, `icp_fit_score`, `is_personal_email_domain`) — point-in-time customer capture (`signup_date`) and current segment, for the entry-tier correspondence check.
  - `fact_opportunities` (`company_id`, `segment`, `opportunity_type`, `is_won`, `amount`, `close_date`) — closed-won `new_business` deals only, the source of every ACV assumption in this artifact.
- **Point-in-time design, stated explicitly rather than assumed**: `market_universe`'s firmographic population is a **static snapshot** — `generators/market_universe.py` builds it once, with no date/month column and no rescoring mechanism that ever touches it afterward. Confirmed at build time, not assumed: `dim_accounts.icp_fit_score` matches `dim_market_universe.icp_fit_score` exactly for all 7,700 real customers, zero drift. So `icp_fit_score`/`icp_tier` carry **no leakage risk regardless of `as_of_date`** — they do not change over the simulation window. What genuinely IS time-varying is which companies have been captured as customers: every function takes `as_of_date` and derives `is_customer_as_of` from `dim_accounts.signup_date <= as_of_date` (not from `dim_market_universe.is_customer`, which reflects the full-history end state and would leak a company's future signup into an earlier snapshot). The realized-ACV assumption is likewise computed only from `fact_opportunities` deals with `close_date <= as_of_date`, and the entry-tier correspondence check is restricted to `dim_accounts.signup_date <= as_of_date` — no later checkpoint's data informs an earlier one anywhere in this module.
- **"Ever captured" convention, inherited from `mart_tam_whitespace`, not invented here**: `dim_market_universe.is_customer` never flips back to `False` on churn (confirmed: `was_ever_customer == is_customer` for all 123,200 rows at build time — 1,837 of the 7,700 real customers have since churned per `dim_accounts.customer_status`, and all 1,837 still read `is_customer = true`). This module's `is_customer_as_of` follows the same convention (captured, not currently-active), consistent with `mart_tam_whitespace`'s own `penetration_rate` semantics rather than a competing definition. Win-back opportunity sizing for churned accounts is out of scope (see Known limitations).
- **TAM/SAM finding — SAM equals TAM at the company-count level, confirmed against the data rather than assumed or forced into an artificial funnel**: Acme's three-segment motion (SMB self-serve / Commercial ISR / Enterprise AE) covers the entire firmographic space by design — personal-email-domain and low-fit companies default to SMB rather than being excluded, so nothing in `market_universe` is structurally unaddressable. Checked directly, per dimension marginally: **every one of the 7 employee-count bands, all 10 industries, and all 4 regions has at least one real customer** in the full-history data (smallest cell: LATAM Tier 2, 9 customers of 995 companies) — this is a marginal coverage check, not a joint one; the finer three-way `employee_band × industry × region` cross-product has plenty of individually-empty cells at only ~6.25% overall penetration, expected sampling variance rather than a structural exclusion, and not what this finding claims. A narrower SAM would need an explicit "out of ICP, unserviceable" bucket this data model does not have; forcing one in would misstate what was actually built. The meaningful decomposition axis is therefore **tier and current capture status, not a further TAM→SAM exclusion funnel.**
- **ACV assumption, the dollar-value basis for every whitespace estimate — grounded in realized deals, not invented**: median realized `fact_opportunities.amount` for closed-won `new_business` deals, by segment, `close_date <= as_of_date`. **At `as_of_date` 2025-12-31: SMB \$1,793 (n=6,500), Commercial \$15,000 (n=365), Enterprise \$237,670 (n=113).** Median, not mean, is the adopted assumption — Enterprise's realized ACV is right-skewed (build spec's own \$75K–\$750K+ range, open-ended at the top; observed mean \$256,122 vs. median \$237,670), and a mean would let a handful of the largest deals set the per-company assumption applied to thousands of whitespace companies. Each tier's assumption is assigned to whitespace companies in that same tier (Tier 1 → Enterprise ACV, Tier 2 → Commercial ACV, Tier 3 → SMB ACV) — the tier a company would enter at is the only grounded signal available for a company that has never transacted.
- **TAM/whitespace result, `as_of_date` 2025-12-31** (123,200 companies total, static across checkpoints; only capture status varies):

  | icp_tier | fit_segment | total_companies | customers_as_of | whitespace_as_of | penetration_rate | assumed_acv | whitespace_dollar_full_capture |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Tier 1 | Enterprise | 2,164 | 115 | 2,049 | 5.31% | \$237,670 | \$487.0M |
  | Tier 2 | Commercial | 26,006 | 376 | 25,630 | 1.45% | \$15,000 | \$384.5M |
  | Tier 3 | SMB | 95,030 | 7,209 | 87,821 | 7.59% | \$1,793 | \$157.5M |
  | **All** | **All** | **123,200** | **7,700** | **115,500** | **6.25%** | — | **\$1,028.9M** |

  **TAM (full-capture ceiling): \$1,074.8M** (whitespace \$1,028.9M + already-captured \$45.9M at their tier's assumed ACV — not the customers' actual realized/current ARR, which this figure deliberately does not attempt to restate). This is a full-capture ceiling, not a probability-weighted forecast: every company is valued at its tier's typical deal size with **no conversion-rate or win-rate discount applied**. **No SOM (serviceable *obtainable* market) is computed** — that would need an assumed cold-outreach conversion rate, and unlike this project's realized win rates (which apply to already-engaged, qualified pipeline), no real observed data grounds a conversion rate from cold TAM to closed deal; inventing one would be exactly the kind of unsourced number this project's conventions exist to avoid. Tier 2's low penetration (1.45%, the lowest of the three) is a genuine finding, not an artifact — Commercial-fit companies are both the largest whitespace-dollar pool after Tier 1 (\$384.5M) and the least penetrated relative to their population.
  - **Second checkpoint, `as_of_date` 2025-06-30** (confirming the shape is stable, not a single-snapshot read): 6,246 customers captured / 116,954 whitespace (vs. 7,700/115,500 six months later — capture grew as expected); ACV assumption SMB \$1,785 (n=5,166), Commercial \$15,000 (n=294), Enterprise \$245,263 (n=90); TAM \$1,090.5M, whitespace \$1,053.0M.
- **By-dimension breakdowns** (`compute_tam_by_dimension`, region / industry / employee_count_band × icp_tier) and **top-N whitespace prioritization** (`compute_top_whitespace_accounts`) are also built — grain one row per (dimension value, tier) and one row per company respectively, both reconciling exactly to the by-tier table above by construction (same underlying partition, different group-by). Top-whitespace ranking rule is **not invented for this artifact**: `generators/accounts.py`'s own `_pick_from_tier()` already treats "higher `icp_fit_score` within the same tier" as the better-fit signal when selecting real customers ("weights mildly toward higher icp_fit_score within the tier — better fits convert more readily"); ranking whitespace the same way (tier first, within-tier score second) is consistent with that established methodology, not a new ranking scheme. At 2025-12-31 the top whitespace account is `CO-0037297` (Tier 1, EMEA, Healthcare, 20000+ employees, `icp_fit_score` 100.0, assumed ACV \$237,670); the full top-50 list spans all four regions.
- **Full tables not logged to `fact_model_performance_history`**: the by-tier, by-region, by-industry, by-employee-band and top-50-whitespace tables are variable-width and are recorded as structured detail in this entry (above) rather than forced into that log's flat scalar grain, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' Persistence note.

### Validation — structural tie-outs and non-vacuousness checks, not statistics

In place of an accuracy/error target (none applies — see the shape decision), four checks in `run_build_time_validation()`. Two are exact structural invariants; two confirm the tier scheme this artifact is built on actually differentiates something real in the data, rather than being an assumption the model quietly assumes into existence. **All 4 pass at both checkpoints.**

| Check | What it pins down | Floor / ceiling | Result (2025-12-31) | Result (2025-06-30) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `universe_partition_reconciles` | `total_companies == customers_as_of + whitespace_as_of` exactly, overall and within every (icp_tier, region) cross-section — no company double-counted, none dropped | 0 max abs diff (companies) | **0** | **0** |
| `tam_dollar_sum_reconciles` | `tam_dollar_full_capture == customer_dollar_full_capture + whitespace_dollar_full_capture` exactly — a straight sum split by one boolean | ≤ \$0.01 max abs diff | **\$0.00** (2.4e-7 float noise) | **\$0.00** |
| `tier_acv_separation_non_vacuous` | Does `icp_tier` actually separate real, realized dollar value among real customers (joins `fact_opportunities` to `market_universe` directly, groups by the same recomputed tier used everywhere else) — the grounding for applying a tier's ACV to whitespace in that tier | median ratio ≥ 3.0x between adjacent tiers | **Tier1/Tier2 15.8x, Tier2/Tier3 8.4x** | **Tier1/Tier2 16.2x, Tier2/Tier3 8.4x** |
| `entry_tier_correspondence_non_vacuous` | Among real customers (`signup_date <= as_of_date`), does the recomputed tier's implied segment correspond to the account's actual current segment? Classified exact-match / upward-migration-explained (current segment more advanced than the tier implies — expected under the no-downgrade-path invariant) / downgrade-anomaly (current segment less advanced — should not occur) | explained share ≥ 98%, anomaly share ≤ 1% | **explained 99.84%, anomaly 0.16%** (6,931 exact + 757 upward-explained + 12 anomaly of 7,700) | **explained 99.87%, anomaly 0.13%** (5,510 + 728 + 8 of 6,246) |

- **All 12 (2025-12-31) / 8 (2025-06-30) downgrade-anomaly rows sit exactly at the 40.0/72.0 threshold boundary** (`all_downgrade_anomalies_at_rounding_boundary: True` both checkpoints) — a `np.round(score, 1)` rounding-up artifact in `generators/market_universe.py`: `fit_tier()` is computed on the raw unrounded score at generation time, `icp_fit_score` is stored rounded to one decimal, so a raw score of e.g. 71.96 is stored as 72.0 and reads as Enterprise-fit here while having actually been assigned to the Commercial pool at generation time. A benign floating-point boundary effect, not a data defect — confirmed directly, not inferred, and reported plainly rather than silently excluded from the anomaly count.
- **Honest limitation, reported rather than suppressed — `describe_within_tier_score_vs_later_migration`**: among accounts that entered as SMB (Tier 3 at entry), does `icp_fit_score` predict later upward migration (SMB→Commercial)? **No** — migration rate is flat at ~10% across all four within-tier score quartiles (10.0%, 10.1%, 10.0%, 9.8% at 2025-12-31, n≈1,800 per quartile). This is the correct, expected result, not a defect: migration is governed by usage/spend crossing a threshold (build spec Section 1), a mechanism genuinely independent of the firmographic fit score that only ever governed entry. Stated explicitly so the tier scheme's real scope is clear: it is grounded to predict entry-appropriate **deal size** (confirmed above), not **who will organically grow past their entry tier** — a different question this artifact does not claim to answer.
- **Sample sizes**: 123,200 companies in `market_universe` (7,700 ever-customers at full history, 115,500 non-customers — the generator's 15x `NON_CUSTOMER_MULTIPLE`); realized-ACV grounding sample at 2025-12-31: 6,500 SMB / 365 Commercial / 113 Enterprise closed-won new-business deals; entry-tier correspondence sample: 7,700 customers (6,246 at the 2025-06-30 checkpoint, respecting `signup_date <= as_of_date`).

### Target and drift threshold

- **Target**: no accuracy/error target applies (structural/logic artifact, per the shape decision above). The four checks above stand in its place, and the two exact tie-outs are stated as **hard invariants with a floating-point tolerance rather than a band** — deterministic arithmetic has no sampling variance, so a wider tolerance would imply an error source that does not exist, the same reasoning Capacity planning's and the Weekly readout's exact-tie-out entries give.
- **Non-vacuousness floors — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed**: `_MIN_TIER_ACV_SEPARATION_RATIO = 3.0x`, `_MIN_ENTRY_TIER_EXPLAINED_SHARE = 0.98`, `_MAX_DOWNGRADE_ANOMALY_SHARE = 0.01`. Nothing upstream (build spec, QA plan, metric tree) states numbers for any of these, so they are this artifact's own proposal at build time, in the same spirit as the Segment migration entry's 10% firmographic-rescore floor and Capacity planning's 15% achievability margin. Each is set with deliberate headroom below/above the observed 2025-12-31 reading (3.0x floor vs. 8.4–15.8x observed; 98% floor vs. 99.84% observed; 1% ceiling vs. 0.16% observed, itself fully explained by a known rounding artifact) — wide enough that ordinary period-to-period variation across future checkpoints will not spuriously trip either check, while still being far short of a value that would pass on a genuinely broken tier scheme (a ratio near 1.0x, or an explained share near the no-signal baseline, would fail both comfortably).
- **Drift review trigger**: per the doc's general rule, 2 consecutive monthly checkpoints failing any of the four checks → flagged for review. The two exact tie-outs are expected to never breach (structural invariants — a breach is a defect to fix, not a trend to watch, since `icp_fit_score` and `icp_tier` are static and cannot drift). The two non-vacuousness floors are worth watching in `fact_model_performance_history` (`tier_acv_separation_ratio_tier1_tier2`/`_tier2_tier3`, `entry_tier_explained_share`, `entry_tier_downgrade_anomaly_share`) mainly as a **generator-integrity** check — since `icp_fit_score` is static, these should be near-constant over time and a real drift would point to a generator or mart change, not to model staleness in the usual sense.
- **Persistence**: scalar time-series metrics logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `log_performance()` — `total_companies`, `total_customers_as_of`, `total_whitespace_as_of`, `tam_dollar_full_capture`, `whitespace_dollar_full_capture`, `whitespace_companies_tier_{1,2,3}`, `whitespace_dollar_tier_{1,2,3}`, `penetration_rate_tier_{1,2,3}`, `acv_assumption_median_{smb,commercial,enterprise}`, `tier_acv_separation_ratio_tier1_tier2`/`_tier2_tier3`, `entry_tier_explained_share`, `entry_tier_downgrade_anomaly_share`, `universe_partition_max_abs_diff_companies`, `tam_dollar_reconciliation_max_abs_diff_usd`, `structural_checks_total`/`_passed`. The variable-width tables (by-tier, by-region, by-industry, by-employee-band, top-50-whitespace) do not fit that log's flat scalar grain and are recorded as structured detail in this entry instead.

### Known limitations

- **TAM/whitespace dollar figures are a full-capture ceiling, not a forecast.** No win-rate or conversion-rate discount is applied to whitespace companies — the \$1,028.9M whitespace figure is "if every whitespace company converted at its tier's typical deal size," not a probability-weighted estimate of what will actually close. Deliberately not built (see the ACV-assumption bullet above): no real observed data in this project grounds a cold-outreach conversion rate the way realized win rates ground already-engaged pipeline.
- **"Ever captured" churned accounts are not re-opened as whitespace.** 1,837 of the 7,700 ever-customers have since churned per `dim_accounts.customer_status`, and this module (following `mart_tam_whitespace`'s own convention) still counts them as captured, not as whitespace. Win-back sizing for the churned population is a distinct question this artifact does not attempt.
- **`icp_fit_score`/`icp_tier` predict entry-appropriate deal size, not organic upward-migration propensity** — see the within-tier-score-vs-migration finding above. A future artifact wanting to prioritize whitespace by "likely to grow fast once captured" would need a different, migration-grounded signal; this one does not claim to provide it.
- **Territory is not used**, though a `territory` column now exists on `dim_market_universe` (added concurrently for build spec item #21's benefit). TAM/ICP sizing is scoped to tier/region/industry/employee-band per the build spec's own item #22 wording; territory-level allocation and whitespace routing belongs to #21, a separate, not-yet-built sibling artifact, and is not pre-empted here.
- **No SAM narrower than TAM at the company-count level** (see the TAM/SAM finding above) is a genuine property of this data model (Acme's PLG entry covers the full firmographic space by design), not a shortcut taken to avoid building a real SAM funnel — but it does mean this artifact's SAM concept carries less information than a company with an explicit "out of ICP" exclusion tier would have. If a future generator change introduces a genuinely unaddressable firmographic segment (e.g., a minimum-viable-company-size floor below which even SMB self-serve doesn't apply), this finding would need re-checking, not assumed to still hold.

## Territory / account coverage & routing

- **Built**: `analytics/territory_coverage.py`. Grain: one row per territory (`NA-East` / `NA-West` / `EMEA` / `APAC` / `LATAM`) for the coverage-ratio and rebalancing tables; supplementary one row per territory × `rep_type` for headcount detail, one row per territory for the open-pipeline lens. Source marts: `mart_tam_whitespace` (whitespace/company-population by territory), `dim_reps` (rep headcount, point-in-time), `dim_accounts` (existing customer-account population, point-in-time), `fact_opportunities` + `dim_market_universe` (open new-business pipeline by territory, point-in-time, supplementary lens only). No dbt model was added or changed for this artifact, and no Phase 1 generator or raw table was added — the territory dimension (`generators/territories.py`, `dim_reps.territory`/`dim_accounts.territory`/`dim_market_universe.territory`, `mart_tam_whitespace`'s territory grain) is a completed, already-shipped Phase 1/2 extension (420/420 dbt checks, 296/296 QA tests passing at the time this module was built) and is read here, not modified.
- **What it is**: build spec item #21 (Wave 5), "Territory / account coverage & routing, including whitespace and TAM-allocation." The distinction it exists to make: is rep headcount, territory by territory, actually proportionate to where the opportunity (and the existing book) sits — not just headcount counted, but headcount checked against a real denominator.
- **Boundary with `analytics/tam_icp_sizing.py` (item #22)** — stated rather than assumed, per that module's own entry above ("Territory is not used... territory-level allocation and whitespace routing belongs to #21"). This module does not recompute TAM dollars, does not re-derive the ICP fit-tier/segment mapping, and does not re-run tam_icp_sizing's median-realized-ACV assumption. Whitespace-company **counts** (not dollars) are pulled from `mart_tam_whitespace` as-is. A dollar-denominated coverage ratio ("high-ICP-whitespace-dollars-per-rep") would need tam_icp_sizing's ACV-by-segment assumption; deliberately not re-derived here, to avoid a second, silently-divergent dollar figure — whitespace-company-**count**-per-rep is used instead, itself a real metric a RevOps team would use and one that needs no dollar assumption.
- **Shape decision — structural/logic artifact, not a fitted model**: every number is a direct aggregation of already-materialized mart rows; a coverage ratio is a straight division, not an estimate. Per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' **"Structural/logic artifacts"** category — same reasoning as the Variance-diagnostic engine, Capacity planning, Segment migration and TAM/ICP entries — **there is no coefficient table, no AUC, no confusion matrix, no R²/RMSE here, and their absence is deliberate, not pending.**
- **Model type selection and rationale (why not a fitted model)**: a predictive "coverage-risk" classifier (estimating which territory is likeliest to breach a future coverage bar) was the credible alternative and was rejected on two grounds. (1) **No trailing history to fit against** — territory is a single current snapshot dimension (rep-to-territory and company-to-territory assignment), not a time series of past coverage levels a model could learn from; it was only just added to the schema. (2) **The quantity a routing decision needs is a direct ratio** ("is headcount proportionate to opportunity, right now"), not a probability — the same "a planner needs a sum/ratio, not a noisy estimate of one" argument Capacity planning's and TAM/ICP's entries make for their own structural shape.
- **Point-in-time design — verified against the data, not assumed, per the build brief's own instruction**:
  - **Rep-to-territory headcount IS genuinely time-varying.** Reps are hired, ramp, and depart over the window — confirmed at build time: active ISR headcount in EMEA is 4 at `as_of_date` 2025-06-30 vs. 3 at 2025-12-31 (one departure). `load_rep_headcount_by_territory()` does a genuine point-in-time lookup against `dim_reps`' capacity-period grain (`period_start_date <= as_of_date <= period_end_date`, or an open-ended current period, `rep_status = 'active'`) — mirroring `analytics/capacity_planning.py`'s own `dim_reps` point-in-time convention, **not** `dim_reps.is_current_period`, which only ever answers "as of now."
  - **Existing-account population IS genuinely time-varying.** Accounts sign up throughout the window — confirmed at build time: 1,454 of 7,700 total accounts (18.9%) signed up in 2025 H2 alone. `load_existing_accounts_by_territory()` filters `signup_date <= as_of_date`, following the same **"ever captured"** convention `mart_tam_whitespace` and `tam_icp_sizing.py` already use (a company that later churns stays counted as captured, not reverted to whitespace or dropped) — consistent with, not competing against, that established semantics. `customer_status` (active/churned) is deliberately **not** used to filter the point-in-time count, since it reflects the account's *latest* status and would leak a later churn event into an earlier checkpoint; churned share is reported for context only (`captured_accounts_now_churned`), computed from that same latest-status field, and is explicitly not point-in-time.
  - **The whitespace/company-population side (`mart_tam_whitespace`) is NOT point-in-time** — it is a static full-history-end-state snapshot, the same limitation the TAM/ICP entry documents for `dim_market_universe.is_customer`. This module does not re-derive a point-in-time-correct whitespace partition the way `tam_icp_sizing.load_universe_snapshot()` does for its own tier-based cut — doing so a second time, reading the same underlying `dim_market_universe` rows through a second, separately-maintained point-in-time implementation, would risk a silently-diverging copy rather than adding real value. The distortion this introduces at the earlier (2025-06-30) checkpoint is **quantified, not ignored**: of 7,700 total accounts, 1,454 (18.9%) signed up in 2025 H2 alone, but against `mart_tam_whitespace`'s ~115,500-company whitespace pool that is a bounded **~1.3% overstatement** of "already captured," not a distortion large enough to change which territory reads as most/least covered (confirmed below — the finding is identical in direction and magnitude at both checkpoints). Every result sourced from `mart_tam_whitespace` is labelled accordingly rather than silently presented as if it were point-in-time.
- **No stochastic step lives in this module** (no train/test split, no sampling, no simulation) — every function is a direct, deterministic aggregation over already-generated mart data, so no random seed applies, matching `analytics/segment_migration.py`'s, `analytics/capacity_planning.py`'s and `analytics/tam_icp_sizing.py`'s precedent.
- **Inputs**, all read from the marts layer:
  - `dim_reps` (`rep_id`, `rep_type`, `territory`, `period_start_date`, `period_end_date`, `rep_status`) — active ISR/AE headcount by territory, point-in-time.
  - `mart_tam_whitespace` (`territory`, `total_companies`, `total_customers`, `whitespace_companies`, `high_icp_whitespace_count`) — summed across its industry × employee-band grain up to territory.
  - `dim_accounts` (`territory`, `signup_date`, `customer_status`) — existing customer-account population, point-in-time.
  - `fact_opportunities` (`company_id`, `opportunity_type`, `created_date`, `close_date`, `amount`) joined to `dim_market_universe` (`company_id`, `territory`) — supplementary open new-business pipeline lens, point-in-time.

### Coverage-ratio results, `as_of_date` 2025-12-31

The core diagnostic is `coverage_index` = territory's share of active ISR/AE headcount ÷ territory's share of a given denominator (whitespace companies, high-ICP whitespace companies, or captured accounts). `< 1.0` = under-resourced relative to that share of opportunity; `> 1.0` = over-resourced. This is deliberately the *same kind* of ratio `generators/territories.py`'s own docstring reports (rep-share ÷ company-population-share, APAC ≈0.60, LATAM ≈1.40) but computed against real opportunity/book denominators a RevOps team would actually use, rather than raw company population — per the build brief's instruction to confirm empirically rather than assume the design doc's stated figure is what a real coverage metric would show.

| territory | active ISR | active AE | active reps | whitespace companies | whitespace/rep | **coverage_index (whitespace)** | high-ICP whitespace | **coverage_index (high-ICP)** | captured accounts | accounts/rep | **coverage_index (existing accounts)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **APAC** | 2 | 1 | 3 | 17,297 | 5,766 | **0.514** | 322 | **0.609** | 1,136 | 379 | **0.521** |
| EMEA | 3 | 4 | 7 | 28,790 | 4,113 | 0.720 | 555 | 0.824 | 1,893 | 270 | 0.730 |
| NA-West | 5 | 6 | 11 | 27,977 | 2,543 | 1.164 | 685 | 1.050 | 1,855 | 169 | 1.171 |
| NA-East | 7 | 8 | 15 | 35,690 | 2,379 | 1.245 | 915 | 1.071 | 2,464 | 164 | 1.202 |
| **LATAM** | 1 | 2 | 3 | 5,746 | 1,915 | **1.546** | 72 | **2.723** | 352 | 117 | **1.683** |

**APAC is confirmed the most under-resourced territory on every one of the three denominators tried, and by a wider margin than the design doc's own stated company-population-share ratio (0.60)** — 0.514 on raw whitespace-company share, 0.609 on high-ICP whitespace-company share, 0.521 on existing-account share. **LATAM is confirmed the most over-resourced (floor-staffed) territory on all three, also by a wider margin than the design doc's stated 1.40** — 1.546 / 2.723 / 1.683. The three lenses agree in direction and rough magnitude, which is itself informative: **APAC's thinness is not narrowly a new-business-prospecting problem — its headcount is thin relative to its entire company footprint, existing book and whitespace alike** (existing-account coverage_index 0.521 is essentially the same reading as whitespace coverage_index 0.514). This is a genuine finding verified against the data, not an assumption carried over from the design doc — the brief's own caveat that the two ratios "may tell a related but not identical story" turned out, on this data, to tell the *same* story on both axes, which is itself worth stating plainly rather than forcing a distinction the data doesn't support.

**Second checkpoint, `as_of_date` 2025-06-30** (confirms the finding is not tuned to one snapshot): active headcount is nearly identical (APAC 3, EMEA 8, NA-West 11, NA-East 15, LATAM 3 — one fewer EMEA ISR departed between the checkpoints); `coverage_index_whitespace` APAC 0.501, EMEA 0.802, NA-West 1.135, NA-East 1.214, LATAM 1.508; `coverage_index_existing_accounts` APAC 0.508, EMEA 0.805, NA-West 1.139, NA-East 1.179, LATAM 1.685. Same ranking, same magnitude, both checkpoints.

**Supplementary lens — open new-business pipeline by territory** (`load_open_pipeline_by_territory`, point-in-time by construction: `created_date <= as_of_date < close_date`). At `as_of_date` 2025-12-31 this is **empty by construction** for every territory — this simulation's data is fully closed out through 2025-12-28 (0 open deals company-wide at that checkpoint), the same "open population naturally empty at a full-history-end checkpoint" shape `analytics/deal_diagnostics.py`'s `open_population()` already documents and relies on, not a defect in this loader. At the 2025-06-30 checkpoint, where real open pipeline exists: APAC 14 deals / \$1.89M, EMEA 23 / \$2.68M, LATAM 4 / \$0.60M, NA-East 31 / \$4.59M, NA-West 19 / \$3.42M — directionally consistent with the headcount-driven reading (APAC and LATAM both carry the least open pipeline in absolute terms) but reported as a secondary, point-in-time-only lens, not folded into the `coverage_index` computation itself (open pipeline's point-in-time nature means it is not comparable across the two checkpoints the way the static-whitespace-based ratios are).

### Rebalancing — a structural "what would it take," not a recommendation

`compute_rebalancing_deltas()`: holding total active ISR/AE headcount fixed at 39 (2025-12-31), what headcount per territory would equalize `coverage_index_whitespace` to 1.0 everywhere (`ideal_headcount = whitespace_share × total_reps`)?

| territory | active reps | ideal headcount | delta (reps) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **APAC** | 3 | 5.84 | **+2.84** |
| EMEA | 7 | 9.72 | +2.72 |
| LATAM | 3 | 1.94 | −1.06 |
| NA-West | 11 | 9.45 | −1.55 |
| NA-East | 15 | 12.05 | −2.95 |

**Headline structural read (`summarize_apac_rebalancing`)**: APAC would need **+3 reps** (2.84 rounded up — a fractional rep cannot be hired) to reach parity with the company-wide whitespace-per-rep average. Ranked donor territories by surplus: NA-East (2.95), NA-West (1.55), LATAM (1.06). **This is a structural equalization target, explicitly not a recommendation** — it cannot and does not account for cost, rep willingness to relocate, ramp-up time for a moved rep (`analytics/capacity_planning.py`'s own ~180-day ramp-to-full-productivity rule would apply to any newly-placed rep, new hire or transfer alike, and is not modeled here), or the minimum-viable-staffing floor `generators/territories.py`'s own docstring states LATAM is deliberately held above (five territories need a floor of reps to function at all — a literal reading of the delta table would draw 1.06 reps *out* of LATAM, which the design's own stated rationale argues against). ISR and AE headcount are also treated as fungible "new-business coverage capacity" in this calculation, since `mart_tam_whitespace` does not split whitespace-company counts by ICP fit-segment/role at the territory grain — a role-specific version (Commercial-fit whitespace ÷ ISR headcount, Enterprise-fit whitespace ÷ AE headcount) is a natural extension using `dim_market_universe.icp_fit_score` directly, not built here (see Known limitations).

### Validation — structural tie-outs and a non-vacuousness check, not statistics

In place of an accuracy/error target (none applies — see the shape decision), five checks in `run_build_time_validation()`. Four are exact structural invariants; one confirms this artifact actually detects the real, deliberately-injected APAC/LATAM imbalance rather than being asserted to. **All 5 pass at both checkpoints.**

| Check | What it pins down | Floor / tolerance | Result (2025-12-31) | Result (2025-06-30) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `headcount_partition_reconciles` | The per-(territory, rep_type) headcount table sums to exactly a direct, un-grouped count of active ISR/AE reps with a non-null territory as of `as_of_date` — no rep double-counted (overlapping capacity periods) or dropped (an off-by-one on a period boundary) | 0 max abs diff | **0** (39 = 39) | **0** (40 = 40) |
| `whitespace_partition_reconciles` | The per-territory whitespace rollup sums to exactly `mart_tam_whitespace`'s own grand total — no territory dropped or double-counted in the group-by | 0 max abs diff | **0** (115,500 = 115,500) | **0** (115,500 = 115,500, static) |
| `account_partition_reconciles` | The per-territory captured-account rollup sums to exactly a direct, un-grouped count of accounts with `signup_date <= as_of_date` — every `dim_accounts` row carries a non-null territory by construction, so a diff would mean a join gap, not a legitimate null | 0 max abs diff | **0** (7,700 = 7,700) | **0** (6,246 = 6,246) |
| `rebalancing_conserves_headcount` | `sum(ideal_headcount)` across all 5 territories equals `sum(active_reps_total)` exactly — the equalization calculation redistributes existing headcount, it does not fabricate or lose reps | ≤ 1e-9 max abs diff | **0.00e+00** | **0.00e+00** |
| `apac_underresourcing_non_vacuous` | **The strongest correctness check here, per the build brief**: does this artifact's own independently-computed `coverage_index_whitespace` (a) rank APAC as the single most under-resourced territory, (b) below a stated floor, (c) rank LATAM as the single most over-resourced, and (d) above a stated floor — confirming the real, deliberately-injected imbalance rather than assuming the design doc's stated figure would show up in a real metric | APAC min & ≤ 0.75; LATAM max & ≥ 1.25 | **Pass** — min=APAC (0.514), max=LATAM (1.546) | **Pass** — min=APAC (0.501), max=LATAM (1.508) |

**Non-vacuousness floors — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed.** `_MAX_UNDER_RESOURCED_COVERAGE_INDEX = 0.75`, `_MIN_OVER_RESOURCED_COVERAGE_INDEX = 1.25`. Nothing upstream (build spec, QA plan, metric tree) states numbers for either, so these are this artifact's own proposal at build time, in the same spirit as TAM/ICP's tier-separation floor and Capacity planning's achievability margin. Both are set with deliberate headroom against the observed 2025-12-31 readings (0.75 ceiling vs. 0.514/0.609/0.521 observed for APAC; 1.25 floor vs. 1.546/2.723/1.683 observed for LATAM) — wide enough that ordinary hiring/attrition movement across future checkpoints will not spuriously trip either side, while still being far short of a value a genuinely balanced roster (all indices near 1.0) would pass.

**Sample sizes**: 39 active ISR/AE reps across 5 territories at 2025-12-31 (40 at 2025-06-30); 123,200-company `market_universe` population (115,500 whitespace, static across checkpoints); 7,700 captured accounts at 2025-12-31 (6,246 at 2025-06-30); 91 open new-business deals / \$13.18M at the 2025-06-30 open-pipeline checkpoint (0 at 2025-12-31, by construction).

### Target and drift threshold

- **Target**: no accuracy/error target applies (structural/logic artifact, per the shape decision above). The five checks above stand in its place; the four tie-outs are stated as **hard invariants with a floating-point tolerance rather than a band** — deterministic arithmetic has no sampling variance, so a wider tolerance would imply an error source that does not exist, the same reasoning Capacity planning's and TAM/ICP's exact-tie-out entries give.
- **Drift review trigger**: per the doc's general rule, 2 consecutive monthly checkpoints failing any of the five checks → flagged for review. The four tie-outs are expected to never breach (structural invariants — a breach is a defect to fix, not a trend to watch). The non-vacuousness check is worth watching in `fact_model_performance_history` (`coverage_index_whitespace_apac`/`_latam` and the other three territories) as a **roster-integrity** signal specifically: if APAC's index ever rose above the 0.75 floor (more reps hired there, or reps departing elsewhere without backfill), that would mean the deliberately-injected imbalance the generator built in has been organically corrected in later data and this check's floor would need loosening — a finding to report, not a defect to silently absorb.
- **Persistence**: scalar time-series metrics logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `log_performance()` — `active_reps_{territory}`, `whitespace_companies_per_rep_{territory}`, `coverage_index_whitespace_{territory}`, `coverage_index_existing_accounts_{territory}` (one set per territory), `apac_reps_needed_for_parity`, `structural_checks_total`/`_passed`. The variable-width tables (the full coverage-ratio table, the rebalancing-delta table, the open-pipeline supplementary lens) do not fit that log's flat scalar grain and are recorded as structured detail in this entry instead, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' Persistence note.

### Known limitations

- **The whitespace/company-population denominator is not point-in-time** (see "Point-in-time design" above) — `mart_tam_whitespace` reflects the full-history end state regardless of `as_of_date`. Quantified, bounded (~1.3% of the whitespace pool at the 2025-06-30 checkpoint), and confirmed not to change the ranking or the non-vacuousness check's pass/fail at either checkpoint tested — but a genuinely point-in-time-correct whitespace partition (mirroring `tam_icp_sizing.load_universe_snapshot()`) is not built here, to avoid a second, separately-maintained implementation of the same correctness-bearing computation.
- **Whitespace-company counts are not dollar-denominated.** Per the boundary with `tam_icp_sizing.py` above, this artifact deliberately does not compute "high-ICP-whitespace-dollars-per-rep" — only company counts. A dollar-weighted version is a natural extension that would need tam_icp_sizing's ACV-by-segment assumption, not re-derived here.
- **The rebalancing calculation is a structural equalization target, not a recommendation.** It does not and cannot account for cost, rep willingness to relocate, ramp-up time for a moved rep, or LATAM's deliberate minimum-viable-staffing floor (`generators/territories.py`'s own stated rationale) — stated explicitly in the Rebalancing section above, not silently assumed away.
- **ISR and AE headcount are treated as fungible "new-business coverage capacity"** in the rebalancing calculation, since `mart_tam_whitespace` does not split whitespace-company counts by ICP fit-segment/role at the territory grain. A role-specific version (Commercial-fit whitespace ÷ ISR headcount, Enterprise-fit whitespace ÷ AE headcount) would sharpen the read further and is a natural extension using `dim_market_universe.icp_fit_score` directly — not built here for scope/time reasons.
- **AM headcount and existing-book management capacity are out of scope**, for the same reason Capacity planning's entry names: AM reps carry no `territory` (they own a portfolio of specific existing accounts, not a geographic prospecting patch — `generators/territories.py`'s own docstring). The `captured_accounts_per_rep` / `coverage_index_existing_accounts` lens in this artifact measures existing-account *population* against ISR/AE headcount as a proxy for total territory footprint, not against the AM headcount that actually services that book — that capacity question already has a home in the metric tree's Onboarding/CS and AM-efficiency nodes (`mart_efficiency`), per Capacity planning's entry, and is not duplicated here.
- **The open-pipeline supplementary lens is not comparable across checkpoints** — it is naturally empty at the full-history-end checkpoint (2025-12-31) and populated only at an earlier `as_of_date`, the same shape `analytics/deal_diagnostics.py`'s open-population design already relies on. It is reported as descriptive detail only and does not feed the `coverage_index` computation or either validation check.

## MMM / incrementality-based measurement

- **Built**: `analytics/mmm_incrementality.py`. Grain: one row per (sub-channel, month) over the window `fact_marketing_spend` actually covers — 2023-01 through the last complete month at or before `as_of_date`. No dbt model was added or changed for this artifact, and no Phase 1 generator or raw table was added; it is built entirely on top of `dim_campaign`, `fact_leads`, `fact_campaign_engagement_events`, `fact_opportunities` and `dim_accounts` via `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s own loaders and functions, reused rather than re-derived.
- **What it is**: build spec item #12, Wave 6 — a media-mix regression estimating each sub-channel's spend-response curve, built to extend incrementality estimation to what the holdout method in the Marketing attribution & channel mix entry above cannot reach. That entry's "Model type selection and rationale" point 3 names this artifact by build-spec item number as the deferred consumer of the channel-grain spend and conversion series it supplies ("This artifact supplies the channel-grain spend and conversion series such a model would need, and does not pre-empt it") — this is that handoff landing. It also closes the one gap that entry states cannot be closed by its own method: organic's incremental share is reported there as `not estimable`, because `dim_campaign` carries `is_holdout = false` on every organic row by design (an organic/SEO program cannot be switched off for a chosen cell). A regression on the spend/conversion time series does not need a holdout, so it is the only route to an organic incrementality read at all in this data — see the headline finding below for how much confidence that read actually carries.
- **The build spec's own line-98 tension, taken at face value, not routed around**: Section 4 states directly — "MMM/incrementality and proxy-metric decay detection both want a long time series to be credible; 36 months monthly is workable but thin for either... flagging so it's a deliberate call when those two artifacts get built, not a surprise when they produce a weak result." This artifact is built against exactly that 36-month window with no unilateral extension of the simulation horizon (a cross-cutting Phase 1 change, out of scope for a single Phase 4 build). The result is weak in the places the line predicted, and that weakness is reported as the artifact's headline finding, not smoothed over — see "Cross-validation against the holdout read" and "Checkpoint instability" below.
- **Relationship to the metric tree**: this artifact's natural output — each sub-channel's incremental contribution to bookings — is the causal read of the tree's **Pipeline generated** Layer-2 branch (`Σ over channels (channel volume × channel-to-lead rate × lead-to-PQL rate)`), the same branch the Marketing attribution entry makes computable in correlational (attributed-credit) and, for paid/community, causal (holdout) form. This artifact adds a third lens — regression-estimated — for the same branch, and is explicitly not a replacement for either: the holdout remains the higher-confidence causal read where it exists (paid, community), and this artifact's marginal value is (a) the only incrementality read organic will ever get in this data and (b) a cross-check on whether a regression-based method would have reached the same conclusion the holdout did, had the holdout not existed.
- **Grain decision — fine (organic/paid/community), not coarse `dim_accounts.channel`**: two channel grains exist in this data. The fine grain is used, deliberately, for three reasons verified against the data rather than assumed: it is the only grain with real holdout data to cross-validate against (six suppressed cells, paid and community only, in the Marketing attribution entry); it is the grain the build spec's own line-98 flag is written against; and a coarse 3-channel model would conflate the one channel with a causal check available (paid, inside `inbound_marketing`) with channels that never will (`outbound_sdr`, `self_serve`). The coarse `fact_marketing_spend` mart itself is not read directly here — `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s `allocate_campaign_spend()` already reconciles campaign-level spend against it (`campaign_spend_reconciles_to_coarse_mart`, 0.331% over the full window), so that cross-check is inherited rather than re-derived.
- **Model shape — log-log, per-channel, with a trend control; NOT a full adstock/saturation MMM, verified against the data rather than assumed**:
  1. **Adstock/carryover — rejected.** Monthly spend is strongly autocorrelated within every channel (lag-1 correlation 0.78 community / 0.87 organic / 0.86 paid), so a fitted decay parameter would be estimated almost entirely from noise: contemporaneous and one-month-lagged spend carry nearly the same information at this frequency, and 36 monthly points per channel cannot separate them. A model including both would split credit unstably between two nearly-collinear regressors — worse than omitting the lag, not better.
  2. **Saturation — reduced to its lightest identifiable form.** A 2-parameter Hill/saturation curve needs curvature information a 36-point series at 3 channels does not reliably carry. A log-log (constant-elasticity) functional form is used instead: a genuine, if simpler, diminishing-returns structure (marginal conversions per marginal dollar fall as spend rises whenever the fitted elasticity is below 1.0, which it is for every channel here), needing one parameter per channel instead of two. It is also data-supported, not merely assumed: log-spend correlates with log-conversions better than raw spend for two of three channels (organic 0.68→0.72, paid 0.54→0.71; community is roughly flat, 0.40→0.41).
  3. **A pooled single elasticity across all three channels — rejected as the primary read, reported only as a secondary robustness check.** The three channels' per-channel elasticities (0.43 organic / 0.22 paid / 0.19 community at the 2025-12-31 checkpoint) are not obviously one number, and the quantity this artifact needs for the holdout cross-validation is channel-specific in the first place (the holdout's own measured shares are 0.6477 paid / 0.8157 community, not one pooled figure). `fit_pooled_elasticity_model()` is reported alongside because pooling triples the sample (n=108 vs. 36) and gives a materially tighter SE on an assumed-common elasticity, useful as a sensitivity check but not as the artifact's primary claim.
- **The trend confound — the single most important specification finding, found by checking the data rather than assumed away**: spend and conversions co-trend strongly across the window for reasons that have nothing to do with spend's own effect — company-wide monthly attributed conversions roughly double from 2023 to 2025, and every channel's own spend grows over the same window (month-index correlation with spend runs 0.55–0.87 across channels, with conversions 0.71–0.83). Fitting `log(conversions) ~ log(spend)` alone, with no trend control, gives a pooled elasticity of **0.730** — but that number is almost entirely the shared growth trend, not a spend effect: adding a linear month-index trend control drops the pooled elasticity to **0.225** and pushes it to only marginal significance (p=0.055 at 2025-12-31; the collinearity is worse at 2025-06-30 — see the second checkpoint below). **The answer flips depending on whether the trend is controlled for**, and the two are collinear enough (VIF on log-spend once the trend term is included: **7.42** at 2025-12-31, **13.24** at 2025-06-30 — the collinearity gets *worse*, not better, with less history) that this data cannot cleanly separate "spend caused it" from "the business was growing anyway." Every fitted model in this artifact includes the trend control; the no-trend variant is reported purely as the sensitivity check that surfaces this finding, not as a candidate specification.
- **Outcome variable — linear-attributed conversions, not bookings, and why**: build spec item #12 asks for "incremental contribution to bookings/pipeline," but the regression itself is fit on monthly **conversions** (linear-attributed converting leads, the same credit basis `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s own Layer-3 diagnostics use, reused via `compute_channel_mix(period_grain='month')`), translated to dollars afterward via each channel's own realized average booking value per conversion. Verified, not assumed: attributed monthly bookings are far lumpier than attributed monthly conversions in this data (coefficient of variation 1.0–1.5 for bookings vs. 0.35–0.49 for conversions across the three channels — a handful of large Enterprise deals dominate a given month's bookings almost independent of that month's lead volume), and log(spend)-to-log(bookings) correlation is markedly weaker than log(spend)-to-log(conversions) for every channel (organic 0.09 vs. 0.72, community 0.15 vs. 0.41, paid 0.46 vs. 0.71). Regressing bookings directly would mostly fit deal-size noise; conversions is the funnel-volume quantity spend plausibly drives, and `compute_incremental_bookings()` does the dollar translation afterward.
- **Inputs**, all read via `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s already-marts-scoped, already-validated functions rather than re-derived:
  - `allocate_campaign_spend()` — day-prorated `dim_campaign.budget` by sub-channel and month.
  - `attribute_credit()` + `compute_channel_mix(period_grain='month')` — linear-attributed monthly conversions and won new-logo bookings by sub-channel, itself built on that module's point-in-time lead panel and touch sequencing.
  - `measure_incrementality()` — the holdout-measured pooled incremental share per channel, read directly for the cross-validation step, never recomputed.
  - **Why reuse rather than re-derive**: recomputing touch sequencing, the point-in-time lead panel or attribution credit independently here would produce a second, divergent implementation of logic `analytics/marketing_attribution.py` already computes and validates (credit conservation, the pipeline-generated identity, the first-touch/lead-source tie-out) — there would be no basis to trust either implementation more than the other if they disagreed. This module contributes exactly one new computation: the regression itself.

### Per-channel elasticity models — the primary read, at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31 (n=36 months per channel)

| Channel | n | R² | Elasticity (β on log-spend) | SE | p | 95% CI | Trend coef. | Nested F-test p (spend adds value vs. trend-only) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Organic | 36 | 0.585 | **0.4297** | 0.2387 | **0.0810** | [−0.056, 0.915] | 0.0168 | 0.0810 |
| Paid | 36 | 0.713 | **0.2206** | 0.1273 | **0.0924** | [−0.038, 0.480] | 0.0302 | 0.0924 |
| Community | 36 | 0.423 | **0.1923** | 0.2751 | **0.4894** | [−0.367, 0.752] | 0.0352 | 0.4894 |

**Headline finding, stated here rather than left for the reader to find**: at n=36 per channel, no channel's spend coefficient clears the conventional 5% significance level. Organic (p=0.081) and paid (p=0.092) are marginal at 10%; community's is not distinguishable from zero (p=0.489) — the same underpowered-channel-level read `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s own holdout gives community (z=1.95, resting on one control conversion) for a different reason (there, too few control conversions; here, too few independent months of spend variation net of the growth trend). **Organic's 95% CI includes values near zero** — the one channel this artifact was built to give a genuinely new answer for is also the one whose answer is least certain, and that is this artifact's headline uncertainty finding, not a footnote.

**Pooled robustness model** (secondary, not primary — see model-shape reasoning above): n=108, R²=0.774, common elasticity **0.2246** (SE 0.1157, p=0.0550), vs. **0.7304** with no trend control. VIF on log-spend with the trend control included: **7.42**.

**Residual diagnostics** (per channel, primary models): Breusch-Pagan heteroscedasticity test (regressing squared residuals on fitted values, n·R² ~ χ²(1)) — organic p=0.135, paid p=0.920, community p=0.151, all homoscedastic at the 5% level. Lag-1 residual autocorrelation is negative for all three (organic −0.370, paid −0.272, community −0.302; Durbin-Watson 2.5–2.6, i.e. mild negative autocorrelation, not the positive autocorrelation that would most concern a trending series) — reported rather than corrected for, since a Newey-West or GLS correction would be adding machinery this artifact's own sample size cannot really support estimating. `corr(residual, fitted)` is not reported as a diagnostic: it is identically ~0 for OLS by construction (the normal equations make residuals orthogonal to fitted values), and a prior draft of this validation wrongly treated it as informative before catching that it cannot fail on a real bug — the same category of mistake `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s own validation entry records and corrected for its pipeline-identity check.

**Out-of-sample validation** — two reads, since neither alone is fully honest at n=36:
- **LOOCV** (each month held out once, model refit on the other 35): RMSE on conversions — organic 5.37, paid 4.84, community 2.56 (25.8%, 26.4% and 37.1% of each channel's own mean monthly conversions respectively — an error floor at roughly a quarter to a third of the mean, not a precise forecast). The full model beats a trend-only baseline for organic (+0.7% log-RMSE improvement) and paid (+1.7%) but not community (−0.6%, i.e. spend adds no measurable out-of-sample value for community at this checkpoint) — consistent with community's non-significant coefficient above.
- **Forward holdout** (last quarter, Oct–Dec 2025, withheld; trained on the other 33 months): RMSE on conversions — organic 5.96, paid 5.26, community 4.23. Explicitly caveated: this rests on 3 points per channel and should not be read as precise; it is reported because it is the economically relevant test (this is what "forecast next quarter's response" actually looks like), not because its own error estimate is tight.
- Both readings are back-transformed from log-scale predictions via **Duan's smearing estimator** (mean of `exp(training residuals)`, computed fold-by-fold so no held-out point's own residual leaks into its own correction) rather than a naive `exp(log-prediction)`, which is a biased estimator of the conditional mean under log-normal-ish errors (Jensen's inequality).

### Cross-validation against the holdout read — the artifact's central test

`compare_to_holdout()` puts this artifact's model-implied incremental share next to `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s holdout-measured incremental share, for the two channels that have one. **The counterfactual is deliberately the holdout's own suppressed-spend level (10% of normal budget — `generators/config.py`'s `HOLDOUT_BUDGET_SHARE = 0.10`), not spend = 0.** A log-log model's curve is only informative over the spend range it was fit on; extrapolating to spend = 0 asks it to answer outside the data it saw, and because every channel's fitted elasticity is positive, that extrapolation trivially predicts 0 conversions at spend = 0 regardless of the elasticity's actual value — a spurious 100% "incremental share" that reflects the functional form's boundary behavior, not a finding. Evaluating at 10% of spend instead answers exactly the question the holdout answers, on the same scale: `implied_share = 1 − 0.10^elasticity`.

**Result at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31:**

| Channel | MMM-implied incremental share | 95% CI | Holdout-measured incremental share (SE) | Gap (MMM − holdout) | Holdout within MMM's CI? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Paid | **0.398** | [−0.092, 0.669] | **0.648** (0.101) | **−0.249** | Yes |
| Community | **0.358** | [−1.330, 0.823] | **0.816** (0.185) | **−0.458** | Yes |
| Organic | **0.628** | [−0.137, 0.879] | not estimable (never runs a holdout) | not applicable | — |

**Three honest readings, no reconciliation fabricated where one is not warranted:**
1. **The MMM materially understates the holdout's causal read for both channels it can be checked against** — by 25 points for paid, 46 points for community. This is a genuine, reportable finding about the limits of regression-based estimation on this data, exactly the kind of result the build spec's line-98 flag predicts, not a defect to explain away. The most plausible mechanism, directly supported by the VIF finding above: the trend control that is *necessary* to avoid the no-trend model's inflated 0.73 elasticity also *absorbs* some of spend's real effect wherever spend and the trend co-move, because the two cannot be cleanly separated at this sample size (VIF 7.4–13.2). A regression cannot recover what a real randomized holdout can when the regressor of interest is this collinear with an omitted-if-uncontrolled confound.
2. **The holdout value sits inside the MMM's own 95% CI for both channels**, despite the point estimates disagreeing by 25–46 points. The CIs are wide enough that "the MMM's estimate is statistically consistent with the holdout" and "the MMM's point estimate materially understates the holdout" are both true at once — reported as such rather than picking the more flattering of the two framings.
3. **Organic's implied share (0.628) is this artifact's one genuinely new number**, and it carries the same order of uncertainty as the other two (CI spanning −0.137 to 0.879) with no external check available to narrow it. It should be read as "consistent with organic marketing having a real, plausibly substantial incremental effect, with too much uncertainty at this sample size to say more" — not as a confident 63% figure. This is the headline finding for the one channel this artifact exists to give an answer for.
