**Incremental bookings translation** (`compute_incremental_bookings()`, window totals at 2025-12-31): organic \$3.33M implied incremental bookings (749.6 attributed conversions × \$7,082 avg/conversion × 0.628 implied share), paid \$2.92M (659.7 × \$11,103 × 0.398), community \$1.65M (248.7 × \$18,510 × 0.358). Reported as the dollar-denominated answer build spec item #12 asks for, carrying every uncertainty caveat above — these are not point estimates to plan against without the CI attached.

### Checkpoint instability — the second checkpoint is itself a finding, not a confirmation

Every other Phase 4 artifact's second checkpoint (2025-06-30) is reported as evidence the artifact **is not tuned to one period** — the same shape holding up on less history. This artifact's second checkpoint shows the opposite, and that is reported as directly as the shape-holding-up story is reported elsewhere: with 30 months instead of 36 (n=90 pooled), **community's elasticity swings from 0.192 to 0.915** — a near-5x change — and **which channels "beat" the trend-only baseline in LOOCV flips entirely** (organic and paid beat it at 2025-12-31; only community beats it at 2025-06-30). The pooled VIF on log-spend rises from 7.42 to **13.24** with six fewer months of history, not fewer. **Zero of three channels clear the nested F-test's p<0.10 non-vacuousness floor at the 2025-06-30 checkpoint** (organic p=0.153, paid p=0.250, community p=0.261), against two of three at 2025-12-31. This instability is exactly what build spec line 98 predicts for a 36-month series and is exactly why it is reported as a first-class result of this build rather than a caveat buried in Known limitations.

| Channel | Elasticity 2025-12-31 | Elasticity 2025-06-30 | MMM-implied share 2025-12-31 | MMM-implied share 2025-06-30 | Holdout share (unchanged) | Gap 2025-12-31 | Gap 2025-06-30 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Organic | 0.430 | 0.402 | 0.628 | 0.604 | not estimable | — | — |
| Paid | 0.221 | 0.224 | 0.398 | 0.404 | 0.648 | −0.249 | −0.226 |
| Community | 0.192 | **0.915** | 0.358 | **0.878** | 0.816 | −0.458 | **+0.063** |

Community's swing is large enough that its cross-validation gap flips sign — a meaningful understatement at 2025-12-31 becomes near-exact agreement at 2025-06-30 (gap +0.063). **Reading this as "the model got better with less data" would be exactly backwards**: it did not resolve toward the truth, it swung past it on a smaller, noisier sample and happened to land close on this particular checkpoint. Paid and organic, by contrast, are comparatively stable across the two checkpoints (paid elasticity 0.221 vs. 0.224; organic 0.430 vs. 0.402) — the instability is channel-specific, concentrated in community, the channel with the smallest spend base and the fewest touches in this data.

### Validation — a genuine regression artifact, the first one in this portfolio

Per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' **"Regression models"** category — the first artifact in this project to actually be this shape, per that same skill's own note that capacity planning was expected to be a regression and turned out not to be ("verify the shape against the data... the same lesson capacity planning recorded"). No coefficient table, R²/RMSE, or residual diagnostic is skipped or left TBD; all are reported above. There is no accounting-identity tie-out to check the way capacity planning's gap decomposition or marketing attribution's credit conservation have — a fitted spend-response curve has no closed-form invariant of that kind — so this artifact's non-vacuousness checks are a nested F-test (does spend add real explanatory power over a trend-only baseline) and a comparison of LOOCV error against that same baseline (does spend add real *out-of-sample* value), run in `run_build_time_validation()`.

| Check | What it pins down | Floor | Result (2025-12-31) | Result (2025-06-30) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `spend_adds_value_organic` | Nested F-test p<0.10 **and** LOOCV beats trend-only baseline | both conditions | **Pass** — F p=0.0810, LOOCV +0.7% | **Fail** — F p=0.1533, LOOCV −0.8% |
| `spend_adds_value_paid` | same | both conditions | **Pass** — F p=0.0924, LOOCV +1.7% | **Fail** — F p=0.2499, LOOCV −0.4% |
| `spend_adds_value_community` | same | both conditions | **Fail** — F p=0.4894, LOOCV −0.6% | **Fail** — F p=0.2610, LOOCV +0.5% |

**2 of 3 checks pass at 2025-12-31; 0 of 3 pass at 2025-06-30.** This is reported plainly rather than reframed — a regression artifact whose own non-vacuousness checks fail more often than they pass at a 30-month window is an honest demonstration of the build spec's line-98 concern, not a build defect. Unlike every structural/logic artifact in this portfolio, where "0 of N checks pass" would mean something is broken, here it means the data genuinely does not support a confident regression-based incrementality read at this sample size for two of three channels — which is the finding, correctly surfaced rather than hidden behind a looser threshold chosen after seeing the result. (The p<0.10 floor and the LOOCV-beats-baseline condition were both fixed before the second checkpoint was run.)

**Sample sizes**: 36 monthly observations per channel / 108 pooled at 2025-12-31 (30 / 90 at 2025-06-30); LOOCV uses 35 training points per fold (29 at 2025-06-30); the forward holdout uses 33 training months / 3 test months (27/3 at 2025-06-30).

**Persistence**: scalar time-series metrics logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `log_performance()` — `elasticity_{channel}`, `elasticity_se_{channel}`, `elasticity_p_{channel}`, `r2_{channel}`, `n_months_{channel}`, `spend_nested_f_p_{channel}`, `loocv_rmse_conversions_{channel}`, `loocv_mae_conversions_{channel}`, `model_implied_incremental_share_{channel}`, `forward_holdout_rmse_conversions_{channel}`, `elasticity_pooled_common`, `elasticity_pooled_no_trend_control`, `vif_log_spend_with_trend`, `r2_pooled`, `gap_vs_holdout_{paid,community}`, `checks_total`/`checks_passed`. The full coefficient tables (with SE/t/p/CI), the residual-diagnostic detail and the per-channel forward-holdout actual-vs-predicted series do not fit that log's flat scalar grain and are recorded as structured detail in this entry instead, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' Persistence note.

### Target and drift threshold — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed

No accuracy/error target existed for this artifact before it was built (methods doc TBD, per this doc's own convention), and unlike every classification artifact in this portfolio there is no QA-plan-stated AUC range to adopt. The following is this artifact's own proposal at build time, in the same spirit as the variance engine's ±8% threshold and capacity planning's 15% achievability margin, grounded in what this build actually produced rather than an external benchmark this data has no way to be compared against:

1. **LOOCV RMSE on conversions ≤ 50% of the channel's own trailing mean monthly conversions.** Grounded directly in what was observed: 25.8% (organic), 26.4% (paid), 37.1% (community) at 2025-12-31 — all comfortably inside the floor with 1.3–2x headroom, so this is a real but not knife-edge bar. An artifact whose typical miss exceeds half the average month's volume is not offering useful precision, which is the property this floor actually protects.
2. **Nested F-test p<0.10 is the non-vacuousness bar, not a claim of statistical significance in the conventional sense.** Deliberately looser than the usual p<0.05, for the same reason `analytics-engineering-conventions`' non-vacuousness checks elsewhere in this portfolio are set to catch "no real effect at all" rather than to certify precision (e.g. marketing attribution's mix-shift floor is set at the null's 99th percentile, not at an arbitrary round number). At p<0.10, this floor is already failed by every channel at the 2025-06-30 checkpoint — stated as a real, expected consequence of a thinner window, not evidence the floor is miscalibrated.
3. **No numeric drift band is proposed for the elasticity point estimates themselves.** With only two checkpoints six months apart and a documented ~5x swing in one channel's coefficient (community 0.192 → 0.915), any band narrow enough to be informative would also flag ordinary small-sample noise as "drift," and any band wide enough to avoid that would be uninformative. This is stated as a deliberate non-decision rather than a placeholder: a numeric elasticity drift band is deferred until at least 4–6 quarterly checkpoints accumulate enough history to distinguish real drift from the instability this artifact's own build already demonstrates is present at this sample size. In its place, two qualitative drift signals are proposed for `drift-monitor` now: **`checks_passed`** dropping to 0 of 3 for 2 consecutive checkpoints (already observed once, at 2025-06-30 — a third consecutive occurrence would be the trigger), and **`vif_log_spend_with_trend`** (pooled) exceeding 15 (roughly 2x the 7.42 observed at 2025-12-31, using the conventional "severe multicollinearity" rule-of-thumb threshold of 10 with headroom) — a rise past that level would mean the trend/spend confound has gotten too severe for the coefficient to mean anything at all, as opposed to merely being uncertain.
4. **`gap_vs_holdout_{paid,community}`** is watched but not gated: per the Cross-validation section above, a nonzero gap is an expected finding at this sample size, not a defect. It is logged so a future checkpoint's movement (gap narrowing as more months accumulate, or widening further) is itself observable, the same treatment marketing attribution's own `measured_incremental_share_pooled` gets for a different reason.

### Known limitations

- **This artifact is underpowered for two of three channels by its own stated non-vacuousness bar**, and materially more so at a 30-month window than a 36-month one. This is the build spec's line-98 concern, realized and reported rather than avoided — it is the primary limitation of this artifact, not a secondary one.
- **The trend/spend collinearity (VIF 7.4–13.2) means "spend's effect" and "the business's organic growth" cannot be cleanly separated at this sample size.** The trend control is necessary (the no-trend model's 0.73 pooled elasticity is not credible — see the trend-confound finding above) but very likely still absorbs some of spend's true effect wherever the two co-move, which is the leading explanation for why this artifact understates the holdout's causal read for both channels it can be checked against.
- **Organic's incremental-share estimate has no external check and cannot get one from this data** — the same structural gap `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s own entry names for its holdout method (`dim_campaign` carries `is_holdout = false` on every organic row by design). This artifact is organic's only incrementality read in this portfolio, and its wide CI (spanning near-zero to substantial) is why it is reported as a headline uncertainty finding rather than a confident number.
- **No full adstock/saturation MMM structure is fit** — see the model-shape reasoning above. If the simulation horizon is ever deliberately extended (a cross-cutting Phase 1 decision this artifact does not make unilaterally, per the build spec's own line-98 framing), a 2-parameter saturation curve and a geometric-decay carryover term would both become identifiable in a way they are not at 36 monthly points per channel, and this module's `_design_matrix()` seam is where that extension would land.
- **Classical OLS standard errors assume independent, identically-distributed errors**, an assumption a trending monthly time series puts real pressure on. The residual diagnostics above (Breusch-Pagan, Durbin-Watson) do not find severe violations in this data, but a heteroscedasticity/autocorrelation-consistent (HAC/Newey-West) standard-error correction was not implemented — at n=36 the correction itself would be poorly estimated, adding a second source of imprecision on top of the one already reported rather than removing it.
- **The forward-holdout read rests on 3 points per channel (9 pooled)** and should not be treated as a precise forecast-error estimate — it is reported because it is the economically relevant test, with that caveat attached, not because its own number is tight.
- **No mart exposes this artifact's monthly channel panel.** Every number here is built by calling into `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s Python functions at request time; a `mart_marketing_attribution` (already flagged as worth building in that module's own entry) would let this artifact read a materialized panel instead of recomputing the credit panel on every run — not built here, the same kind of gap capacity planning and marketing attribution both record for their own rep-quarter and credit-panel outputs.

## Retention / expansion cohort analytics

- **Built**: `analytics/retention_cohorts.py`. Grain: one row per (signup-quarter cohort × entry segment) per months-since-acquisition for the two "cohort triangles" (logo retention, revenue retention); one row per entry_segment (or per channel) per months-since-acquisition for the coarser pooled cross-cut curves. Source marts: `dim_accounts` (`signup_date`, `channel`), `fact_account_segment_history` (entry segment, via `is_initial_segment` — **not** `dim_accounts.segment`, which is the account's current, possibly-migrated segment), `fact_revenue_monthly` (`mrr`, and account-month **presence** — a churned account's rows simply stop, confirmed contiguous with no gaps for all 7,700 accounts at build time). No dbt model was added or changed; no Phase 1 generator or raw table was added.
- **What it is**: build spec item #20 (Wave 6) — "descriptive, population-level view by acquisition vintage." A standard SaaS "cohort triangle": accounts grouped by when they signed up, tracked forward from that anchor.
- **Boundary with Segment migration analysis (`analytics/segment_migration.py`) — stated explicitly, per that entry's own established convention**: that artifact is an **event lens** — which accounts moved segment, when, why, and how much MRR they carried with them (`mart_segment_migration`'s migration-event grain). This module is a **cohort lens** — population-level survival/revenue-retention curves anchored to acquisition vintage, entirely independent of whether any given account ever migrated. The two do not duplicate each other's numbers, and this module deliberately does **not** follow `mart_durability`'s segment-level convention of excluding migration-driven MRR change from the source segment's own NRR/GRR (reported separately there as "graduated revenue" — see the Segment migration entry above). A cohort here is defined by **account identity at entry**, not by current segment, so an account that migrates and grows is still the same cohort member growing — its higher post-migration MRR is correctly part of its cohort's revenue trajectory, not carved out. Logo retention is unaffected by migration either way in both artifacts, per the build spec ("the account persists, just at a different segment") and `mart_durability.sql`'s own comment confirming a migrating account counts as retained.
- **Boundary with Account health score (`analytics/health_score.py`) — the build spec's own stated distinction**: that artifact is **predictive** and **account-level** — will *this specific* account churn, scored today, via a fitted logistic regression against a stated AUC target. This module is **descriptive** and **population-level** — what does the *typical* account in a given acquisition cohort look like over elapsed time. No churn classifier lives here; there is nothing fitted, no train/test split, no probability, no AUC.
- **Shape decision — structural/descriptive, not predictive**: every number is a direct, deterministic aggregation of already-materialized mart data (account presence/absence in `fact_revenue_monthly` at a given elapsed month, summed `mrr`). Per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' **"Structural/logic artifacts"** category — same reasoning the Segment migration, Capacity planning, Territory coverage and TAM/ICP entries all give — **there is no coefficient table, no AUC, no confusion matrix, no R²/RMSE here, and their absence is deliberate, not pending.**
- **Model type selection and rationale (why not predictive)**: a churn-probability-by-cohort model was the credible alternative and was rejected for the same reason the Segment migration entry rejects a migration-propensity classifier and the Account health score's calibration note guards against scoring inside a deterministic decline window: this artifact's job is to describe what already happened to a fixed, closed population (a cohort's membership never changes after month 0), not to predict what will happen to an open, still-evolving one. A genuinely predictive, account-level churn model already exists and is not duplicated here — that is exactly the Account health score, item #4, described above.
- **No stochastic step lives in this module** (no train/test split, no sampling, no simulation) — every function is a direct aggregation, so no random seed applies, matching `analytics/segment_migration.py`'s and `analytics/capacity_planning.py`'s precedent.

### Cohort granularity — quarterly, chosen against the real signup-volume distribution

Monthly signup volume ranges from ~35–50/month in 2020–2022 up to ~200–270/month in 2025, but that is the **blended** total across all three segments. Split by entry segment (the primary cut this artifact needs), Enterprise signups run as low as 0–15 **per quarter** even in 2024–2025, and Commercial 2–39 per quarter — both far too thin at monthly grain for a stable cohort curve. **Quarterly cohorts are used uniformly across all three segments** (not a different grain per segment) so the three are directly comparable, and because a single artifact reporting different grains per segment would be a harder thing for a consumer to read correctly than one that is honestly thin in a few early Enterprise cells. Every triangle row carries its own `cohort_size`, so a thin cell (e.g., a 2020-Q1 Enterprise cohort of 0–4 accounts) is visible as thin rather than hidden.

### Right-censoring — two different, deliberately different, conventions

- **Cohort triangle (fine grain — one specific signup-quarter × entry-segment cell)**: **full-cohort-membership consistency.** `elapsed_months` is computed relative to each account's own signup month (not the cohort label's nominal quarter-start — this avoids a late-in-quarter signup's pre-existence period reading as false early non-activity), then a cohort's curve is reported only out to `min()` of every member account's own max-observable elapsed month as of `as_of_date` — i.e., capped by the **last** account to join that quarter. This guarantees every reported column reflects the cohort's full, fixed membership; no member silently drops out of the denominator as elapsed grows. A cohort with no observable months yet (e.g., a 2025-Q4 cohort at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31) simply reports elapsed_months=0 only.
- **Pooled cross-cuts (entry_segment or channel, spanning many vintages of different ages)**: the standard survival-curve **"number at risk"** convention instead — at each elapsed month N, the denominator is every account (in that group, across all vintages) old enough to have reached N as of `as_of_date`, and it shrinks as N grows. Applying the triangle's full-membership convention here would cap the *entire* pooled segment curve at its single youngest vintage's censoring point, discarding real signal from every older vintage — the wrong trade for a curve that exists specifically to pool across ages. `n_accounts_at_risk` is reported alongside every pooled-curve row so a consumer can see the sample thinning as elapsed time grows (e.g., Enterprise: 113 accounts at risk at month 0, down to 23 by month 36).
- Neither convention ever extrapolates or silently truncates a censored value — an elapsed month beyond what a cohort/group has actually reached is simply absent from the output.

### Segment and channel differentiation — a genuine, checkable finding

**Contract-term structure shows up directly in the pooled logo-retention curves, unprompted** — this is exactly the kind of causally-wired relationship this project's data is built to produce, not decoration:
- **SMB** (month-to-month, no contract) shows smooth, continuous logo erosion starting at elapsed month 1 — no cliff, consistent with usage-driven churn that can happen at any time.
- **Commercial** (annual contract) sits at a **flat 100% cumulative logo retention through elapsed month 12**, then drops sharply starting month 13 (86.5% at month 13) — the first annual-renewal decision point, exactly where the build spec's churn mechanic ("Commercial/Enterprise: ... churn (non-renewal)") says it should land.
- **Enterprise** (multi-year contract) sits flat at 100% through elapsed month 24, then drops starting month 25 (92.5%) — its first multi-year-term renewal decision.

This has a direct methodological consequence: **checking logo retention against the QA plan's "annual" benchmark at the 12-months-since-acquisition mark would read Commercial and Enterprise as a trivial, uninformative 100%** — a contract-term ceiling artifact, not a genuine retention reading (neither segment has had a chance to churn yet at that mark). The benchmark-grounding check below is therefore evaluated at **elapsed month 36** — the smallest round (12-month-increment) mark that clears both segments' first observed renewal cliff — and the raw cumulative rate is **annualized** via `implied_annual = cumulative ** (12 / 36)` before comparison, the same compounding convention `analytics/segment_migration.py`'s module comment already uses to move between annual and monthly rates (deriving monthly shares from an annual GRR/NRR figure).

**Result at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31**, pooled by entry_segment, elapsed month 36:

| entry_segment | cumulative logo retention (36mo) | implied annual logo retention | QA benchmark range ("Logo retention, annual") | n accounts at risk | within ±5pp tolerance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SMB | 58.9% | **83.8%** | 75–85% | 1,449 | **Yes** |
| Commercial | 77.9% | **92.0%** | 88–93% | 68 | **Yes** |
| Enterprise | 91.3% | **97.0%** | 93–97% | 23 | **Yes** |

**Segment ordering holds strictly**: SMB < Commercial < Enterprise on both the raw cumulative and the annualized figure, at every elapsed mark from ~month 25 onward (the point after which all three have separated from their respective ceilings). All three annualized figures land inside — not merely near — the QA plan's benchmark reference table range, evaluated at a mark chosen specifically to give every segment a genuine chance to have churned. Enterprise's and Commercial's `n_accounts_at_risk` (23 and 68 respectively) are thin by month 36 — expected, given Enterprise totals only 200 accounts company-wide and the oldest vintages are the only ones old enough to have reached 36 months — so these two figures are directional, not high-confidence point estimates; reported with their sample size alongside rather than smoothed over.

- **Revenue retention is reported but deliberately NOT numerically benchmark-checked**, for a reason worth stating rather than silently omitting: because this module's revenue base is each cohort's **month-0 MRR** (per the build spec's own definition — "total mrr at month-since-acquisition N relative to month-0 mrr"), and Acme is a usage-metered product where a brand-new signup's first (partial) month of usage is small relative to its mature run-rate, cohort revenue-retention ratios are **dominated by onboarding ramp-up**, not by steady-state expansion/contraction — e.g., pooled Commercial revenue retention reads ~12x at month 12, and pooled SMB ~8x at month 6. This is real, expected behavior given the company's PLG/usage-based motion, and a genuinely different signal than `mart_durability`'s calendar-anchored, already-ramped-population NRR (which is what the QA plan's NRR/GRR benchmark rows describe) — not a bug to paper over by picking a later "base" month, which would just redefine the metric away from what the build spec asked for. The logo-driven vs. expansion-driven decomposition (`revenue_retention_rate == logo_retention_rate × expansion_factor`, verified exactly below) is what actually isolates the two effects; the raw revenue-retention ratio by itself is reported as a descriptive trajectory, not a number graded against a target.
- **Channel cut**: computed pooled by `channel` alone (not crossed with entry_segment — channel is orthogonal to segment per this project's invariants, and crossing the two leaves cells as thin as 18 total accounts for `outbound_sdr`). At month 12, logo retention is close across the three channels (`self_serve` 86.4%, `inbound_marketing` 86.5%, `outbound_sdr` 100% on n=12) — channel does not show the sharp, structural differentiation entry_segment does in this data; `outbound_sdr`'s n=18 total accounts (12 at risk by month 12) is too thin to draw a real conclusion from either way, stated rather than implied by a clean-looking 100%.

### Validation — structural reconciliations and one non-vacuousness check, not statistics

In place of an accuracy/error target (none applies — see the shape decision), three checks in `run_build_time_validation()`, all passing at both checkpoints tested (`as_of_date` 2025-12-31 and 2025-06-30):

| Check | What it pins down | Tolerance | Result (2025-12-31) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `validate_cohort_population_reconciliation` | Each cohort's month-0 population (`cohort_size`, built via a pandas groupby over `load_cohort_accounts`) matches an **independently computed** count — a fresh SQL group-by using DuckDB's own `date_trunc('quarter', ...)`, an entirely separate implementation of the same quarter-boundary logic. Confirms no account gained/lost in the pandas assembly and that the two date-truncation implementations agree | 0 max abs diff | **0.0** (68 cohorts checked) |
| `validate_active_count_reconciliation` | Every triangle cell's `active_count` (pandas merge/groupby pipeline) matches an **independently computed** figure — a single SQL query using DuckDB's own `datediff('month', ...)` for elapsed_months, a separate implementation of the elapsed-month arithmetic. Since `cohort_size` is independently reconciled above and `active_count` is independently reconciled here, the "active + churned = cohort_size, no accounts gained or lost" invariant is validated end-to-end through these two reconciliations — `churned_count` is arithmetically `cohort_size − active_count`, and both terms are now pinned by a separate computation path each, rather than needing a third, tautological anti-join check on top | 0 max abs diff | **0.0** (2,340 cells checked) |
| `validate_revenue_decomposition_identity` | `revenue_retention_rate == logo_retention_rate × expansion_factor` for every triangle cell — an exact arithmetic identity by construction (mirrors `analytics/capacity_planning.py`'s gap-decomposition tie-out) | ≤ 1e-9 max abs diff | **1.4e-14** (floating-point noise only, 2,340 cells checked) |
| `validate_benchmark_grounding` | Non-vacuousness: segment ordering (SMB < Commercial < Enterprise, logo retention) at elapsed month 36, plus each segment's annualized 36-month logo retention against the QA plan's benchmark range ± the tolerance below | see below | **Pass**, all 3 segments, ordering holds |

**Second checkpoint, `as_of_date` 2025-06-30**: 62 cohorts, 1,952 cells checked, both reconciliations exact (0.0), decomposition identity 7.1e-15, benchmark check passes for all 3 segments (implied annual: SMB 83.8%, Commercial 91.7%, Enterprise 98.2% — Enterprise sits just above its 93–97% range but comfortably inside the ±5pp buffer, on a thinner n=19-at-risk sample than the later checkpoint), ordering holds. Not tuned to one snapshot.

**Benchmark tolerance: ±5 percentage points — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed.** Nothing upstream (build spec, QA plan, metric tree) states a number for comparing a from-acquisition cohort curve against the QA plan's calendar-anchored "annual" benchmark row, so this is this artifact's own proposal at build time, in the same spirit as the segment-migration analysis's 10% firmographic-rescore floor and Territory coverage's 0.75/1.25 non-vacuousness floors. Grounding: the cohort-curve figure and the benchmark figure are **related but not definitionally identical constructs** (a from-acquisition cumulative survival rate, annualized by compounding, vs. `mart_durability`'s calendar-anchored monthly retained/starting ratio compounded to an annual figure on an already-mature population) — a small buffer is needed to absorb that construct gap without being so wide it could never catch a genuine miscalibration. All 6 segment-checkpoint readings observed at build time (3 segments × 2 checkpoints) landed inside the buffer, most inside the raw benchmark range itself with no buffer needed at all — evidence the buffer is not doing the work of masking a real miss.

**Sample sizes**: 7,700 accounts in scope at 2025-12-31 (6,246 at 2025-06-30), spanning 68 (cohort_quarter × entry_segment) cohorts (62 at 2025-06-30); 2,340 logo-triangle cells at 2025-12-31 (1,952 at 2025-06-30). Entry-segment population (month-0, i.e. every account that ever entered that segment — this is larger than `dim_accounts`' CURRENT-segment counts of 6,500 / 1,000 / 200 for accounts that have since migrated up): SMB 7,222, Commercial 365, Enterprise 113. `n_accounts_at_risk` shrinks from those totals as elapsed time grows — down to 1,449 / 68 / 23 respectively by elapsed month 36, since only the oldest vintages within each entry segment have aged that far as of `as_of_date`.

### Target and drift threshold

- **Target**: no accuracy/error target applies (structural/descriptive, per the shape decision above). The three checks above stand in its place. The two structural reconciliations and the decomposition identity are stated as **hard invariants with a floating-point tolerance rather than a band** — deterministic arithmetic has no sampling variance, the same reasoning every other structural-artifact entry in this doc gives for its own exact tie-outs.
- **Drift review trigger**: per the doc's general rule, 2 consecutive monthly checkpoints failing any check → flagged for review. The two reconciliations and the decomposition identity are expected to never breach (structural invariants — a breach is a defect to fix, not a trend to watch). The benchmark-grounding check is the one worth watching in `fact_model_performance_history` (`implied_annual_logo_retention_smb`/`_commercial`/`_enterprise`) as new cohorts age into the 36-month mark over time — if a segment's annualized figure drifted outside its ±5pp buffer, that would mean this data's retention behavior has genuinely shifted (or the generator's causal wiring has drifted), worth a real look, not silently absorbed by loosening the buffer after the fact.
- **Persistence**: scalar time-series metrics logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `log_performance()` — the three reconciliation max-abs-diffs, `total_accounts_in_scope`, `total_cohorts`, and per-segment `logo_retention_month36_{segment}`, `revenue_retention_month36_{segment}`, `n_accounts_at_risk_month36_{segment}`, `implied_annual_logo_retention_{segment}`. The full cohort triangles and pooled curves (variable-width, one row per elapsed month per cohort/group) do not fit that log's flat scalar grain, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' Persistence note, and are the structured artifact output itself rather than a logged checkpoint.

### Known limitations

- **Quarterly grain leaves early Enterprise (and some early Commercial) cohort cells thin** — a genuine consequence of real signup volume, stated in the granularity section above and visible directly via each row's `cohort_size`/`n_accounts_at_risk`, not smoothed over. A coarser (annual) Enterprise-only cohort cut is a natural extension if finer Enterprise-specific granularity is ever needed; not built here since the pooled-by-segment cut already answers the "does Enterprise retain better" question the task asks for.
- **Revenue-retention's month-0 base is ramp-dominated for a usage-metered product** (see the segment/channel differentiation section above) — reported as a descriptive trajectory including the logo/expansion decomposition, not benchmark-checked numerically, since forcing a numeric target onto a construct that diverges from the benchmark's intent for a principled, documented reason would be a worse choice than stating the divergence plainly.
- **Channel cut shows no sharp differentiation in this data** (unlike entry_segment) and `outbound_sdr`'s n=18 accounts total is too thin to conclude much either way — reported as a genuine (if modest) finding rather than forced into a narrative the sample doesn't support.
- **No AM-ownership-continuity or graduated-revenue cross-check against Segment migration analysis** — deliberately out of scope per the boundary stated above; the two artifacts answer different questions from the same underlying accounts and are not reconciled against each other's specific numbers (there is nothing to reconcile — they compute genuinely different quantities, unlike the exact-match reconciliations this entry's own validation section performs internally).

## Scenario planning / sensitivity analysis

- **Built**: `analytics/scenario_planning.py`. Grain: one scenario run per `(as_of_date, set of hypothetical leaf/Layer-2 changes)` — `run_scenario()` recomputes every real, marts-validated ancestor formula in `docs/acme-corp-gtm-metric-tree.md` under the hypothetical, up to Layer 1 and the Growth pillar bridge. Source marts: `mart_growth_bridge`, `mart_efficiency`, `mart_durability` — read exclusively through `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s own loaders (`vd.load_growth_bridge`/`load_efficiency`/`load_durability`/`blend_layer1_actuals`), never a second, independent read of the same marts. No dbt model or Phase 1 generator was touched to build this artifact.
- **What it is**: build spec item #19 (Wave 6): "propagates a changed assumption through the tree's existing equations — distinct from forecast, which predicts, not simulates a hypothetical." Nothing here is fitted; every output is the exact arithmetic result of a stated formula applied to a stated hypothetical input, on top of a real, already-observed baseline.

### Which tree representation this builds on

This module reuses `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s `_TREE` and its loader/blending functions as the **sole** source of node identity (key, layer, parent_key, pillar, computability) — imported directly (`from . import variance_diagnostic as vd`), never re-typed. It does **not** use `semantic/metric_registry.json` (Wave 3's MCP-facing parse of the tree markdown): the registry carries metadata for the MCP server's own SQL layer to execute, not a Python function this module could call to get real, point-in-time Layer-1/Layer-2 baseline values to perturb, and `vd._TREE` already has a build-time-proven track record (its own synthetic-scenario suite, plus `analytics/data_quality_governance.py`'s independent metric-tree-integrity checker) that the registry's parsed structure has no equivalent for. Reusing `vd`'s functions directly means this module's baseline is *proven* identical to the already-shipped variance-diagnostic engine's baseline — verified explicitly by `check_baseline_matches_variance_diagnostic()`, not merely assumed (see Validation below).

Neither `vd._TREE` nor the registry stores an evaluable "combine children into parent" function, so this module adds a small formula layer (`compute_contraction_churned_revenue`, `compute_expansion_consumption_revenue`, `compute_win_rate`, `compute_avg_initial_commitment`, `compute_onboarding_cs_efficiency`, `compute_consumption_payback`, `compute_nrr_grr_monthly`, `compute_logo_retention_monthly`, `compute_am_expansion_arr`, `compute_growth_pillar_bridge`, and for the cost side `compute_net_new_arr_ce`, `compute_prior_sm_cost_ce`, `compute_am_cost_ce`, `compute_expansion_mrr_ce`, `compute_ttm_ratio`) grounded directly in `analytics/data_quality_governance.py`'s `_METRIC_TREE_EDGES_STATIC` list — every edge this module propagates through is one of that artifact's governance-PASSING edges (edge_id cited on each formula function and each `NodeResult`), and every edge governance found `NOT_COMPUTABLE` is honoured as `NOT_COMPUTABLE` here too (see below), never re-derived or back-solved to fabricate a missing factor.

### NOT_COMPUTABLE handling

A scenario naming a driver whose only path to Layer 1 runs through a governance-`NOT_COMPUTABLE` edge is not silently dropped or absorbed at a stale baseline value — it is reported as `NOT_COMPUTABLE` with the exact governance reason, alongside whatever real consequence *does* compute. The build-time synthetic scenario for this, and the one for the cost drivers below, run `run_scenario()` end to end against real marts data at `as_of_date` 2025-11-30, because the reporting logic lives in `run_scenario()`'s node-registration, not in a standalone formula a hand-built DataFrame could exercise:

- **`_case_not_computable_new_logo`** — the build spec's own example ("what if win rate improves by 5 percentage points"). `win_rate__Commercial` moves by its real new value (to within one whole-deal quantization step — `new_won = round(scenario_win_rate × closed_count)`, since won-deal count is a real integer and a continuous rate request naturally lands off a whole deal); `new_logo_bookings_amount_rep_sold` (the real, distinct bookings-lens consequence) computes; and `new_logo_consumption_revenue` (Layer 1) is reported `computable=False` with the exact reason ("New logo = Pipeline × Win rate × Avg initial commitment" is `NOT_COMPUTABLE`: `win_rate`/`avg_initial_commitment` are computed over closed SQO-stage opportunities while Pipeline generated is a different, lead-grain population — multiplying the three would manufacture a tie-out governance already proved does not hold). The Growth pillar bridge is reported with New Logo held at its real baseline and an explicit `partial` note — the reachable legs (Contraction, Churn, Expansion, migration) still move, New Logo does not, and that is stated rather than silently absorbed either way.
- **`_case_cost_driver_propagates_to_magic_number`** — the cost drivers (`s_m_cost`, `am_cost_by_segment`) propagate through the real Magic number and AM efficiency equations, which governance PASSes (`sm_cost_sum`, `magic_number_ratio`, `am_efficiency_ratio`). A `+10%` change to Commercial's prior-period S&M cost must report `magic_number` computable, lower than baseline, and equal to the trailing-twelve-month ratio recomputed independently from the mart's own twelve numerator/denominator pairs (≤1e-9). Both cost drivers are defined over Commercial and Enterprise only — SMB has no AM, and its program-spend-only cost reaches no blended Layer-1 node — so an SMB cost change is rejected with a `ValueError` rather than silently ignored. The same pass exercises the numerator side: the four flows behind net new ARR (new logo, expansion, contraction, churn) also reach `magic_number` through `compute_net_new_arr_ce`, and expansion reaches `am_efficiency`.

Both pass at both checkpoints (2025-12-31, 2025-06-30).

### Multi-input scenarios and no double-counting

Multiple leaves can be changed in one `run_scenario()` call. Every requested change is resolved (`_resolve_changes()`) to a canonical `(driver, segment)` pair against one shared perturbed copy of the evaluation month's mart rows — there is no per-path delta accumulation anywhere in the module, so two requests that resolve to the *same* underlying dollar quantity (e.g. `contraction_mrr` for Commercial, requested either directly or via the `nrr_contraction_rate`/`grr_contraction_rate` rate-space aliases) cannot silently double-apply. **`_case_conflicting_rate_aliases_raises`** confirms this structurally rather than by caller discipline: requesting `nrr_contraction_rate: +0.02` and `grr_contraction_rate: +0.05` for the same segment in one scenario — both aliases of `contraction_mrr` — raises `ValueError` rather than picking one arbitrarily. `_case_ratio_of_sum_and_shared_ancestor` (a pure-function synthetic case) separately confirms the positive side of the same guarantee: one leaf (`contraction_mrr`, +200 on a 10,000 starting-MRR base) reached via three real ancestors on different tree branches (Contraction+churned revenue, NRR, GRR, plus the Growth pillar bridge) moves each by exactly the amount the single underlying change predicts, applied once.

`_DUAL_MART_DRIVERS` (`contraction_mrr`/`churn_mrr`/`expansion_mrr`) is the other half of the no-double-counting mechanism worth naming: these three are the same physical dollar quantity independently materialized under the identical column name in both `mart_growth_bridge` and `mart_durability` (confirmed by `analytics/data_quality_governance.py`'s own `durability_growth_cross_mart` check), so `_apply()` writes both mart copies in lockstep — writing only one would leave NRR/GRR (which read only `mart_durability`) blind to a scenario change that had genuinely been applied.

### Count-driver quantization

Every `unit="count"` driver's `scenario_value` is rounded to the nearest whole count in `_resolve_changes()`, immediately after being computed and before its `ResolvedChange` is built — so the rounded value is what gets reported *and* what gets written, never a fractional count shown in one place and a silently-truncated one applied in another. This matters because pandas' nullable `Int64` columns — the correct dtype for a real headcount like `mart_efficiency.am_touchpoint_count`, the same treatment `mart_growth_bridge.new_business_won_count`/`new_business_lost_count` and `mart_durability.starting_accounts`/`churned_accounts` get — reject a fractional write outright rather than silently truncating it. `run_build_time_validation()`'s own example scenario illustrates the mechanics: at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31, Enterprise's real `am_touchpoint_count` baseline for the evaluation month is `261`; a `-10%` pct request against it computes `261 × 0.9 = 234.9`, which rounds to `235` (a delta of `-26` off the real baseline) before being applied, and the scenario propagates through `onboarding_cs_efficiency` cleanly.

This mirrors the rounding `win_rate` scenarios already apply to the resulting won-deal count (`new_won = (scenario_value × closed).round()`), documented in `_case_not_computable_new_logo`'s own docstring as "a real quantization effect of a discrete population, not an engine bug" — and applies uniformly to every count-typed driver (`am_touchpoint_count`, `automated_actions_delivered`, `starting_accounts`, `churned_accounts`), not only the one in the module's own example scenario. `starting_accounts`/`churned_accounts` (`mart_durability`) and `new_business_won_count`/`new_business_lost_count` are covered by the same guard under a `pct`/`delta` request.

### Shape decision and model type selection

Structural/logic artifact, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' "Structural/logic artifacts" category (deterministic equation propagation, nothing fitted) — the same category `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`, `analytics/capacity_planning.py` and `analytics/data_quality_governance.py` are built as. No coefficient table, no AUC, no confusion matrix, no R²/RMSE, and their absence is deliberate, not pending.

**Why not a fitted sensitivity/elasticity model** (the credible alternative, considered and rejected): regressing each Layer-1 metric on its Layer-2/3 drivers to estimate an empirical sensitivity coefficient was rejected for the same reason `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py` and `analytics/data_quality_governance.py` reject a fitted approach to their own tree-walking jobs — the metric tree's parent-child relationships are not unknown quantities to estimate, they are exact arithmetic identities `analytics/data_quality_governance.py` has already independently confirmed hold in the built marts to floating-point precision (14/14 real edges PASS at both its checkpoints). Fitting a coefficient to approximate a relationship that is already known exactly would trade a correct deterministic answer for a noisy estimated one. A deterministic walk of the same declared, already-validated structure is the only defensible model class for this artifact's actual job.

### Validation — synthetic scenarios and a real-data baseline self-check

`run_build_time_validation(as_of_date)` runs `run_synthetic_scenarios()` (8 known-answer cases, six of them hand-computable, per the "Structural/logic artifacts" correctness bar — "does propagation through a real formula produce the exact hand-computable answer, does a `NOT_COMPUTABLE` gap get reported rather than fabricated, does a shared ancestor tie out without double-counting") plus `check_baseline_matches_variance_diagnostic()` (a zero-change scenario's baseline side must reproduce `vd.blend_layer1_actuals()`'s own real value exactly, proving the reuse this module's tree-representation section claims actually holds).

**8 of 8 synthetic scenarios pass at both checkpoints (2025-12-31, 2025-06-30):**

| Scenario | What it checks |
|---|---|
| `sum_node_contraction_churned_revenue` | +30 to `contraction_mrr` moves `contraction + churn` by exactly +30 |
| `product_node_am_expansion_arr` | AM expansion ARR = (Σ expansion_mrr) × 12; a +200 change to the sum moves the annualised product by exactly +2,400 |
| `ratio_node_onboarding_cs_efficiency` | A numerator-only change (touchpoints 500→600, actions held fixed) moves the ratio to exactly 600/100,000 |
| `shared_ancestor_contraction_mrr_no_double_counting` | One leaf, three real ancestors (Contraction+churned revenue, NRR, GRR, plus the Growth pillar bridge) — each moves by exactly the single underlying change, once |
| `not_computable_new_logo_from_win_rate_scenario` | `win_rate` scenario computes its own new value; `new_logo_consumption_revenue` reports `NOT_COMPUTABLE` with the exact governance reason, never a fabricated figure |
| `ttm_ratio_node_magic_number_shape` | Twelve months of numerator 100 over denominator 50 is 2.0; changing only the evaluation month's denominator 50→55 gives 1200/605; a window shorter than twelve months is NaN |
| `cost_driver_propagates_to_magic_number` | `s_m_cost` +10% on Commercial moves `magic_number` down to the independently recomputed trailing-twelve-month ratio; an SMB cost change is rejected |
| `conflicting_rate_aliases_raise` | Two rate-space aliases of the same underlying quantity, requested with conflicting values, raise `ValueError` rather than picking one |

**Baseline self-check (`check_baseline_matches_variance_diagnostic`): all 9 covered nodes match `vd.blend_layer1_actuals()`'s real output to ≤1e-6 at both checkpoints** — `contraction_churned_revenue`, `expansion_consumption_revenue`, `onboarding_cs_efficiency`, `consumption_payback`, `nrr`, `grr`, `logo_retention`, `magic_number`, `am_efficiency`.

**Example multi-input scenario, `as_of_date` 2025-12-31** (`contraction_mrr +15% Commercial, am_touchpoint_count -10% Enterprise`): 19 nodes computed (15 plus S&M cost, Magic number, AM cost and AM efficiency, all real nodes), all `computable=True`, no `NOT_COMPUTABLE` gap hit (neither driver touches a `NOT_COMPUTABLE` path); the Commercial contraction change also moves `magic_number` through net new ARR, and the Enterprise touchpoint change does not. Selected results — `contraction_churned_revenue` $1,183,648.05 → $1,219,746.48 (+$36,098.43, exactly the Commercial contraction delta); `nrr` 1.91542 → 1.90801 (monthly rate 0.93955 → 0.93592, the other 11 trailing months real and unperturbed); `growth_pillar_bridge` $9,341,156.35 → $9,305,057.92 (−$36,098.43, tying to the same Commercial contraction delta with the sign the pillar formula subtracts it); `am_touchpoint_count` Enterprise resolves 261 → 235 (delta −26, per the count-driver quantization above), `onboarding_cs_efficiency` 4.327e-06 → 4.187e-06 accordingly.

**Drivers registered: 13** (`contraction_mrr`, `churn_mrr`, `expansion_mrr`, `am_touchpoint_count`, `automated_actions_delivered`, `blended_cac`, `avg_utilized_action_margin_per_account`, `starting_accounts`, `churned_accounts`, `win_rate`, `avg_initial_commitment`, `s_m_cost`, `am_cost_by_segment`), plus 5 rate-space aliases (`nrr_contraction_rate`, `grr_contraction_rate`, `nrr_churn_rate`, `grr_churn_rate`, `nrr_expansion_rate`) that resolve to 3 of the 13.

### Determinism

No stochastic step exists anywhere in this module — every output is the exact arithmetic result of a stated formula applied to a stated input, so no random seed applies. Matches `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s and `analytics/data_quality_governance.py`'s own precedent.

### Target and drift threshold

- **Target**: no accuracy/error target applies (structural/logic artifact). The 8 synthetic scenarios and the baseline self-check stand in its place — both are stated as **hard invariants** (exact hand-computable answers to ≤1e-9, baseline match to ≤1e-6), not a statistical band, since deterministic arithmetic has no sampling variance.
- **Drift review trigger**: per this doc's general rule, 2 consecutive monthly checkpoints with any synthetic scenario or the baseline self-check failing → flagged for review. In practice these are structural invariants expected to never fail on correctly-built data — a failure is a defect to fix (as the count-quantization bug above was), not a trend to watch. The one thing genuinely worth watching in `fact_model_performance_history` over time is `baseline_self_check_nodes_passed` against `_total`: a drop there would mean this module's reuse of `vd`'s baseline computation has silently diverged (e.g. `vd._TREE` or its loaders changed underneath this module without a corresponding update here), which is exactly the failure mode the self-check exists to catch before a scenario's baseline could be silently wrong.
- **Persistence**: scalar metrics logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `log_performance()` under `model_name = 'scenario_planning'` — `synthetic_scenarios_total`, `synthetic_scenarios_passed`, `baseline_self_check_nodes_total`, `baseline_self_check_nodes_passed`, `drivers_registered`. Logged at 2025-12-31 and 2025-06-30 (both 8/8 synthetic, 9/9 baseline-check nodes, 13 drivers); an earlier 2025-11-30 entry from the module's own `__main__` smoke-test block (which uses `vd`'s own hand-run canonical date, distinct from this project's two official checkpoints) is also present in the log and reflects the same fully-passing result. The variable-width detail (the full node table per example scenario, the per-scenario expected/observed dict) does not fit that log's flat scalar grain and is the structured detail recorded in this entry instead, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' Persistence note.

### Known limitations

- **Only leaf/Layer-2 drivers that have a governance-PASSING path to a real Layer-1 ancestor can be scenario-tested end to end.** The governance-`NOT_COMPUTABLE` edges (New logo = Pipeline × Win rate × Avg commitment; Expansion = Wallet share progression × Overage realization) are honoured as gaps here, not closed — a scenario naming a driver on one of those paths gets an honest `NOT_COMPUTABLE` report for the blocked node plus whatever real, distinct consequence does compute (e.g. the bookings-lens figure for a win-rate scenario), never a fabricated headline number.
- **The Growth pillar bridge is reported `partial` (New Logo held at baseline), not fully computable, whenever a scenario touches `win_rate`/`avg_initial_commitment`.** This is a stated modelling limitation carried in the node's own `note` field, not a claim that New Logo genuinely would not move under that scenario.
- **Count-typed drivers are quantized to the nearest whole count** (see "Count-driver quantization" above) — a requested `pct`/`delta` change that would imply a fractional headcount lands at its nearest whole-count neighbour instead, the identical treatment `win_rate` scenarios give won-deal counts. This is a real, small (≤0.5-unit) precision loss on count drivers specifically, not present on the `usd`/`ratio`/`usd_per_account` drivers, and is visible directly in `ResolvedChange.scenario_value`/`.delta` rather than silently absorbed.
- **A hypothetical is applied only to the evaluation month's own mart row(s)** — every prior month in a trailing-twelve-month NRR/GRR/logo-retention window is held at its real, unperturbed value. This is the only well-defined interpretation of "what if X changes" without a multi-month forecast model (a different artifact — see the Forecast entry's own "no forward-quarter forecast" limitation, which this module's docstring cross-references), but it does mean a scenario cannot express "this change persists for N months" — only "this one evaluation month is different."
- **Enterprise `win_rate`/`avg_initial_commitment` can be undefined (`NaN`) at some evaluation months** (e.g. 2025-12's evaluation month has 0 Enterprise won deals that month, so `avg_initial_commitment__Enterprise` is `NaN` at both baseline and scenario) — a real, sparse-population data fact surfaced honestly (`NaN` in, `NaN` out) rather than imputed or hidden.
- **No dbt model or Phase 1 generator was added or changed** to build this artifact, and none was needed — every driver and formula already existed in the built marts and in `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s/`analytics/data_quality_governance.py`'s own prior work.

## Proxy-metric health / analytics investment prioritization

- **Built**: `analytics/proxy_metric_health.py`. Build spec item #15 (Wave 7, infrastructure/governance), explicitly named in Section 8's Wave 7 description as "last by necessity — not computable meaningfully until real history accumulates."

### Headline finding, stated first because it is the point of this entry, not a caveat on it

**No genuine drift or decay claim is possible yet for anything in this project.** `checkpoint_counts_by_model()` reconciles directly against `data/model_performance_history.csv` (via `analytics/model_performance.py`, the one named `fact_*` exception every Phase 4 module reads/writes directly) and reports, at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31: **21 models with any logged checkpoint** (19 built Phase 4 artifacts, the executive-summary narrative step, which logs a checkpoint on every run, and this one), **1–3 real checkpoints each**, spanning at most three real dates per model, all produced inside one development pass rather than a monthly-recurring operational cadence — `account_health_score` and `segment_migration_analysis` have exactly **1** checkpoint each; most artifacts have **2** (typically 2025-06-30 and 2025-12-31, though `forecast`'s are 2025-08-15/2025-11-14 and `deal_diagnostics`'/`pricing_packaging_analytics`'s are 2025-06-30/2025-11-30); `automated_playbook_triggers`, `scenario_planning` and `weekly_executive_readout` have **3**. A trend needs more than 1–3 points to be a trend rather than a coin flip. Fabricating additional historical checkpoints to make this artifact look more capable than the data supports would violate this project's own "don't fabricate to fill a gap" discipline — the same discipline the MMM/incrementality entry demonstrates by reporting a weak, underpowered regression result honestly rather than inventing confidence it hadn't earned. **This artifact is therefore a governance catalog and framework, not a time-series drift tool.** It inventories every drift-monitor hook this project's own build process has already identified, reconciles exactly how many readings each one currently has, and ranks where the next unit of analytics-engineering investment does the most good — using only what is genuinely computable today. It becomes more useful as `drift-monitor` actually runs on a recurring cadence and `fact_model_performance_history` accumulates real history; that is stated honestly rather than overclaiming present usefulness.

### Shape decision and model type selection

Structural/logic artifact, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' "Structural/logic artifacts" category (the same category the variance-diagnostic engine, segment migration, capacity planning, marketing attribution, data quality governance and scenario planning all use). Nothing here is fitted: no coefficient table, no AUC, no confusion matrix, no R²/RMSE, no calibration note applies, and their absence is deliberate, not pending. **Why this shape over the credible alternative (a fitted anomaly/change-point detector over `fact_model_performance_history`)**: with 1–3 points per model, any such detector would either be undefined (needs more history than exists) or would be fitting noise and calling it a pattern — precisely the failure mode this entry's headline finding exists to avoid. Every question this artifact actually answers today ("what has this project already flagged as worth watching," "how many readings does each hook genuinely have," "which data gap blocks the most downstream evidence") has an exact, already-materialized answer; fitting anything to answer them would manufacture false precision, the same reasoning the Variance-diagnostic engine entry gives for rejecting a regression-based variance attribution against the metric tree's own exact identities.

### Inputs — all governance/bookkeeping, no mart, per this artifact's own nature

- `docs/acme-corp-analytics-methods.md`'s own text (this file) — the source of the drift-monitor hook catalog below, read and pattern-matched at run time by `verify_hooks_trace_to_doc()` and `validation_maturity_summary()`, never re-typed from memory without a live check.
- `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s `_TREE` (imported directly, `from . import variance_diagnostic as vd`, the same reuse pattern `analytics/scenario_planning.py` and `analytics/mmm_incrementality.py` already establish for borrowing another Phase 4 module's already-validated logic) — the live NOT_COMPUTABLE tree-coverage read.
- `analytics/data_quality_governance.py`'s `_METRIC_TREE_EDGES_STATIC` (imported directly) — the live governance-edge status reported alongside the engine-marking consistency check below.
- `data/model_performance_history.csv` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `read_performance_history()` — the checkpoint-depth and hook-reading-count reconciliation.

No `mart_*`/`dim_*`/`fact_*` business-input table is read anywhere in this module — there is no business question here, only a governance read over other artifacts' own already-recorded state, the identical scope this project already grants `fact_model_performance_history` itself.

### `as_of_date` — genuinely point-in-time for one component, structural for the rest

`as_of_date` filters `checkpoint_counts_by_model()` and `hook_reading_counts()` to `as_of_date_ <= as_of_date` — real, dated behavior, verified directly: run at `as_of_date` 2025-06-30, only 16 of the eventual 21 models have any checkpoint yet (`account_health_score`, `segment_migration_analysis`, `variance_diagnostic_engine` and `forecast` all postdate 2025-06-30 entirely), and 7 of 26 hooks have zero readings rather than 3 — a materially different answer, not a cosmetic one. The drift-monitor hook catalog, the live NOT_COMPUTABLE tree-coverage read and the governance-edge cross-reference are **not** point-in-time in that sense: they are a structural read of the *current* state of this codebase and this doc, because no versioned history of the metric tree, the governance-edge table or the methods doc exists to query "as of" a past date. Stated explicitly rather than fabricating pseudo-point-in-time behavior for components that genuinely have none.

### The drift-monitor hook catalog

**26 hooks catalogued across 15 already-built artifacts**, extracted by inspection from this doc's own per-artifact "Target and drift threshold" sections — every artifact this doc names a specific metric/relationship as a "drift-monitor hook" or "worth watching" is represented, not a curated subset. `verify_hooks_trace_to_doc()` confirms all 26 hooks plus the 2 explicitly-not-monitored artifacts (Variance-diagnostic engine, Weekly executive readout — both state their own "no calibration to drift" reasoning) plus the 1 not-yet-built sibling (Segment/lead scoring model, still TBD) trace to a real, verbatim substring of this doc's current text — **29 of 29 pass**.

**A genuine documentation-consistency finding, not just an inventory**: this doc's own two general rules at the top (**Proxy-metric decoupling** — a stated trailing-6-month-vs-12-month correlation-drop formula for a true Layer-3-to-Layer-2 tree edge — and the **Drift review trigger** cadence rule) are *not*, in practice, what most of the 26 cataloged hooks actually implement. **Zero of the 26 hooks compute the formally-stated proxy-decoupling correlation formula anywhere in this project yet.** Each artifact instead defined its own bespoke "is my grounding constant/apparatus/relationship still real" check (a VIF, a coverage-index band, a mix-shift-TVD floor, a firmographic-rescore share). `analytics/data_quality_governance.py`'s three not-independently-random spot-checks (`win_probability` gap/z, `churn_probability` median decline, `usage_growth` ratio) are the closest conceptual relative — they operationalize CLAUDE.md's "independently-random columns are a bug" invariant, which is exactly what the formal decoupling rule exists to catch in production — but even these use a margin/gap mechanism, not the stated trailing-correlation-drop formula. Cataloged hooks are therefore classified into three families rather than forced into the doc's own two-rule framing: **`model_calibration_accuracy_drift`** (a fitted classifier's AUC/calibration — `account_health_score`, `forecast`'s ML lens, `deal_diagnostics`' classifier — 6 hooks), **`grounding_or_apparatus_integrity`** (does a stated constant/relationship/control-group still hold — 15 hooks, the large majority), and **`firing_rate_or_reuse_integrity`** (has a rule/flag/reuse-dependency's firing count silently collapsed to zero — 5 hooks). **Actually implementing the formal proxy-decoupling correlation check, for at least one real Layer-3-to-Layer-2 edge, is itself the single highest-priority open item this catalog surfaces** — it is the rule this whole Wave 7 item's name references and it has never been run once.

**A second, more specific finding, only visible by reconciling the catalog against the persisted log rather than trusting each artifact's own prose**: `find_unpersisted_hooks()` shows **3 of 26 hooks have zero persisted readings today — not a thin two, literally zero** — a nearer-term, cheaper fix than "wait for more history," since in each case the metric already exists in the artifact's own code and was simply never passed to `log_performance()`:
  1. **`rep_productivity`'s `cohort_recovery_against_raw_quantile`** — named in that entry's own drift-threshold paragraph as "the genuinely worth-watching series," but `analytics/rep_productivity.py`'s Persistence paragraph only logs `reps_flagged_<flag_name>` counts and the synthetic/structural check totals; the enrichment-rate value itself is never logged.
  2. **`data_quality_governance`'s three not-independently-random spot-check margins** — the check functions (`check_win_probability_not_random`, `check_churn_probability_not_random`, `check_usage_growth_not_random`) exist and run at every checkpoint, but only the aggregate `invariant_checks_passed`/`_total` pass/fail counts are logged, never each check's own margin value.
  3. **`deal_diagnostics`' per-flag real-data firing counts** — that entry's own drift-threshold paragraph names "any flag's real-data firing count collapsing to exactly 0" as the hook; its own Persistence paragraph (three sentences later in the same entry) lists only the classifier's AUC/calibration/confusion-matrix scalars, not the six flags' firing counts. An internal inconsistency within one artifact's own entry, not a cross-artifact gap.

Full per-hook reading counts (all computed live against the CSV, at `as_of_date` 2025-12-31) are in `analytics/outputs/proxy_metric_health_2025-12-31.md`.

### Data-gap / investment-prioritization ranking

`live_not_computable_gaps()` walks `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s `_TREE` directly (never a copy) and finds **23 of the tree's 71 registered nodes NOT_COMPUTABLE** (0 of 11 Layer-1 nodes, 8 of the tree's 28 Layer-2 nodes, 15 of its 32 Layer-3 leaves — consistent with the Variance-diagnostic engine entry's coverage table). `rank_data_gap_priorities()` groups these into **14 root subtrees** (the highest point at which each gap starts) and ranks them cheapest-to-close first, then by live downstream-node count blocked, using four cost tiers curated by inspection of each gap's actual root cause (not derivable from the tree structure alone) and cross-checked live against `vd._TREE` by `verify_data_gap_keys_are_live()` (**14 of 14 pass** — every cited key still exists and still carries the computability this catalog claims for it; `find_uncatalogued_gap_roots()` is the reverse check and returns none, so every live blocked root is catalogued):

| Tier | Meaning | Roots | Nodes blocked |
|---|---|---|---|
| A — already computed elsewhere | Data + computation already exist in another Phase 4 module; pure wiring/reuse. Empty today, kept empty by the standing check under Headline finding #3 | 0 | 0 |
| B — Phase 2 mart-exposure only | Raw/fact data already exists; needs a mart column exposed, no new data | 1 | 1 |
| C — Phase 4 code/design fix | Mart already carries everything; needs a code change to an existing model | 1 | 1 |
| D — genuine new Phase 1 data | No source anywhere in this project, or a generator that logs no variation to measure; needs a generator change | 10 | 19 |
| E — not a real gap | Definitionally redundant with its own parent, or defined purely by reference to nodes catalogued elsewhere; no investment closes it | 2 | 2 |

**Headline finding #3, a standing cross-artifact consistency check**: `check_pipeline_generated_staleness()` re-reads `analytics/variance_diagnostic.py`'s `_TREE` and `analytics/marketing_attribution.py`'s `PIPELINE_CHANNEL_TO_TREE_KEY` on every run and fails if any tree node that `marketing_attribution.py` computes and validates (`pipeline_generated`, `organic_content`, `paid`, `community_events`) is marked NOT_COMPUTABLE or missing. It guards against one failure class: a node carrying a no-data reason ("no mart exposes leads, campaign touches or PQL signals") while a different shipped artifact computes and validates it (`reconcile_pipeline_generated_identity`, ≤1e-9), with `analytics/data_quality_governance.py`'s governance-edge table (edge_id `pipeline_generated_channel_formula`) already reading `VALIDATED_ELSEWHERE`. The engine reads that computation (see the Variance-diagnostic engine entry's Pipeline-generated subsection), the check returns zero stale nodes, and the tier-A row above is empty. `mart_growth_bridge`'s own header comment describes the same class of drift for the same node. The check is scoped to the nodes one artifact covers; it is not a general cross-artifact audit, which remains a separate piece of work. The Layer-1 product identity (`New logo = Pipeline × Win rate × Avg initial commitment`) is genuinely, separately NOT_COMPUTABLE (different populations — converted leads vs. closed SQO-stage opportunities — cannot be multiplied into one tie-out), and the governance edge table keeps it so.

Runner-up findings: (1) **Every Layer-1 node has a computable actual**; the gaps are Layer 2/3 only. `magic_number` and `am_efficiency` compute from rep fully-loaded cost (`fact_rep_monthly_cost`) and marketing spend, and the two nodes still blocked in those branches are `cost_per_channel_activity` (Tier B: cost/MQL and cost/SDR meeting need channel spend joined to per-channel lead and meeting counts — the cost side and the activity counts both exist, only the mart join is missing) and `expansion_revenue_drivers` (Tier E: defined by reference to Growth's wallet-share and overage drivers; overage realization is now computed, wallet share is catalogued separately). (2) **The opportunity, workflow-chain and overage nodes are wired, not catalogued as gaps.** `discount_rate_vs_list`, `deal_size_trend_within_band`, `loss_reason_mix`, `poc_pass_rate`, `renewal_win_rate`, `overage_realization`, `workflow_chain_underutilization` and `rep_capacity_ramp_mix` are computed from three marts, so `cost_per_channel_activity` is the only Tier-B root. (3) **`account_health_score`** is one blocked node: its four Layer-3 inputs are computed from `mart_account_health` but cannot be reached while the composite is blocked. It needs zero new data — only a code change to `analytics/health_score.py`'s `score_accounts()` to gate its scored population on `churn_month > month` instead of `customer_status = 'Active'` and to hold a monthly history, a fix that entry's own text already names as out of the variance-diagnostic engine's scope. (4) **Tier D includes four roots that sit under computable parents**: `stage_to_stage_conversion` (a generator limitation rather than a mart gap: every deal logs every stage, so there is no variation to expose), `churn_reason_category` (no churn reason field exists), and `time_to_respond_churn_risk_flag` and `loud_vs_silent_churn_mix` (children of the computable `renewal_win_rate`). Full ranked table in `analytics/outputs/proxy_metric_health_2025-12-31.md`.

### Validation-maturity summary

`validation_maturity_summary()` scans this doc's own text, per top-level artifact section, for "PROPOSED, not yet confirmed" against "CONFIRMED by `analytics-model-validator`" — a structural text-scan of the doc's own language, not a statistical estimate. Result at build time: **1 of 15 built-and-thresholded artifact sections** (Segment/segmentation migration analysis) carries a threshold that has actually been independently reviewed and confirmed; **14 of 15** carry at least one threshold still self-proposed at build time and not yet independently reviewed. This is a real, countable signal of where this portfolio's analytics maturity is thinnest in the specific sense of "has anyone other than the artifact itself checked this number" — not a criticism of any individual artifact (most of these thresholds are honestly reasoned first-build proposals, several already reviewed once by `analytics-model-validator` for their grounding logic even where not formally marked CONFIRMED), but a concrete backlog: which self-proposed threshold gets independently confirmed next is itself a real investment-prioritization decision this catalog surfaces rather than making.

### Validation — structural/logic artifact, three correctness checks (`run_build_time_validation`)

Per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' "Structural/logic artifacts" category: no coefficient table, no AUC, no confusion matrix, no R²/RMSE — none applies, stated explicitly rather than left blank/TBD. Three checks, each appropriate to what a catalog artifact actually needs to prove about itself (does it accurately represent its own sources, not does it predict anything):

| Check | What it pins down | Result (2025-12-31) |
|---|---|---|
| `verify_hooks_trace_to_doc` | Every cataloged hook, not-monitored artifact and not-yet-built artifact traces to a real, verbatim substring of this doc's current text — no fabricated entries | **30 of 30 pass** |
| `verify_data_gap_keys_are_live` | Every node key cited in the data-gap ranking still exists in `vd._TREE` and still carries the computability this catalog claims for it | **14 of 14 pass** |
| `verify_checkpoint_reconciliation` | `checkpoint_counts_by_model()`'s per-model checkpoint count matches an independent, from-scratch parse of `data/model_performance_history.csv` exactly — two independent implementations, the same discipline `analytics/segment_migration.py`'s growth-bridge reconciliation and `analytics/forecast.py`'s `rollup_ties_to_deal_detail` use | **PASS, 21 of 21 models checked, 0 mismatches** |

**3 of 3 pass at both build-time checkpoints (2025-06-30 and 2025-12-31)** — logged as `checks_passed`/`checks_total`.

### Target and drift threshold

- **Target**: no accuracy/error target applies — this is a structural/logic catalog artifact with no predictive or fitted component, per the shape decision above. The three correctness checks stand in its place.
- **Drift review trigger**: per this doc's general rule, 2 consecutive monthly checkpoints with any of the three correctness checks failing → flagged for review — in practice these are structural invariants expected to never fail on a correctly-curated catalog (a failure would mean this catalog itself has gone stale relative to the doc, the live tree, or the CSV — a defect to fix, the same treatment every other structural artifact in this doc gives its own tie-outs). **The genuinely worth-watching series over time, once real recurring `drift-monitor` runs exist**: `hooks_with_zero_persisted_readings` (should only ever *decrease*, as the three unpersisted-hook gaps above get wired in — an increase would mean a newly-built artifact shipped a "worth watching" hook without logging it, repeating the same gap); `max_checkpoints_across_models` and `min_checkpoints_across_models` (both should climb over real calendar time as `drift-monitor` actually runs; this is the single most direct measure of whether this project's own recurring-monitoring discipline is happening); `artifacts_with_confirmed_threshold` (should only ever *increase*, as `analytics-model-validator` works through the 14-artifact PROPOSED backlog this entry surfaces). None of these are meaningful drift signals yet at 2 checkpoints six months apart — they are the series this catalog exists to make trackable once real recurring history accumulates, stated as a forward-looking seam rather than a present claim.
- **Persistence**: scalar summary counts logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `log_performance()` under `model_name = 'proxy_metric_health'` — `checks_total`/`_passed`, `hooks_cataloged`, `hooks_traced_to_doc`, `hooks_with_zero_persisted_readings`, `not_drift_monitored_artifacts`, `not_yet_built_artifacts`, `models_with_any_checkpoint`, `min_checkpoints_across_models`, `max_checkpoints_across_models`, `data_gap_roots_cataloged`, `data_gap_roots_live_confirmed`, `pipeline_generated_stale_marking_confirmed` (0 when the engine's markings agree with `marketing_attribution.py`), `artifacts_with_confirmed_threshold`, `artifacts_with_proposed_not_confirmed_threshold`. Logged at 2025-06-30 and 2025-12-31 (this artifact's own two checkpoints now appear in the same checkpoint-depth table it reports on everything else — the identical thin-history condition genuinely applies to itself, not stated as an exemption). The variable-width detail (the full 26-hook catalog with reading counts, the 14-root data-gap ranking, the per-artifact validation-maturity table) does not fit that log's flat scalar grain and is the structured detail recorded in `analytics/outputs/proxy_metric_health_<as_of_date>.json`/`.md` and in this entry instead, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' Persistence note.

### Known limitations

- **This artifact cannot make any of the 26 cataloged hooks more observable by itself** — closing the three zero-reading gaps above needs a code change to `analytics/rep_productivity.py`, `analytics/data_quality_governance.py` and `analytics/deal_diagnostics.py` respectively, each outside this artifact's own scope (this catalog names the gap; it does not fix the modules it is cataloging, the same boundary the Variance-diagnostic engine entry draws around `analytics/health_score.py`'s `score_accounts()` population gate).
- **The cost-tier assignment on each data-gap root (A/B/C/D/E) is curated judgment, not derived from the tree.** `verify_data_gap_keys_are_live()` confirms every cited key's *existence and computability status* is still accurate, but it cannot verify that a tier label ("this needs only a mart change" vs. "this needs new raw data") is still correct if the underlying marts or generators change — that would need a human or a follow-up build-time review to re-confirm.
- **The formal Proxy-metric decoupling rule (trailing-6-month-vs-12-month correlation drop) has never been run once in this project**, for any of the tree's real Layer-3-to-Layer-2 edges — this catalog surfaces that as its single highest-priority open item rather than treating the 26 bespoke hooks as a substitute for it. Building that check is `drift-monitor`'s job, not this artifact's; this entry only establishes that the job has not started.
- **No historical version of this doc, the metric tree, or the governance-edge table exists**, so the "structural" components of this catalog (the hook list, the data-gap ranking) cannot themselves be queried point-in-time the way the checkpoint reconciliation can — stated in the `as_of_date` section above, repeated here because it is a real scope boundary, not an oversight.
- **This artifact does not build or extend `drift-monitor`.** It is explicitly the catalog `drift-monitor` should read when it does start running on a recurring cadence — the analogous seam the Weekly executive readout entry leaves for the executive-summary narrative, filled with a real, working framework rather than a placeholder, but not the recurring monitoring process itself.

## Testing / experimentation methodology & platform

- **Built**: `analytics/experimentation_platform.py`. Grain: one evaluation run per (experiment_id, as_of_date), operating over `dim_experiment` (one row per registered experiment) and `fact_experiment_assignment` (one row per experiment-unit assignment); the one outcome-specific adapter this artifact ships (`EXP-0001`'s) additionally reads `fact_leads` and `fact_campaign_engagement_events`. Build spec item #13, Wave 7, marked **infrastructure**.

### Honest scope, stated first

This project has exactly **one** real, catalogued, randomized experiment today — `EXP-0001`, the paid/community marketing holdout program, catalogued in `dim_experiment` as this project's Wave 7 raw-data prerequisite. This entry does not inflate that into a richer experimentation history than exists — the same discipline this doc's Proxy-metric health entry applies to its own thin checkpoint history. What genuinely *is* complete, and is the actual deliverable here, is the **framework**: a set of general, parameterized functions over the registry/assignment tables that would evaluate any future registered experiment the same way, proven correct against the one real case with a known-correct answer, and ready for the next registration via one new outcome adapter — not a rewrite of the statistical core.

### What it is, and what it deliberately is not

Build spec item #13's own framing is "platform," not "a one-off analysis of the one experiment that exists." This artifact is the reusable evaluation engine a RevOps/growth team would want for **any** experiment registered in `dim_experiment`/`fact_experiment_assignment`: target-metric lift (treatment vs. control) with a significance test, guardrail-metric monitoring, and sample-size/power context — plus a separate, fully general registry-completeness governance check.
