It is explicitly **not** a recomputation of `EXP-0001`'s already-published result. `analytics/marketing_attribution.py` already derives the holdout incrementality read correctly (point-in-time-safe cell resolution, censored-cell exclusion, a design-based two-proportion estimator with a closed-form standard error) and **is not modified or imported by this module** — every formula this module needs (resolution-horizon derivation, point-in-time outcome-state resolution, the two-proportion z-test, cell pooling with censoring discipline) is reimplemented independently in `analytics/experimentation_platform.py`, so the reproduction check below is a genuine second derivation, not a call into the module it is cross-checking against.

**The one honest limit of "general": outcome sourcing needs an adapter.** The statistical core (`derive_group_resolution_horizon`, `resolve_binary_outcome_state`, `two_proportion_ztest`, `evaluate_cells`, `power_two_proportion`, `minimum_detectable_effect`, `sample_size_power_context`) is 100% generic — it operates on plain `(unit_id, arm, group, created_date, event_flag, event_date)` columns and contains no `EXP-0001`-specific logic anywhere. What cannot be made generic from free text alone is *where* a given experiment's target-metric outcome event actually lives: `dim_experiment.target_metric` is a human-written sentence, not a machine-actionable join path, and `fact_experiment_assignment` deliberately carries no outcome column of its own (the same reason `fact_leads` and `fact_opportunities` don't carry each other's columns — an assignment fact is not an outcome fact). So each experiment needs exactly one small **outcome adapter** (`OUTCOME_ADAPTERS`, a function that knows which mart(s) hold its outcome event and returns the framework's plain schema) and, where its `guardrail_metrics` reference something the outcome adapter doesn't already cover, one **guardrail adapter** (`GUARDRAIL_ADAPTERS`). Registering a new experiment means writing that one adapter pair; the evaluation math in section 2 of the module does not change. This is the same shape of documented seam this project already uses for the weekly readout's narrative generation (build spec Section 5) — a real, load-bearing boundary, not a placeholder standing in for unfinished work.

### Shape decision and model type selection

**Structural/logic artifact**, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' "Structural/logic artifacts" category (the same category the variance-diagnostic engine, segment migration, capacity planning, marketing attribution, data quality governance, scenario planning and proxy-metric health all use). Nothing here is fitted: no coefficient table, no AUC, no confusion matrix, no R²/RMSE, no calibration note applies, and their absence is deliberate, not pending.

**Model type selection and rationale (why a design-based two-proportion z-test, and not a fitted alternative)**: the only credible alternative considered was a regression-adjusted (CUPED-style) covariate-adjusted estimator, which reduces variance by conditioning on pre-experiment covariates. Rejected for this artifact's actual constraint: the correctness bar for a brand-new platform is "does it get the one already-known-correct answer right," not "can it get it right with a tighter interval" — adding a covariate-adjustment layer changes precision, not correctness, and would still need to run on the same treated/control arms and the same closed-form variance machinery already validated in `marketing_attribution.py`. It is a legitimate future refinement once a second registered experiment genuinely needs the extra precision, not a requirement for proving this one correct. The design-based two-proportion test itself is the right choice for the same reason `marketing_attribution.py` chose it: `EXP-0001` is a genuinely randomized holdout, so a fitted propensity/uplift model would replace a causal answer with a correlational proxy for a quantity already measured causally.

### Inputs, all read from dbt's marts layer

- `dim_experiment` (`experiment_id`, `hypothesis`, `population_definition`, `treatment_definition`, `control_definition`, `assignment_mechanism`, `target_metric`, `guardrail_metrics`, `designed_effect_size`, `status`, `result_*`) — the registry. `result_value`/`result_se`/`result_z` are `EXP-0001`'s already-published, already-validated read, reused only as the reproduction check's known-correct target, never read as an input to this module's own computation.
- `fact_experiment_assignment` (`assignment_id`, `experiment_id`, `lead_id`, `channel`, `cell_quarter`, `arm`, `assigned_date`) — the real population, both arms, 6,265 rows (5,026 treatment / 1,239 control) for `EXP-0001`.
- `EXP-0001`'s outcome adapter additionally reads `fact_leads` (`lead_id`, `channel`, `created_date`, `converted_date`, `is_converted`) for conversion outcomes, and its guardrail adapter reads `fact_campaign_engagement_events` + `dim_campaign` (`is_holdout`) to independently recompute control-arm crossover.
- **Read-scope note**: every table read is `dim_*`/`fact_*` under `dbt/models/marts/`, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' corrected marts-layer boundary. No `stg_`/`int_`/raw source is touched anywhere in this module.

### `as_of_date` — what "as of" means for a closed experiment vs. a running one

Three independent point-in-time cuts, all driven by the same `as_of_date`, so the framework behaves correctly whether the experiment it evaluates is fully resolved (`EXP-0001`'s real case) or hypothetically still enrolling: (1) which **units** are visible — `load_experiment_assignment()` only returns rows with `assigned_date <= as_of_date`; (2) which **outcome events** are visible — `resolve_binary_outcome_state()` only counts a unit's target event as having happened if its event date is `<= as_of_date`; (3) which units are **resolved** (usable in a denominator) at all — a unit that has neither hit the event nor exceeded its group's observed resolution horizon is `open` and excluded from both numerator and denominator, so a cell whose tail hasn't finished resolving by `as_of_date` is measured on the part of it that has, never silently scored as a block of non-events.

For `EXP-0001` specifically, its own `end_date` (2025-03-31) is already in the past at both checkpoints this module runs, so cut (1) is moot — every real difference between the two checkpoints below is driven entirely by cut (3): more cells cross their resolution horizon and become poolable between 2025-06-30 and 2025-12-31. That is not a limitation of evaluating a "closed" experiment — it is the identical resolution-horizon mechanic a still-running experiment needs, demonstrated on real data because this dataset's own simulation window (2023-01 to 2025-12) ends before community's 314-day horizon closes out its final cell (community 2025Q1 stays censored at both checkpoints).

### The reproduction check — does the general framework get the known-correct answer?

`evaluate_experiment("EXP-0001", as_of_date)` independently re-derives the pooled holdout incrementality read through the generic machinery above and compares it to `dim_experiment`'s stored `result_value`/`result_z` — but only when `as_of_date` equals the registry's own `result_as_of_date` (2025-12-31). The registry stores exactly **one** result, the checkpoint the experiment was actually resolved at; comparing an earlier checkpoint's own point-in-time read (genuinely different — fewer cells resolved) against that single stored figure would produce a false "mismatch," so the check is reported `applicable=False` rather than failing at any other date.

**At `as_of_date` 2025-12-31 (the applicable checkpoint):**

| | Recomputed by this framework | Published (`dim_experiment` / `marketing_attribution.py`) | Abs diff |
|---|---|---|---|
| Treated (resolved / conv / rate) | 4,751 / 179 / 3.7676% | 4,751 / 179 / 3.77% | exact |
| Control (resolved / conv / rate) | 1,168 / 14 / 1.1986% | 1,168 / 14 / 1.20% | exact |
| Pooled incremental share | **0.681861** | **0.6819** | **0.0000392** |
| Pooled z-statistic | **4.4289** | **4.43** | **0.0011** |
| Fully-resolved cells | 5 of 6 (community 2025Q1 censored) | 5 of 6 (same) | exact |

**Reproduces within tolerance: `True`.** Both diffs are within the registry's own rounding precision (`result_value` stored to 4dp, `result_z` to 2dp — the tolerances used, 0.0002 and 0.02 respectively, are roughly 2× each figure's own half-rounding-unit, tight enough to catch a real disagreement, loose enough not to fail on the registry's own rounding). This is treated as the single most important check in this artifact: a framework that could not reproduce a known-correct answer on the one case where the answer is already known would not be trustworthy for the next one.

**At `as_of_date` 2025-06-30 (not applicable — reported for framework robustness, not as a reproduction claim):** recomputed pooled incremental share **0.6847** (z = 3.7703) over 4 of 6 fully-resolved cells (3,158 treated / 821 control resolved). This independently matches — via the same generic machinery, not a lookup — the second-checkpoint figure this doc's own Marketing attribution & channel mix entry already reports for `as_of_date` 2025-06-30 ("pooled incremental share 0.6847 ... over 4 fully-resolved cells"), which is a further, informal confirmation that the framework's point-in-time behavior is genuinely correct across time, not tuned to the one final checkpoint.

### Sample-size / power context

`sample_size_power_context()` computes, from this run's own actual pooled fully-resolved arm sizes (not the design-time assumption): achieved power to detect the registry's `designed_effect_size` (0.65) at the real arm sizes, and the minimum detectable effect (MDE) these real arm sizes could reliably distinguish at a conventional 80%-power bar. Both are closed-form normal-approximation reads, no simulation, no seed.

**At 2025-12-31**: 4,751 treatment / 1,168 control resolved, observed control rate 1.1986%. Implied treatment rate at the designed 0.65 incremental share: 3.4247%. **Achieved power at the designed effect: 0.9970** — this experiment's actual cell sizes were comfortably adequately powered. **MDE at 80% power: an absolute rate difference of 1.27pp**, equivalent to an incremental share of **~0.515** — meaning these arm sizes could reliably have detected an effect roughly a third smaller than the one actually observed (0.68), consistent with the observed result clearing significance by a wide margin (z = 4.43 against a 1.96 or even 2.58 bar) rather than barely scraping by.

### Guardrail-metric monitoring

`dim_experiment.guardrail_metrics` for `EXP-0001` specifies two guardrails, both evaluated structurally (not merely cited) by the `EXP-0001` guardrail adapter:

| Guardrail | Observed (2025-12-31) | Threshold | Passes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `resolved_control_cell_lead_volume >= 300` (QA plan Test D power floor) | 1,168 | ≥ 300 | **True** |
| `control_arm_crossover_rate` stays low | 1.05% (13 of 1,239 assigned-control leads) | ≤ 5% (**PROPOSED**, not yet confirmed — see Target and drift threshold below) | **True** |

The crossover figure is **independently recomputed** here from `fact_campaign_engagement_events` + `dim_campaign.is_holdout` (a control-assigned lead that later picks up a non-holdout touch, and the symmetric check for treatment), not copied from this doc's Marketing attribution entry — and it reproduces that entry's own cited 13/1,239 (1.05%) and 38/5,026 (0.76%, reported for completeness, not gated) figures exactly, a further independent confirmation alongside the reproduction check above.

### Registry / methodology governance — structural completeness check

`check_registry_completeness()` is fully general over `dim_experiment`'s own schema — mirroring the discipline `analytics/data_quality_governance.py` applies to the metric tree — and asks, per registered experiment: does it carry a non-blank `hypothesis`, `population_definition`, `treatment_definition`, `control_definition`, `assignment_mechanism`, `target_metric`, `guardrail_metrics` and `status`, a real `designed_effect_size`, **and** a genuine two-armed population actually present in `fact_experiment_assignment` (not just a definition on paper)? At `as_of_date` 2025-12-31: **1 of 1 registered experiments (`EXP-0001`) is complete** — no missing fields, real arm counts (5,026 treatment / 1,239 control).

### Validation — known-answer test for the completeness checker

Per this project's standing discipline (the same one every structural artifact in this doc uses — `data_quality_governance.py`'s `run_synthetic_violation_tests()`, `variance_diagnostic.py`'s synthetic scenarios, `scenario_planning.py`'s baseline self-check), `run_synthetic_registry_completeness_test()` runs the completeness checker against two hand-built, in-memory registry definitions never written to any real table:

- **Clean synthetic registration**: every required field filled, a real two-armed synthetic assignment population. **Expected: complete. Actual: complete. Match: True.**
- **Deliberately broken synthetic registration**: blank `control_definition`, missing `guardrail_metrics`, and a synthetic assignment population containing only `treatment` rows (no real control arm). **Expected: incomplete. Actual: incomplete, with `missing_fields = ['control_definition', 'guardrail_metrics', 'real_treatment_and_control_population']` — all three injected defects named individually, not just a generic fail. Match: True.**

**5 of 5 synthetic scenarios matched their expected outcome** (the two top-level pass/fail cases plus three sub-checks confirming each specific missing-field reason fired). Combined with the reproduction check, this module's own `run_build_time_validation()` reports **3 of 3 checks pass at the applicable checkpoint (2025-12-31)** and **2 of 2 checks pass at the non-applicable checkpoint (2025-06-30)**, where the reproduction check is correctly excluded from the denominator rather than counted as a failure.

### Target and drift threshold

- **Target**: no accuracy/error target applies — structural/logic artifact, per the shape decision above. The reproduction check (exact-answer reproduction within the registry's own rounding precision) and the registry-completeness known-answer test stand in its place, both stated as **hard invariants** rather than a statistical band.
- **Guardrail crossover threshold — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed**: `control_arm_crossover_rate <= 5%`. `EXP-0001`'s own registry row states the guardrail as "stays low" without a numeric bar; this artifact proposes 5% for the same reason the health-score entry's accuracy range and this doc's other self-proposed thresholds are grounded rather than guessed: the observed rate (1.05%) sits comfortably below it (~4.8× headroom), while 5% is still low enough that a genuine crossover collapse (e.g. a holdout campaign's isolation breaking down) would trip it well before the control arm stopped being a clean control. Flagged for `analytics-model-validator` review before being treated as final.
- **Drift review trigger**: per this doc's general rule, 2 consecutive monthly checkpoints with the reproduction check failing (at an applicable date), the completeness check failing on any registered experiment, or a guardrail flipping to `passes=False` → flagged for review. The genuinely worth-watching series once a second experiment exists and `drift-monitor` runs on a real recurring cadence: `n_cells_resolved` climbing toward `n_cells_total` over calendar time (confirms the resolution-horizon mechanic is behaving, the same signal this doc's Marketing attribution entry already watches for the identical cells), and `experiments_registry_complete` against `experiments_registered` (should stay at parity — a newly-registered experiment failing completeness on arrival is exactly the failure mode this checker exists to catch before it ships).
- **Persistence**: scalar metrics logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `analytics/model_performance.py`'s `log_performance()` under `model_name = 'experimentation_platform'` — `checks_total`/`_passed`, `all_checks_pass`, `experiments_registered`, `experiments_registry_complete`, `reproduction_check_applicable`, `reproduces_marketing_attribution_result` (only logged when applicable), `recomputed_incremental_share_pooled`, `recomputed_z_stat`, `abs_diff_incremental_share`/`abs_diff_z` (only logged when applicable), `n_cells_total`/`_resolved`, `n_control_resolved`, `n_treatment_resolved`, `achieved_power_at_designed_effect`, `mde_at_80pct_power_incremental_share`, `adequately_powered`, `guardrails_total`/`_passed`, `synthetic_completeness_checks_total`/`_passed`. Logged at 2025-12-31 (applicable checkpoint, 3/3 checks pass) and 2025-06-30 (non-applicable checkpoint, 2/2 checks pass). The variable-width detail (the full per-cell results table, the guardrail detail, the registry-completeness row, the synthetic-test scenario list) does not fit that log's flat scalar grain and is the structured detail recorded in `analytics/outputs/experimentation_platform_<as_of_date>.json`/`.md` and in this entry instead, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' Persistence note.

### Known limitations

- **Outcome and guardrail adapters are per-experiment, by necessity, not a gap to close.** A future experiment's `target_metric`/`guardrail_metrics` are free text; knowing which mart(s) actually hold its outcome event is not mechanically derivable from that text. Registering `EXP-0002` needs one new entry in `OUTCOME_ADAPTERS` (and, if its guardrails reference something the outcome adapter doesn't already cover, one in `GUARDRAIL_ADAPTERS`) — the statistical core does not change. This is stated as the framework's real, permanent scope boundary, not a TODO.
- **`_infer_arms()` assumes a two-arm design with a `'control'`-named arm value**, matching `fact_experiment_assignment.arm`'s actual `{treatment, control}` convention. A future multi-arm (A/B/n) design would need an explicit extension, not a silent guess — the function raises rather than infers incorrectly.
- **The completeness checker verifies structural presence, not semantic correctness.** A `control_definition` field containing a non-empty but nonsensical string (e.g. describing the same population as `treatment_definition`) would still pass — the same category of limit the Proxy-metric health entry's cost-tier judgment carries: structural checks catch what's missing, not what's wrong-but-present. A human review remains the check for whether a *complete* registration is also a *correct* one.
- **Only one real experiment exists to prove the framework against.** The reproduction check, by construction, can only validate against a case with an already-known-correct answer, and only one such case exists in this project. The framework's genericity claim rests on the statistical core's construction (no `EXP-0001`-specific logic in section 2 of the module) rather than on having been exercised against a second, differently-shaped real experiment — stated plainly rather than implied away.
- **No dbt model or Phase 1 generator was added or changed** to build this artifact. `dim_experiment` and `fact_experiment_assignment` were built as this artifact's stated prerequisite (476/476 dbt checks, 354/354 QA tests passing before this module was written) and are read, never modified, here.

## LTV by segment × acquisition channel

- **Built**: `analytics/ltv_by_segment.py`. Grain: one lifetime-value reading per (as-of date, **entry segment**) over a 60-month horizon, plus one LTV:CAC row per (entry segment, acquisition-channel cell) where the cell is large enough to be shown (SMB only). Source marts: `dim_accounts` (signup date, acquisition channel), `fact_account_segment_history` (entry segment, via `is_initial_segment`), `fact_revenue_monthly` (per-account MRR by month; a churned account's rows simply stop), `fact_marketing_spend`, `fact_rep_monthly_cost` and `fact_opportunities` (won new-business logos). Account loading is shared with `retention_cohorts.py` (`load_cohort_accounts`). No Phase 1 generator and no dbt model was added; the one dbt change lifts the `0.80` literal in `mart_efficiency` into the var `consumption_gross_margin` (see "Constants"), with every mart value unchanged.
- **What it is**: the model behind the metric-tree node "LTV by segment × acquisition channel" (registry key `ltv_by_segment_acquisition_channel`), a **non-additive** diagnostic overlay on Consumption payback with no children, computed outside the mart engine. This module reads the tree and redefines nothing in it; the tree fixes the three inputs it reuses (5-year horizon, flat 10% annual discount rate, the ~80% gross margin Consumption payback uses). The node's three prose inputs (retention curve, expansion trajectory, LTV:CAC) stay prose in the tree; here the retention curve is a Kaplan-Meier survival curve by entry segment, the expansion trajectory is the **observed** MRR per surviving account by age (it embeds overage and graduation; wallet-share progression is `NOT_COMPUTABLE`, so nothing is modeled from it), and LTV:CAC is reported against both CAC bases.
- **Counting**: it is the twenty-second `analytics/*.py` artifact module and the 24th portfolio artifact, a scope addition made after the build spec's 22-artifact order closed (the spec had absorbed "LTV:CAC and marginal-CAC economics" into the Efficiency nodes; the Wave 10 plan added a standalone model behind the tree's overlay node). The tree itself is unchanged at 70 nodes.
- **LTV is computed by entry segment**: the segment an account had at signup, never `dim_accounts.segment` (the current, possibly migrated segment). An account that migrates up and grows stays in its entry-segment cohort, so the graduation upside belongs to the segment that acquired it. The channel split is shown for **SMB only**; Commercial and Enterprise get **ranges**, not point estimates; Enterprise is **directional only**; channel gaps are stated as CAC-driven.
- **Shape decision — a projection from observed rates, not a classifier.** There is no AUC, no coefficient table and no confusion matrix, and their absence is deliberate. Its correctness is a modeling-assumption question, validated structurally (reconciliation to the facts, a no-look-ahead perturbation, synthetic planted-hazard scenarios with closed-form answers) and by an out-of-time backtest, per `analytics-engineering-conventions`' structural / projection category.
- **Model type selection and rationale.** A Kaplan-Meier (discrete monthly) survival curve with a constant-hazard tail was chosen over three credible alternatives. (1) *Compounding `mart_durability` GRR*: monthly GRR counts month-to-month usage contraction and compounds to roughly 50% a year, against logo retention of about 83% (SMB) to 99% (Enterprise) a year; it measures revenue contraction, not whether the account is still a customer, and would understate every segment's retention. (2) *A parametric survival fit (Weibull) or a Cox model with channel as a covariate*: Commercial churn is contract-structured (annual renewal cliffs: hazards of 13.5%, 8.0%, 5.7% and 6.1% at the first four renewals) which a smooth parametric shape blurs, and the generators never use channel in churn, so a channel covariate has nothing to find (log-rank p = 0.17 within SMB). (3) *`retention_cohorts`' pooled at-risk ratio (active ÷ old enough)*: a cross-section that mixes vintages of different ages at each age, so its month-60 value is only the 2020 vintage's; Kaplan-Meier uses every vintage's hazard at the ages it has observed and is monotone. The pooled ratio is kept as a reconciliation: the two agree at month 1 by construction. Across each segment's own observed window the largest difference is 3.1 percentage points for SMB (month 58; at month 60 Kaplan-Meier 42.2%, pooled 44.8%), 7.1 for Commercial (month 46) and 1.4 for Enterprise (month 28, its last observed month). The dated reports tabulate the standard marks (`km_vs_pooled_ratio`), where the differences are up to 2.6 (SMB), 4.3 (Commercial, month 48) and 4.8 percentage points (Enterprise, month 48, on 14 accounts, which lies beyond Enterprise's 28-month observed window and so is a projected value on one side).
- **Stochastic steps**: none in the model. The synthetic scenarios draw seeded random accounts (`_RANDOM_SEED` 42).

### Inputs and definitions — all PROPOSED, not yet confirmed

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Age `a` | Months since the signup month (`a = 0` is the signup month, a partial month). |
| `S(a)` | Probability an account is still a paying customer at age `a`. Kaplan-Meier over every account of the entry segment, pooled across vintages: at risk at transition `k → k+1` = accounts present at `k` and old enough to be observed at `k+1`; `S(a) = ∏ (1 − churned / at risk)` over `k < a`. **Observed** through the last age whose transition still has **30 accounts at risk** (`MIN_AT_RISK`); 95% band by Greenwood's variance. |
| Tail | Beyond the observed age, a constant monthly hazard = churn events ÷ at-risk months over the last **12 observed transitions** (one full renewal cycle for the annual-contract segments); its band is the Wilson 95% interval on that rate. The high band uses the lower hazard from the upper survival band, the low band the upper hazard from the lower survival band, so the ends compound worst × worst and best × best: deliberately wide, and **not a 95% interval** (the Greenwood band over the observed ages is). |
| `m(a)` | Mean MRR per **surviving** account at age `a`. Three paths (below). |
| Margin | `GROSS_MARGIN = 0.80`, the flat gross margin of Consumption payback. MRR × margin; the payback model's utilization haircut is **not** applied (the tree defines LTV as cumulative margin-adjusted revenue). |
| Discount | 10% a year, monthly factor `(1 + r) ** (−a / 12)`. **Timing convention**: the revenue of age `a` is treated as received `a` months after signup; month 0 is undiscounted. |
| LTV | `Σ_{a=0}^{59} S(a) · m(a) · margin / (1 + r)^(a/12)`: margin-adjusted, discounted revenue per **original** account (not per survivor). |
| Evidence grade | **point** (≥ 200 churn events in the entry segment), **range** (20 to 199), **directional only** (< 20). SMB has 1,784 churn events, Commercial 44, Enterprise 5. |
| Observed / projected share | The LTV terms from age 0 to `min(last survival-observed age, last MRR-observed age)` are *observed*; the rest is projection. The *observed-window-only* LTV is the same sum truncated there, a lower bound. |

**Constants.** The horizon (60 months), discount rate (0.10) and margin (0.80) are defined once in the module; the margin is also the dbt var `consumption_gross_margin`, which `mart_efficiency` reads (the SQL carries no margin literal). `tests/test_phase4_ltv_by_segment.py` and the build-time check `gross_margin_constant_ties_to_the_dbt_var` fail if the two differ or a margin literal returns to the SQL. The change leaves `mart_efficiency`'s consumption-payback columns (`blended_cac`, `avg_utilized_action_margin_per_account`, `consumption_payback_months`) byte-identical across a before-and-after `dbt build` (SHA-256 of the 214-row column set unchanged); four other columns (`net_new_arr`, `magic_number`, `am_expansion_arr`, `am_efficiency`) differ between two builds of the same code through float summation order in `int_revenue_movements`, which is the known dbt row-order non-determinism and is independent of the margin.

### Per-survivor MRR path — the dominant uncertainty

Mean MRR per surviving account rises with age **and** with calendar time, and the two cannot be separated in this history: ages beyond 36 months are only observed in calendar 2023 onward, and newer vintages start higher and grow faster (SMB month-12 MRR is $298 for the 2020 vintage, $324 for 2022 and $506 for 2024). A pooled MRR-by-age curve therefore understates newer vintages. Per entry segment, three paths are computed:

| Path | Construction | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `recent_flat` | Direct mean over accounts that signed up in the **last 36 months**, to the last age with at least `max(15, 5% of the window's accounts)` survivors, then **held flat** (no further expansion beyond what recent vintages have shown) | **Central** |
| `recent_chain` | The same, then grown by link ratios (ratio of summed MRR at `a+1` to `a` among accounts present at both, any vintage, at least 15 matched accounts), so older vintages supply the tail growth | Alternative (growth continues) |
| `older_flat` | Direct mean over vintages older than the recent window (2020–2022 at the primary checkpoint), then held flat | Alternative (the older vintages' own path) |

The share floor exists because mean MRR is heavy-tailed: about one account in ten graduates to a segment with 5 to 30 times the MRR, so a mean over a few dozen survivors is swung by a single graduate. **The floor was added after seeing the SMB LTV instability between the two checkpoints**: without it SMB's central LTV moved from $12.2K to $22.4K on the mean of 29 to 40 accounts at month 35; with it the two checkpoints read $15.6K and $15.8K. The two-checkpoint stability is therefore partly built in, not an independent confirmation, and the MRR path remains the largest unmonitored error. The **range** crosses the survival band with the three paths and takes the lowest and highest LTV. Which alternative is lower differs by segment: the older vintages are *lower* than the recent ones for SMB and Commercial and *higher* for Enterprise (recent Enterprise vintages start slightly below older ones).

**For SMB the central path is conservative, and the SMB backtest says so**: in all four SMB backtest comparisons (below) realized discounted revenue exceeded the projection (−6.5% to −32.0%), and the gap is almost entirely MRR (retention-only error −0.5% to +1.0%). That finding is SMB's alone: Commercial's 36-month LTV backtest reads −0.3% at the 2022-12 origin (n = 68) and **+10.6% at 2022-06 (n = 57), where the projection was above realized**, on thin samples, and Enterprise has no LTV backtest.

### CAC, on entry segment

`mart_efficiency` computes its `blended_cac` on the **current** segment; the LTV math is by **entry** segment, so CAC is rebuilt on entry segment. CAC exists only for entry vintages from **2023-01** (marketing spend begins there); the 1,540 earlier accounts supply the retention tail and the MRR path, never an LTV:CAC. CAC window: 2023-01 through the as-of month (36 months at 2025-12-31), a three-year average set against retention and MRR learned from all vintages.

- **Marketing-only CAC** = marketing spend allocated to the entry segment ÷ new accounts of that entry segment in the window. Allocation is `mart_efficiency`'s rule, on entry rather than current segment: each channel-month's spend is split across segments in proportion to each segment's share of that channel-month's new accounts (a channel-month with spend and no new accounts, outbound SDR only, uses that channel's whole-window mix). Because the split is proportional, **every segment in a channel gets that channel's CAC**: segment differences come from channel mix, and the allocation cannot show that an Enterprise account costs more to acquire through the same channel.
- **Rep-loaded CAC** = marketing-only CAC + acquisition-role rep cost per won new-business logo: **ISR** for Commercial, **AE and SE** for Enterprise, from `fact_rep_monthly_cost` (accrued fully-loaded cost including ramp), divided by the segment's won new-business logos in the same window. **AM cost is excluded** (retention and expansion, not acquisition). ISRs and AEs own only new-business opportunities (AMs own every renewal and expansion). Lost opportunities carry no `account_id`, so effort on losses cannot be split by channel or by logo: all rep cost is spread evenly over the wins, which is the usual fully-loaded convention and means CAC includes the cost of losing. **SMB has no reps**, so its rep-loaded and marketing-only CAC are the same figure. Rep cost per logo is identical within a segment, so it **adds a segment level and no channel signal**. Marketing headcount and SDR/BDR cost are absent from the data, so every CAC is a **floor**.
- The tree says the LTV:CAC ratio "closes when Consumption payback's CAC carries rep fully-loaded cost". That condition is not met for the payback node itself (`mart_efficiency.blended_cac` is marketing-only); this model carries the rep-loaded basis for the LTV:CAC ratio only. SMB's channel CACs ($62 self-serve, $1,589 inbound) differ slightly from the channel-wide `cac_unblended` ($62 and $1,598) because each month's spend is split by that month's segment mix before pooling.

### Results at the primary checkpoint (as of 2025-12-31; data through 2025-12-31)

| Entry segment | Churn events / evidence | Survival at month 60 (band) | LTV per new account (range) | Observed share of LTV | Marketing-only CAC | Rep-loaded CAC | LTV:CAC, rep-loaded (range) | LTV:CAC, marketing-only |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SMB | 1,784 / point | **42.2%** (39.6–44.8), observed | **$15.8K** ($14.7K–$20.3K) | 57% (observed through month 30) | $454 (5,773 accounts) | $454 (no reps) | **34.7x** (32.5–44.7x); 13.9x counting only revenue earned inside SMB | 34.7x, a floor on cost |
| Commercial | 44 / range | 67.4% (51.8–78.9), projected beyond month 49 | **$223.1K** ($125.9K–$270.2K) | 48% (month 33) | $776 (297) | $31.8K (ISR $31.1K per logo) | **7.0x** (4.0–8.5x); 1.9x counting only revenue earned inside Commercial | 288x, **not decision-grade** |
| Enterprise | 5 / directional only | 78.0% (51.2–94.4), projected beyond month 28 | **$1.14M** ($1.03M–$1.52M) | 51% (month 25) | $967 (90) | $338.9K (AE+SE $338.0K per logo) | **3.4x** (3.0–4.5x) | 1,182x, **not decision-grade** |

Bands: a survival value that is observed carries a Greenwood 95% interval; a projected value carries a compounded worst-to-best range, which is **not** a 95% interval. LTV ranges cross that survival band with the three MRR paths. The decision-grade columns follow a stated rule (`decision_grade`): a rep-loaded ratio is decision-grade only with point-estimate evidence, rep cost loaded where the segment has acquisition reps, and no more than half of the LTV arriving after the account moves up; the marketing-only ratio additionally requires a segment with no acquisition reps. **All three segments fail it today** (SMB because 60% of its value arrives after the move; Commercial and Enterprise on evidence grade and, for Commercial, the same share), so no ratio in the table is decision-grade; the flags are computed, not fixed.

At 2025-06-30 (data through 2025-06-30) the same table reads SMB $15.6K, Commercial $202.6K, Enterprise $1.15M, with rep-loaded LTV:CAC 33.9x, 5.9x and 3.1x. Survival at month 60 is observed only for SMB (482 accounts old enough to have reached it); Commercial has 22 and Enterprise 6.

**Reading the table.** (1) **SMB marketing-only LTV:CAC is large by construction**: the CAC spread is a data-generation parameter (target CAC of roughly $60 self-serve, $1,400 inbound marketing, $600 outbound, times noise), and no marketing or customer-success headcount sits in the denominator; it is a direction, not a multiple. (2) **The marketing-only ratio is never decision-grade for Commercial or Enterprise**: their reps are the acquisition cost, and the 288x and 1,182x figures are shown only to make that visible. (3) **Most of SMB's and Commercial's LTV is revenue earned after the account moves up a segment**: 60% (SMB) and 72% (Commercial) of the central LTV, carried by 722 of 7,222 SMB-entry accounts (10.0%) and 38 of 365 Commercial-entry accounts (10.4%). Counting only revenue earned inside the entry segment, SMB LTV is $6.3K (13.9x) and Commercial $61.5K (1.9x rep-loaded). **Account-management cost does not explain that gap.** The module sizes it from the data: over the CAC window AM cost is $1.67M of $29.7M of Commercial-segment revenue (5.62%) and $5.70M of $104.5M of Enterprise-segment revenue (5.45%), which moves the margin factor by about 7%; netting it takes Commercial from 7.0x to about 6.5x (SMB 34.7x to about 33.3x, Enterprise 3.4x to about 3.1x), nowhere near 1.9x (the approximation applies each segment's AM-cost share to the part of the value earned there; `am_netting_factor`). The gap is **attribution**: only about one account in ten moves up, those accounts carry most of the value, and the mean is heavy-tailed, so the ratio depends on how much of the upside of the few that move up is credited to the segment that acquired them. The two ratios bracket the answer; neither is the answer, and for Commercial they straddle any sensible threshold, which is the actionable finding. Enterprise is the top segment and has no such split. (4) The Enterprise range is wide because its tail rests on three churn events in the tail window and the first renewal cliff falls at month 25.

### Channel: a CAC split; no detectable retention or revenue difference

Acquisition channel is `dim_accounts.channel` (7,700 accounts, no nulls; `self_serve` 5,621, `inbound_marketing` 2,061, `outbound_sdr` 18), joined in Python from the marts; it survives to every revenue account, so the dependency on channel persisting to the revenue facts holds, with no dbt change. It is **not** `fact_leads.channel` (organic / paid / community), a different domain that is never joined here. Channel is not independent of entry segment by design (`outbound_sdr` is Enterprise-only; the mix differs by segment) and is stable across vintages within a segment.

The generators never use channel in churn or usage, and the test says the same: log-rank p = **0.17** for SMB `self_serve` vs `inbound_marketing` (1,310 and 474 churn events) and **0.09** for Commercial (31 and 13 events); neither is significant at 5%, and the month-60 survival gap in SMB is 3.9 points (43.2% against 39.3%). Revenue by channel is tested too: a Mann-Whitney comparison of the MRR of SMB accounts present at months 6, 12, 24 and 36 (self-serve against inbound, all vintages pooled) gives p = 0.83, 0.20, 0.30 and 0.35 (mean month-36 MRR $847 against $586, medians $324 against $299, so the means differ but not significantly, on a heavy-tailed distribution). Commercial is untested for revenue and Enterprise is untested altogether: their channel cuts are suppressed, and a test on 5 churn events (a log-rank p of about 0.03 on that few accounts) is not interpretable. **This is an absence-of-evidence statement on synthetic data, not proof of no effect.** The SMB channel rows therefore use the segment-wide survival and MRR path (identical LTV), and each channel's own survival is shown as a sensitivity:

| SMB channel | Accounts / churn events | LTV (segment-wide) | LTV with the channel's own survival | Marketing-only CAC (accounts in window) | LTV:CAC |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `self_serve` | 5,373 / 1,310 | $15.8K | $15.9K | **$62** (4,291) | **256x** (258x own survival) |
| `inbound_marketing` | 1,849 / 474 | $15.8K | $15.4K | **$1,589** (1,482) | **9.9x** (9.7x own survival) |

**The whole channel difference is CAC** (self-serve is about 26 times cheaper to acquire), which is a generator parameter, not an observed market price. Cells not shown, with reasons: Commercial (44 churn events in the whole segment), Enterprise (5), and SMB `outbound_sdr` (no SMB account came through it; its 18 accounts are all Enterprise, with tooling spend only and no SDR headcount in the data). If the log-rank test ever finds a channel difference at 5%, the module switches that channel row to its own survival and says so in the row.

### Validation — structural reconciliation, perturbation, planted-hazard scenarios and an out-of-time backtest

`run_build_time_validation()` runs 17 structural checks, 2 target checks and 3 reported comparisons (`[INFO]`, gating nothing); **all 17 structural checks pass at both checkpoints** (`[FAIL]` prints only for a structural failure; an unmet target prints `[MISS]`).

| Check | What it pins down | Tolerance | Result (2025-12-31) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `account_revenue_rows_start_at_signup_and_are_contiguous` | Every account has a signup-month row, none precedes signup, rows have no gaps (the premise of "presence = alive") | exact | 7,700 accounts, 0 violations |
| `at_risk_and_active_counts_tie_to_retention_cohorts` | This module's at-risk and still-present counts equal `retention_cohorts`' pooled curve, age by age, for all three entry segments | exact | 0 difference over 210 segment-ages |
| `oldest_cohort_margin_adjusted_revenue_ties_to_fact_revenue_monthly` | For accounts old enough to have 60 months observed, survival × MRR-per-survivor × margin × discount equals an independent SQL sum of their revenue; Kaplan-Meier equals the plain alive fraction when nothing is censored | 1e-9 relative | 9.9e-16 (SMB 517 accounts $14,778.47, Commercial 25, Enterprise 6) |
| `marketing_allocation_sums_back_to_spend` | The entry-segment allocation loses no spend | $0.01 | $2,936,472.18 on both sides |
| `new_accounts_by_channel_month_tie_to_fact_marketing_spend` | New accounts by channel-month equal the spend fact's | exact | 0 of 108 differ |
| `won_new_business_logos_equal_entry_accounts_commercial_enterprise` | Rep-cost denominators equal the entry accounts acquired | exact | 0 |
| `acquisition_rep_cost_ties_to_fact_rep_monthly_cost` | ISR + AE + SE cost equals an independent SQL sum; AM excluded | $0.01 | $39,640,930.14 both sides |
| `am_cost_share_inputs_tie_to_the_facts` | The AM cost and Commercial + Enterprise revenue behind the AM-cost share equal independent SQL sums | $0.01 | $7,373,308.30 of $134,253,927.15, both sides |
| `ltv_node_stays_a_non_additive_overlay_with_no_children` | Registry: `additive = false`, no children, parent `consumption_payback`, no source mart; the tree marks it non-additive; this module emits no `children` | exact | holds |
| `gross_margin_constant_ties_to_the_dbt_var` | Module margin = dbt var; no `0.80` literal in `mart_efficiency` | exact | 0.80 = 0.80 |
| `horizon_discount_rate_and_margin_equal_the_metric_tree` | 60 months, 0.10, 0.80 equal the tree's node text | exact | holds |
| `channel_retention_signal_not_overclaimed` | Where the log-rank test finds no difference every SMB channel row uses the segment-wide LTV | exact | p = 0.170, rows identical |
| `backtest_fit_on_truncated_frames_equals_a_direct_reading_at_the_origin` | The backtest's fit (full frames cut at the origin) equals `run_ltv` computed directly at the origin date | $0.01 | $0.00, all three segments |
| `no_lookahead_reading_unchanged_by_post_<date>_data` (×2) | On an in-memory copy of six marts in which everything after the date is corrupted (later revenue ×7 and re-segmented, churned accounts revived, later spend ×10, rep cost ×5, later accounts re-channelled, later deal outcomes flipped) the reading is identical, at 2025-12-31 and 2023-06-30 | exact | identical |
| `synthetic_known_answer_scenarios` | 11 planted scenarios (below) | per scenario | 11 of 11 |
| `backtest_realized_ltv_equals_survival_times_mrr_identity` | On accounts with the horizon fully observed, realized discounted margin per account equals the alive-fraction × mean-MRR sum | $1e-6 | 0.0 |

**Synthetic planted-answer scenarios** (seeded, through the same fit functions; the suite tests the arithmetic, the survival and tail rule, and the MRR-path construction, not the real data's assumptions): (1) constant 1.5% monthly hazard, fully observed: month-12/24/36/48/60 survival within 1.5pp of truth (max 1.20pp) and LTV within 1% of the closed form (0.75%); (2) constant 2% hazard observed only to month 29: the extrapolated month-60 survival 28.8% against 29.8% truth, LTV within 0.61%; (3) **a hazard that rises from 1% to 3% a month at month 36, beyond the observed window**: the module reproduces the constant-hazard rule's prediction (54.6% against 54.7%) and *misses the regime change* (truth 33.5%, LTV overstated by 5.6%), and the band (52.8–56.4%) does not contain the truth, which is the documented limit of the tail rule (the band covers sampling error, not a change of regime); (4) a hazard that rises from 1% to 2.5% at month 12, inside the window: tracked within 0.39pp and 0.16% LTV; (5) annual renewal cliffs of 8% observed to month 47: month-60 survival within 2.5pp, LTV within 0.17%; (6) MRR levels planted by vintage (recent 1.5× older): the recent path recovers $222.00 and the older path $148.00 at month 12 exactly, flat after the last supported age; (7) zero churn with constant MRR equals the closed-form discounted sum; (8) 20 accounts are `unavailable` with a reason, not a number; (9) a zero-event tail keeps a wide low band (0.80 against a flat central 1.0); (10) a margin of 0.55 and a 17% discount rate that are not the defaults, recovered within 0.60% of the independent closed form; (11) an exact survival-times-growing-MRR sum (survival 0.985^a, MRR 100·(1 + 0.04a)) at the same non-default margin and rate, equal to an independent loop to 1e-9. **Every expected LTV is computed by an explicit loop written in the scenario code (`_independent_ltv`), not by the module's `ltv_value` or `discount_factors`**, so an error in the module's LTV arithmetic cannot cancel against itself. `tests/test_phase4_ltv_by_segment.py` carries mutation guards that break the discount exponent (`a/12` to `a/11`), drop month 0, use the wrong margin and misalign survival and MRR by one age, and require the exact scenario (and, for the first two and the margin, the zero-churn or non-default-margin scenario) to fail under each.

**Out-of-time backtest.** The fit uses only data to the origin (as-of month minus 36: 2022-12-31 for the primary checkpoint, 2022-06-30 for the other), projects survival to month 60 and the 60-month LTV, and is compared with what the same accounts did through the as-of month. Realized survival is Kaplan-Meier on the same accounts (the pooled ratio is shown beside it).

SMB, origin 2022-12-31 (1,449 accounts, 288 churn events, survival observed through month 34, tail hazard 1.39% a month from 35 events):

| Month | Projected (band) | Realized (Kaplan-Meier) | Pooled ratio | At risk | Gap (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 36 | 60.8% (55.7–65.7) | 58.9% | 58.9% | 864 | −1.9 |
| 42 | 55.9% (49.6–61.8) | 54.5% | 54.5% | 660 | −1.4 |
| 48 | 51.4% (44.2–58.2) | 50.2% | 51.0% | 490 | −1.1 |
| 54 | 47.2% (39.3–54.8) | 46.0% | 45.9% | 329 | −1.2 |
| 60 | 43.4% (35.0–51.6) | 42.9% | 44.8% | 217 | −0.6 |

At origin 2022-06-30 the gaps are −1.3, −0.9, −1.2, −1.7 and −0.6 pp (months 36 to 60). **The projection is slightly optimistic (realized below projected at every mark, by up to 1.9pp) and every realized value sits inside the band.** LTV backtest on accounts old enough to have the horizon observed: 36-month discounted margin per account, projected **$6.57K against $7.40K realized (−11.1%)** on 1,449 accounts (retention-only error +1.0%, MRR-only −11.9%), and **$10.05K against $14.78K (−32.0%)** over 60 months on the 517 accounts with 60 months observed (retention +0.1%, MRR −32.3%); at the earlier origin −6.5% (n = 1,198) and −24.4% (n = 280). The error is entirely the MRR path: usage per surviving account kept rising after 2022, faster than the flat hold assumes. At the 2022 origins the `recent_chain` alternative could not extend growth (no vintage had been observed past month 35), so it equals the flat path there; the older-vintage tail growth is untested.

Commercial is **directional**: at origin 2022-12-31 only 68 accounts exist and 9 churn events, survival is observed through month 17, and the 12-month tail window contains the *first* renewal cliff (7 events; in the full data the first renewal's hazard is 13.5% against 8.0%, 5.7% and 6.1% at the next three). The projection is therefore pessimistic: month 36 projected 66.4% (band 44.9–84.5) against 77.9% realized (+11.6pp), month 42 61.4% against 73.5% (+12.1), month 48 56.9% (32.7–78.4) against 73.5% (+16.7); all inside the band, which is wide. The 36-month LTV is within 0.3% (n = 68). At origin 2022-06-30 the gaps are +9.5 and +7.6pp and the 36-month LTV +10.6% (n = 57). **Enterprise cannot be backtested**: 23 accounts have reached month 36 (19 at 2025-06-30), at origin 2022 there are too few at risk for any fit, and the first renewal cliff (month 25) leaves almost no churn history.

**Min-at-risk sensitivity (LTV, central).** SMB is unchanged under gates of 20, 30 and 50. Commercial: $223.9K, $223.1K, $223.2K (survival observed through month 57, 49, 37). **Enterprise: $1.156M, $1.143M and $1.237M** (observed through month 39, 28 and 20): at a gate of 50 the observed window ends *before* the month-25 renewal cliff, the tail window contains no event and the tail hazard is zero (+8%). The Enterprise tail is sensitive to whether the 12-month tail window contains the first cliff.

### Target and drift threshold — PROPOSED, not yet confirmed

- **Target (PROPOSED, not yet confirmed).** No AUC-style target applies. The target is the out-of-time backtest, by segment, because one threshold does not suit three segments of very different evidence: **SMB** (a point-estimate segment): realized retention at months 48 and 60 within **5 percentage points** of the projection **and** inside its band, and the 36-month discounted-margin LTV within ±25% of realized. **Met at both checkpoints**. The target table reports both horizons:

  | Checkpoint | SMB retention max gap, months 48 and 60 | SMB 36-month LTV error (gated, limit ±25%) | SMB 60-month LTV error (the horizon the headline uses; reported, no limit) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 2025-12-31 | 1.1pp | −11.1% (n = 1,449) | −32.0% (n = 517) |
  | 2025-06-30 | 1.2pp | −6.5% (n = 1,198) | −24.4% (n = 280) |

  The gated horizon is the shorter one. The 60-month error, which is the horizon the LTV headline is built on, would fail a ±25% limit at the primary checkpoint; it is reported without a limit because it measures the MRR path rather than retention, which is the larger unmonitored error. The 5pp and ±25% limits were set after seeing the scale of the backtest errors (up to 1.9pp, 11%), as the pipeline-coverage target was; they are not tuned to pass, and the rule has never been exercised. **Commercial** (a range segment): held to its own band at months 48 and later; reported, not gated, because the origin fit is thin. **Enterprise**: not backtestable until 30 accounts have reached month 36 (23 now); until then the backtest is replaced by the sample-size gate.
- **Why not a single 15-percentage-point gap.** A 15pp rule is loose for the segment that matters most and meaningless for the one that matters least. For SMB the realized-minus-projected gap never exceeded 1.9pp, so a 15pp rule could not fire before the curve was badly wrong. For Enterprise the entire month-36 to month-60 drop is under 15pp (projected 88.6% to 78.0%), so it could never fire at all. For Commercial it would already have fired at 2025-12-31 (+16.7pp at month 48), on a gap that is a known pessimism of a first-renewal tail window and well inside the band. The proposed rule keeps the general 2-consecutive-checkpoint trigger, with the caveat that **the two checkpoints are overlapping looks**: their backtest origins are six months apart on mostly the same accounts, so a second consecutive breach adds little independent confirmation.
- **Drift threshold (PROPOSED, not yet confirmed).** Flag for review on **2 consecutive checkpoints** on which `backtest_retention_max_abs_gap_pp_smb` above **5.0** (the largest realized-minus-projected gap at months 48 and 60 for SMB, in percentage points) or `backtest_retention_realized_in_band_smb` equal to 0, or any of the 16 structural checks fails (a defect to fix, not a trend to watch). Commercial: `backtest_retention_realized_in_band_commercial` equal to 0 for 2 consecutive checkpoints. Enterprise: re-evaluate when `enterprise_accounts_at_risk_month_36` reaches 30. Separately, **the discount-rate, margin and horizon assumptions are revisited every 12 months**. **Its limits:** with two checkpoints logged the rule has never been exercised; the checkpoints are overlapping looks (above); the monitored backtest lags the fit by 36 months, so drift in what a newly acquired account does is seen three years late; the limits were set after seeing the error scale; the SMB limit protects survival, not the MRR path, whose conservative bias in SMB is the larger, unmonitored error (`backtest_ltv_error_pct_smb_36m` and `_60m` are logged and watched, not gated); and the rule cannot see a regime change beyond the observed window (synthetic scenario 3).
- **Persistence**: scalar metrics are logged to `fact_model_performance_history` via `log_performance()` under `model_name = 'ltv_by_segment'`: per entry segment `ltv_central_usd_<seg>`, `ltv_low_usd_<seg>`, `ltv_high_usd_<seg>`, `ltv_observed_share_<seg>`, `retention_month36_<seg>`, `retention_month60_<seg>`, `churn_events_<seg>`, `tail_monthly_hazard_<seg>` (where a tail exists), `cac_marketing_only_usd_<seg>`, `cac_rep_loaded_usd_<seg>`, `ltv_to_cac_rep_loaded_<seg>` (graduation-inclusive) and `ltv_to_cac_entry_only_<seg>` (counting only revenue earned inside the entry segment; equal to the inclusive ratio for Enterprise, the top segment), always logged together; `smb_channel_log_rank_p_value`; `structural_checks_total`/`_passed`, `synthetic_scenarios_total`/`_passed`; the backtest scalars `backtest_retention_gap_pp_smb_m<36|42|48|54|60>`, `backtest_retention_max_abs_gap_pp_smb`, `backtest_retention_realized_in_band_smb`, `backtest_ltv_error_pct_smb_<36|60>m`, `backtest_retention_max_abs_gap_pp_commercial`, `backtest_retention_realized_in_band_commercial`; and `enterprise_accounts_at_risk_month_36`. The survival curves, MRR paths, backtest rows and scenarios are variable-width and live in the dated report (`analytics/outputs/ltv_by_segment_<date>.json` and `.md`). Logging is an upsert: re-running a checkpoint leaves the log and both reports byte-identical.

### Output contract

`run_ltv(as_of_date)` returns a JSON-safe dict the dashboard can render verbatim: `artifact`, `node`, `label`, `non_additive` (true), `as_of_date`, `data_through` (the last complete month end on or before the date), `constants`, `headline`, `segments` (one per entry segment: `status` `present` / `unavailable` with `reason_code` and `reason`, `evidence_grade`, `n_accounts`, `n_churned`, `retention` with `observed_through_month`, `checkpoints` at months 6, 12, 24, 36, 48 and 60 (central, low, high, `observed` or `projected`), `tail` and the full `curve`; `mrr_path` per scenario; `ltv` with `central_usd`, `low_usd`, `high_usd`, `observed_through_month`, `observed_share`, `projected_share` and `sensitivity_usd`; `cac` (marketing-only and rep-loaded, with the rep roles and the logos behind them); `ltv_to_cac` (marketing-only and rep-loaded, each with central, low, high, the entry-segment-only ratio and, for rep-loaded, `if_am_cost_netted`; plus the computed `*_decision_grade` flags, `decision_grade_unmet_conditions`, `decision_grade_rule` and `how_to_read`); `graduation` (share of LTV after the move, accounts moved up, the AM-cost shares and the netting factor); `caveats` and a `display` block of plain-language strings including `summary`), `cac_window`, `smb_channel_cut`, `channel_cells_not_shown` (with reasons), `channel_retention_tests`, `channel_mrr_tests` (Mann-Whitney on MRR by channel at months 6/12/24/36; Commercial and Enterprise marked untested), `channel_finding`, `am_cost_context`, `status_rule`, `definitions` and `caveats`. A segment with too few accounts, or a date before the data, is `unavailable` with its reason and no number; a date before 2023 has LTV and no CAC (`cac_status` `unavailable`), never a zero. The call is total: it returned a reading for every month end and mid-month date from 2019-12 to 2026-06 (156 dates), under both the root interpreter and `dashboard/.venv` (Python 3.12, pandas 3), with identical values to 1e-9.

### Known limitations, stated up front

- **A modeled view, not an observed value.** The oldest accounts are 71 months old; 43% (SMB), 52% (Commercial) and 49% (Enterprise) of the five-year value is projection beyond what the data directly shows.
- **The MRR path** is the largest single uncertainty (age and calendar time are confounded; mean MRR is heavy-tailed). The central path is a deliberate conservative convention; the SMB backtest shows it understates realized SMB revenue, but Commercial's backtest was mixed (−0.3% and +10.6%) and Enterprise has none; the high scenario is untested out of time.
- **Thin Commercial and Enterprise tails**: 22 and 6 accounts at month 60, 44 and 5 churn events. Their LTVs are ranges (Commercial) and directional (Enterprise); the Enterprise tail depends on whether its tail window contains the first renewal cliff.
- **Hazard extrapolation** assumes the last observed 12-month hazard persists; a regime change beyond the window is invisible to it and to its band (synthetic scenario 3). A tail set from a first renewal overstates later churn (Commercial: 13.5% first renewal, 8.0% to 5.7% after).
- **Generator-parameter CAC**: the CAC spread by channel is the data generator's, not a market observation, so SMB's very large marketing-only ratio is by construction. Every CAC is a floor (no marketing headcount, no SDR/BDR cost in the data). Marketing allocation by account share cannot differentiate segments within a channel.
- **Entry-segment LTV includes graduation**: most of SMB's and Commercial's value is revenue earned after the account moves up, carried by about one account in ten. Account-management cost is not netted, but it is small (about 5.5% of segment revenue, about 7% of the margin factor) and does not drive the gap between the inclusive and inside-segment ratios; the gap is attribution of the few accounts' upside. Both ratios are reported side by side.
- **CAC window versus retention vintages**: CAC averages 2023 to 2025; retention and MRR come from all vintages. Pre-2023 vintages have no CAC. SMB is 94% of entry logos.
- **Rep cost is spread over wins** and counted without a lag between cost and the wins it produces (Enterprise cycles are about 120 days).
- **`mart_efficiency.blended_cac` is still marketing-only**: the tree's LTV node text states both CAC bases, but Consumption payback itself does not carry rep cost.
- **All definitions, limits and the target are proposals**, supported by two checkpoints and one backtest origin each.

### Why this exists

MMM and marketing attribution calibrated against short-term outcomes (win rate × initial deal size) can systematically misallocate budget toward channels that convert cheaply but retain or expand poorly. This model is the check on that, and what it can say about channel is limited and specific. In this synthetic data it finds no detectable difference in retention or in revenue per surviving account between SMB's two channels (an absence of evidence, not proof), so the channel difference in LTV:CAC is a CAC difference, and that CAC spread is a generator parameter. It has **no rep-loaded channel cut**: SMB has no reps, the Commercial and Enterprise channel cuts are suppressed, and rep cost per logo is identical within a segment. It therefore cannot support weighting channel value by a rep-loaded LTV:CAC; what it supports is that, between SMB's channels, retention and revenue are not a reason to prefer one, so the choice rests on CAC. The larger economic spread it finds is by **segment** and by how much of an account's value arrives after it moves up (Commercial rep-loaded LTV:CAC reads 7.0x with graduation and 1.9x without). A budget-reallocation brief built on attribution or MMM should state this LTV:CAC context (see the `business-analyst` agent's required limitation check) rather than optimize on conversion alone.
